# Awesome Jev

[![Awesome](https://awesome.re/badge.svg)](https://awesome.re)
[![PRs Welcome](https://img.shields.io/badge/PRs-welcome-brightgreen.svg)](https://github.com/robokrunch/awesome-jev/pulls)

A curated list of resources for **Jev** — TypeSafe AI's "System One" model that returns typed, calibrated decisions instead of text.

Jev doesn't chat. You send it structured state; it returns a probability distribution over your predefined options in 70–500ms, at **$0.042 per million input tokens with output free**. It was built by Diogo Almeida (ex-OpenAI, co-inventor of RLHF/ChatGPT), emerged from stealth in September 2026 with $40M in funding, and **went generally available on 2026-09-21**: no waitlist, $5 starter credits (~120M tokens), with Vercel, Cloudflare, LangChain and Langfuse already adding it to their stacks (per Indian Express).

This list is maintained by [RoboKrunch](https://robokrunch.com) — we benchmark Chinese edge-AI hardware and run our own real-measurement Jev experiments (300 real API calls, real latency, real bills). Entries with our own measured numbers are marked 📊.

> **No official paper.** TypeSafe has not published a formal paper on Jev as of 2026-09-23. If one appears, it goes here first.

## Official

- [typesafe.ai](https://typesafe.ai/) — Official site (generally available since 2026-09-21; $5 starter credits).
- [Quickstart](https://docs.typesafe.ai/introduction/quickstart) — Official docs: `POST /v1/systemone`, question types (`choice` up to 255 options, `noul` yes/no, `score`).
- [@typesafeai on X](https://x.com/typesafeai) — Official announcements.
- [Founder launch thread](https://x.com/CompleteSkeptic/status/2099925682726002904) — Diogo Almeida introduces Jev and RLCD.
- [Jev on OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai/typesafe/jev-1.13) — `typesafe/jev-1.13`, $0.042/M input, $0/M output, 32K context. Note: OpenRouter exposes Jev via its decisions endpoint, not `/chat/completions`.
- [Jev on Cloudflare](https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai/models/typesafe/jev/) — Workers AI model page.
- [Jev 1.13 on LLM Gateway](https://llmgateway.io/models/jev-1.13.0) — Third-party provider listing: $0.042/M input, $0/M output, 64k context, OpenAI-compatible API with automatic provider routing and fallback.
- [$40M stealth emergence (Business Wire)](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260915525333/en/TypeSafe-AI-Emerges-From-Stealth-With-%2440M-in-Funding-With-New-Model-for-Composable-AI) — Funding announcement, September 2026.

## Models & API

- **Native API**: `POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone` — structured DOM/state in, `{operation, target}` style typed decisions out. One call can carry many questions in parallel.
- **Question primitives**: `choice` (pick from up to 255 options, per-option probabilities + `confidence`), `noul` (yes/no as a single probability), `score` (numerical).
- **Pricing**: $0.042 / 1M input tokens, $0 output. No tokens wasted on explanations.
- **Latency envelope**: TypeSafe quotes 70–500ms; independent testers measure p50 ~0.2–0.8s depending on task and route (see [Benchmarks](#benchmarks--evaluations)).

- [mohdyaser/Jev-multimodal](https://github.com/mohdyaser/Jev-multimodal) — Jev-inspired multimodal decision prototype on Gemma 4 E4B: typed decisions from a question plus zero to four ordered images, scoring one-token option markers over the supplied options. Apple Silicon MLX stack with a pinned model manifest, synthetic-only data, and a RESULTS.md local run dated 2026-09-29. Author's own framing: interaction-pattern inspiration only — not Jev's private architecture, no Jev-equivalence claim. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified, RESULTS.md verified)
- [dtheofr/typesafe-jev-ruby](https://github.com/dtheofr/typesafe-jev-ruby) — Ruby client gem for the Jev System One API (0.7.0): `Typesafe::Client#evaluate` posts state + typed questions; `Typesafe::Jev` pins the model to `jev-latest`; typed error subclasses for every non-2xx status; immutable `NoulAnswer` / `ChoiceAnswer` / `ScoreAnswer` objects with probabilities and confidence. Published on RubyGems, CI green. (1★, Ruby, MIT, created 2026-09-20; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, CHANGELOG verified)
- [thehumanworks/jevgrep](https://github.com/thehumanworks/jevgrep) — Rust crate `typesafe-jev` (0.2.0) with the three question types as real Rust types: `Noul`, `Choice` and `Score` question types follow TypeSafe's API reference field-for-field, with matching `NoulAnswer` / `ChoiceAnswer` / `ScoreAnswer` response types, `Question` enum and ordered `Questions` set. Independent of the two `jevgrep` code-search tools listed in Projects & Code (same name, different projects). (1★, Rust, no license declared, created 2026-09-17; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, crate CHANGELOG verified)
- [CMaintz/jev-dotnet](https://github.com/CMaintz/jev-dotnet) — Unofficial dependency-free .NET client for TypeSafe's Jev: send a state + typed questions, get typed answers with calibrated confidence that code can act on (README notes it is not published or endorsed by TypeSafe). (0★, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [CMaintz/jev-java](https://github.com/CMaintz/jev-java) — Unofficial dependency-free Java client for TypeSafe's Jev (Java 21+): send a state + typed questions, get typed answers with calibrated confidence; README states it is community-built, not published or endorsed by TypeSafe. (0★, Java, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [ivanblagdan/jev-cli](https://github.com/ivanblagdan/jev-cli) — Composable Rust CLI for TypeSafe's Jev: `choice`, `score`, `noul`; read state from stdin, ask one typed question per state, streaming JSON to stdout. `jev auth set-key` stores the key in the OS credential store (macOS Keychain / Windows Credential Manager / Linux Secret Service), no plaintext fallback. (0★, Rust, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [PostHog/jeeves](https://github.com/PostHog/jeeves) — Reasoning Jev-style classifier from PostHog: 9B Jev-like model (Qwen3.5-9B, LoRA, pointer head) with a block-4 diffusion drafter, trained with SFT and CISPO to "think before it decides". Author's numbers: beats Kev-9B and Jev on held-out test data (0.889 vs 0.822 / 0.857) and on JevBench public tiers (0.935 vs 0.866 for Jev; hard tier 0.865 vs 0.730). Jev-compatible API (noul/choice/score in one request), full training code + train/dev/test data in-repo, weights on HuggingFace. ~0.3s/request without thinking, 3.3s median with thinking on one H100. All numbers are the author's own runs — treat as self-reported, not independent. (80★, 7 forks, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [solvingSteve/Gevva0](https://github.com/solvingSteve/Gevva0) — "Calibrated Local Decision Gateway": Jev-like decision engine on Gemma 4 via llama.cpp/CUDA — direct token logit scoring, cyclic debiasing, Platt temperature calibration, asymmetric post-decision audit trails. Author claims 15–45ms fast-path classification on consumer hardware, ECE < 0.03, and JevBench v1.4.2 rank #1 (74.63) — all self-reported, unverified independently. (5★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-27; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [avbiswas/bev-decider](https://github.com/avbiswas/bev-decider) — Open System One decision model (Qwen3-0.6B finetune, 0.4B params): drop-in `/v1/systemone` local server speaking the TypeSafe Jev question format. Core claim: choice-order invariance — 960 random shuffles under fp32 with no probability drift beyond 1e-5. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-28; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified; HF weights `avbiswas/bev-decider-0.4B` carry CC-BY-NC-4.0)
- [PabloB07/JevMC](https://github.com/PabloB07/JevMC) — Unofficial dependency-free Java SDK for Jev in PaperMC (Minecraft) plugins: thin typed wrapper around `POST /v1/systemone` (Java 17+, zero shaded deps), async `CompletableFuture` evaluation with retries on 429/529, the three primitives as immutable records. Sample use case: chat moderation — noul "is this message toxic?" then a choice of warn/punish/none. EN + ES + PT READMEs. (0★, Java, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [yuma-seno/local-jev](https://github.com/yuma-seno/local-jev) — Japanese-first unofficial local reimplementation of the Jev idea: Qwen3-1.7B + a learned score head with a single-forward-pass tree attention mask over cached state — no text generation, just typed decisions. Author's numbers (self-reported, not independently verified): 72.7% accuracy and ECE 0.055 across 509 questions in 12 unseen task families, top-30% confident calls at 91% accuracy, ~0.9s/question on a laptop iGPU via llama.cpp (Vulkan). Explicitly unaffiliated with TypeSafe. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [tyrchen/jev-typed](https://github.com/tyrchen/jev-typed) — Typed third-party Rust SDK for Jev with derive macros (`#[derive(Decision)]`, `#[derive(ChoiceOptions)]`, `#[derive(ScoreLevels)]`): the doc comments on your struct *are* the prompt text, and the compiler checks the question/option/answer shape end to end. `Client::from_env()` reads `JEV_API_KEY`. (0★, Rust, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [soumyacodes007/jev-compatible-model-training](https://github.com/soumyacodes007/jev-compatible-model-training) — "Jef": train a small decision model on any Hugging Face classification dataset — give it state, one focused question, and a fixed list of options, it returns the letter of the option it picks. Jev-like interface without TypeSafe weights; a modular rework of ipenywis/Jef. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [avalonreset/legends-jev](https://github.com/avalonreset/legends-jev) — Typed decision advice adapter for an operator choosing application or browser actions: supply small text evidence + explicit candidate actions, get a candidate ID or operator fallback with validated Choice/Score/Noul answers. No screenshot capture, grounding, or actuation — evidence checking stays with the operator. Ships its own synthetic benchmark (`docs/BENCHMARK.md`, 20 pre-labelled cases, `jev-1.13.0` pinned 2026-09-29): Jev-with-local-guards agreed on 17/20 vs 13/20 for direct exact-match code; median 229ms across 14 live calls, zero wrong suggestions in five — author caveat: tiny authored set, not a representative computer-use benchmark. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [lokiwizard/my_jev](https://github.com/lokiwizard/my_jev) — Open-Jev based text decision model (bilingual EN/CN README): Qwen3.5-2B backbone + LoRA (rank 8, alpha 16) + single scalar score head, supporting Choice, Score and Noul. Trained on the ZefanCai/Open-Jev HF dataset (release-v2-redistributable) with train/valid/calibration/test/ood splits of 79,116 / 3,723 / 4,672 / 10,356 / 15,701 decisions; resumable training, calibrated inference. Weights and run outputs kept out of git. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [prashlkam/plk-JEV](https://github.com/prashlkam/plk-JEV) — Independent open implementation of Jev's three observable properties (type safety, calibrated confidence, low latency) built from scratch — explicitly not a clone of TypeSafe's unpublished architecture or RLCD recipe. Ships RESULTS.md with measured runs (including one failed gate and a negative result), HARDWARE.md (8 measured plan revisions for a GTX 1650), a 120k-example corpus pipeline, and a `decide_or_defer` API with per-field confidence; runs on Ollama. (0★, Python, GPL-3.0, created 2026-10-08; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [seongyeon1/dlm-jev](https://github.com/seongyeon1/dlm-jev) — Jev-style typed decision engine on a diffusion LM (DiffusionGemma 26B-A4B, MLX 4-bit): each question gets one label-token slot in the diffusion canvas, one decoder pass yields every question's distribution and confidence, calibration via per-type/per-question temperature fitting. Design doc included. Explicitly unaffiliated with TypeSafe — no Jev weights, API, or data used. (1★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [ejhshen/OpenJev](https://github.com/ejhshen/OpenJev) — "OpenJev-4B": decision model learned from the text backbone of Qwen3.5-4B — given a state, a question and a set of natural-language options, it directly predicts a probability distribution over the options (selection, binary judgments, rubric scoring). Independent reproduction attempt, not affiliated with TypeSafe; weights on HuggingFace (`shenjunhao/OpenJev-4B`, page verified live). (8★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [lexmount/WebJev](https://github.com/lexmount/WebJev) — Jev-like decision models specialized for browser use: given a live web page, pick the next action in a single forward pass. Author-reported head-to-head (their own 125 real-website tasks, deterministic task verifier, not independently reproduced): WebJev-35B-A3B reaches target pages in a few steps where `typesafe/jev-1.13` loops or stalls on the same browser agent. (2★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [yashml-dev/DEV---Decision-Engine-for-Verdicts](https://github.com/yashml-dev/DEV---Decision-Engine-for-Verdicts) — "DEV": a compact Jev-like decision model in Python — typed questions in, typed answers with probabilities and confidence out; the worked example routes a support ticket (department Choice, urgency Score, escalate flag) with no text generation. Independent reproduction, not affiliated with TypeSafe. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [hihaluemen/-open-jev-qwen3.5-2b](https://github.com/hihaluemen/-open-jev-qwen3.5-2b) — "s1-repro": unofficial Jev-style decision readout — renders a classification question as a multiple-choice prompt and reads next-token logits over the option letters on Qwen3.5-2B (LoRA weights at `Hihalue/s1-repro-qwen3.5-2b-lora` on Hugging Face), yielding calibrated probabilities from a single forward pass, no generation. Chinese README. (0★, Python, no license declared, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Pawnnwap/jev](https://github.com/Pawnnwap/jev) — "jpt-4b as a Jev-like decision service (llama.cpp)": local open implementation of the `/v1/systemone` wire format — given a state and typed questions, returns a calibrated probability for every option in a single prefill, no generated reasoning tokens. Open calibrated-decision fine-tune of Qwen3.5-4B (base `kirp/jpt-4b`, GGUF by `prithivMLmods`). (0★, no license declared, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [AraujoJordan/jema](https://github.com/AraujoJordan/jema) — Local, fast decision model for Apple Silicon: Gemma 4 E4B on Ollama's MLX engine, real probabilities read from grammar-masked logprobs (not self-reported) — 8 questions on a new state in ~235ms, one yes/no in ~50–90ms on an M4 Max; JEV-compatible `/v1/systemone` API so Jev clients can point at it, nothing leaves the machine. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-30; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [NandhaKishorM/laya](https://github.com/NandhaKishorM/laya) — The original Laya source repo, by its author Nandakishor Mukkunnoth (ConvAI Innovations): multilingual, non-autoregressive System 1 decision engine — typed decisions over 100+ languages in a single forward pass (author-claimed 33ms), RLCD training, a router picking the checkpoint per request. Ships the 421M English and 322M multilingual checkpoints (`pip install laya`), HF `convaiinnovations/laya` + `laya-multilingual`, Colab notebooks, docs. (29,354★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-18, pushed 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified; speed numbers are the author's own)
- [tnaftali/s1-tui](https://github.com/tnaftali/s1-tui) — `s1`: terminal UI (Textual) for System One typed decisions — type a state and a question, get a typed answer (yes/no, pick, or score) with calibrated confidence and the full probability spread. Two interchangeable backends: `laya` local (MLX) and `jev` (TypeSafe API). (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Shabin2003/CLM](https://github.com/Shabin2003/CLM) — "CLM: the open source alternative of Jev" — Contrastive Language Model, built because Jev's architecture is undisclosed and API-only. Feed it a message plus a set of conditions (with fixed categorical values where they apply) and it returns confidence scores per condition, plus Noul-style boolean checks as a 0–1 score. (0★, MIT, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [nvkudva/olla-jev](https://github.com/nvkudva/olla-jev) — "Ollama, but for Jev-style decision models": pull System One models from Hugging Face (decider, laya, Julia, kev), run them locally, and call them through the Jev-compatible `/v1/systemone` API — `serve`/`run`/`pull`/`list`/`ps` CLI mirrors Ollama; drop-in for the TypeSafe SDK by pointing `TYPESAFE_BASE_URL` at the local server. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [senna-lang/qwen3-4b-system-one](https://github.com/senna-lang/qwen3-4b-system-one) — Inference code for the open `qwen3-4b-system-one-lora` LoRA on Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507: ask several questions about one record in a single forward pass via tree-masked attention, with PyTorch and Apple Silicon MLX backends. Ships with a plain non-affiliation disclaimer and a "not for high-stakes decisions" caveat. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Mercury Decide (Inception Labs, on OpenRouter)](https://openrouter.ai/inception/mercury-decide:free) — Inception's structured decision model served as a System One endpoint: typed choice/score/yes-no questions with calibrated probabilities, same `/v1/systemone` schema as Jev, 32,768-token context, output tokens free. Free tier since 2026-09-30. "Up to 14 decisions per second" is OpenRouter's marketing copy, not an independent measurement. Page verified live 2026-10-01.
- [d1 (Liquid AI)](https://systemonemodels.org/models/) — Liquid AI's first decision model: typed Choice, Score and Noul questions with probabilities, no prose; available as a free `d1:free` tier on Liquid's API since 2026-09-29, with no paid pricing published yet. Listing verified via systemonemodels.org.
- [Solar Decide (Upstage)](https://systemonemodels.org/models/) — Upstage's System One model served on Solar Mini 4 with a 512K-token context: typed Choice, Score and Noul questions with probabilities, no prose; in beta on Upstage's API since 2026-09-22 at $0.1/MTok. Listing verified via systemonemodels.org.
- [strands-labs/strands-decider](https://github.com/strands-labs/strands-decider) — AWS's open-source decision model from Strands Labs (released 2026-10-01, covered by TechCrunch / SiliconANGLE / VentureBeat): Qwen3.5-2B torso with a custom ~1M-param "pointer head" scoring hidden states of the choices against answer positions, fine-tuned with a rank-16 LoRA adapter; AWS reports <150ms local latency and strong JevBench accuracy/calibration vs other open 2B models (AWS figures, not independent). Origin story: AWS distinguished engineer Marc Brooker built his own Jev take after seeing Jev — it briefly topped the Jevbench size-tier ranking, and Amazon productized it as v20. Training data, training scripts, code, examples and HF weights all released. (10★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [kouhxp/gutsy](https://github.com/kouhxp/gutsy) — Open local decision models with calibrated probabilities: `gutsy-0.8b` v0.3 is a Qwen3.5-0.8B fine-tune behind a Jev-style Decisions-format API (yes/no, choice, score), so existing System One clients need only a new base URL — no text generation, no network calls, no per-call cost. (2★, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [mpecan/rippy-kev](https://github.com/mpecan/rippy-kev) — Small local decision model for the author's own agent (`rippy`): a LoRA fine-tune of Kev-4B on shell-command question sets gathered from real Jev usage, served locally as a Jev-compatible `/v1/systemone` endpoint so the existing `[jev]` client talks to it unchanged. Weights on HuggingFace (Risethagain/rippy-kev-4b), RESULTS.md included. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [togethercomputer/tev1](https://github.com/togethercomputer/tev1) — Together AI's open Jev-inspired decision model: supervised fine-tune of Qwen3.5-4B (rank-8 LoRA, ~37,840 examples, full data recipe + the "$17 training" tutorial published) that returns one option letter from a structured state/question/2–24 options. Keeps Qwen's next-token head — a Jev-style classifier, not a non-autoregressive Jev runtime. Weights on HuggingFace (`togethercomputer/Tev1-4B-experimental`), served serverless at $0.042/M input, $0 output; listed on systemonemodels.org and OpenRouter. Author-reported 880/1,000 on their own dev set — not a held-out test. (Apache-2.0 code, verified via GitHub/HF/OpenRouter/Together AI, 2026-10-02)
- [lukas-karsch/jev](https://github.com/lukas-karsch/jev) — TypeScript SDK experiments for TypeSafe's Jev: `packages/jev-sdk` holds the reusable SDK, `/apps` explores Jev use cases on top of it. (0★, no license declared, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [SanthoshReddy352/Evolve-x-OpenJev](https://github.com/SanthoshReddy352/Evolve-x-OpenJev) — "OpenJev": free hosted Jev-style typed-decision API (`POST /v1/systemone`) on top of Laya, served from the author's laptop through a tunnel — "a website should know who is reading it." Honest framing; availability depends on a laptop staying online. (0★, no license declared, created 2026-10-01; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Cloudflare Clef & Clef-flash](https://huggingface.co/Cloudflare/clef) — Cloudflare's first team-trained models and the first major-infra entry in the decision-model race (announced 2026-10-01 during Workers AI Birthday Week, official changelog at [developers.cloudflare.com](https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2026-10-01-clef-workers-ai/)): decision models that speak Jev's `/v1/systemone` wire format, so existing Jev integrations swap in by changing endpoint + model name. Clef is 27B (post-trained Qwen3.8-27B), Clef-flash 9B (Qwen3.5-9B); both keep the backbone vision encoder (up to 4 images/request), 64K context. Open weights on HuggingFace under Apache 2.0 (Cloudflare/clef 369 likes, Cloudflare/clef-flash 121 likes as of 2026-10-02), live on Workers AI at $0.24/M input tokens (Clef) / $0.09/M (Clef-flash), output free; an RL fine-tuning design-partner program launched alongside. Cloudflare's self-reported numbers: a Clef model scores highest on 7 of 10 decision benchmarks; BANKING77 macro-F1 94.20 (Clef) / 90.93 (flash) vs 79.74 (Jev); median latency 209.3ms (Clef) / 38.8ms (flash) vs 524.1ms (Jev) across 43 runs. All benchmarks are Cloudflare's own figures — not independently reproduced on the HF Jev Decision Index; [Flavio Copes' deep dive](https://flaviocopes.com/clef/) independently ran both models on a Cloudflare account and confirms the mechanics. (announcement + benchmarks via official Cloudflare sources, weights and license verified via HuggingFace API, 2026-10-02)

- [Arcobalneo/open-jevlike-infer](https://github.com/Arcobalneo/open-jevlike-infer) — Production inference server for open Jev-like decision models behind Jev's `POST /v1/systemone` API: these models carry a custom scoring head, so `vllm serve` alone can't run them — this serves them with a vLLM backbone + the model's own head, starting with Cloudflare's Clef-Flash. CI green, EN + ZH-CN READMEs. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-02; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [devpilgrin/jeff-guard-multilang](https://github.com/devpilgrin/jeff-guard-multilang) — Prompt-injection guard built on the Jeff System One decision model ([firelex/jeff](https://github.com/firelex/jeff), listed above): a 10.5M-param LoRA screening any text an agent is about to read — one forward pass (~20ms on an RTX 4090), calibrated probabilities, 12 languages, agentic/indirect injections. Author's REPORT claims test accuracy 0.99 and agentic recall 1.00 — self-reported, not independently verified. HF weights + 103K-row dataset published; EN + RU READMEs. (0★, Python, MIT code / Apache-2.0 model, created 2026-10-02; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [Camellia86/Canopy-Jev](https://github.com/Camellia86/Canopy-Jev) — Open Jev-like 27B decision model (frozen Qwen3.8-27B, shared context + isolated question branches, zero generated answer tokens): author claims 87.45% on JevBench (+2.16pp over Open-Jev-27B-v1.1), 75.68% on Hard, MMLU-Pro 64.50%, LoRA 6.26M trainable params / 25.24MB adapter, P50 272–741ms on a shared H200 — all self-reported, not independently verified. MIT, weights on HuggingFace, bilingual EN/中文. (1★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-02; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

(0★, Rust, MIT, created 2026-10-02; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [BarnacleLabs/cyprid](https://github.com/BarnacleLabs/cyprid) — "One API and one way to launch any Jev-type decision model on a Mac": installs, starts/stops and serves local decision models (Laya, decider, nli, gliclass, kev, von, qwen3guard-class family) behind one Jev-style API + a comparison web page; Apple Silicon only, nothing leaves the machine. Explicit non-affiliation note: independent of TypeSafe and of the models it can run. (1★, Go, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-02; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [RazvanManolache/raz-systemone](https://github.com/RazvanManolache/raz-systemone) — Rust CLI + HTTP server speaking Jev's `/v1/systemone` contract over local models: three scorers (NLI with own finetuned checkpoints, embedding, LLM via Ollama), plus an optional `jev` reference scorer against the real TypeSafe API. 12 hermetic unit tests. - [DakotaTexas/jev-compatible-local-decider](https://github.com/DakotaTexas/jev-compatible-local-decider) — ~150-line server speaking Jev's `/v1/systemone` API on any local LLM that returns logprobs (vLLM, SGLang, TensorFold…): each Jev question becomes one lettered-option prompt, one generated token at temperature 0, top_logprobs mass renormalized over the options. On JevBench's 231 public items (DGX Spark GB10, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next): Hard 77.5% vs Jev 1.13 cloud 73.9%, ECE 0.030 vs 0.044, p50 0.34s vs 0.42s — but 95% CIs overlap (68.9–84.3 vs 65.0–81.1): "on par or better", not a definitive ranking; author-reported, not independently verified. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-02; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

Spanish docs. (0★, TypeScript, MIT, created 2026-10-03; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [arbihazanst/ollama2jev](https://github.com/arbihazanst/ollama2jev) — "LLM2Jev": turn a local LLM (SGLang / MLX / Transformers backends) into a Jev-style structured decision model — prefill-only scoring, no token-by-token decoding; text and image scoring, candidate batching, and a `/v1/systemone`-compatible HTTP service so existing clients work unchanged. Explicit non-affiliation note: independent project, not endorsed by TypeSafe. Its README news section reports a JevBench eval (231 public items, P50 latency "below one tenth of the Jev official result") — self-reported, not independently verified. Bilingual EN/中文 READMEs. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-03; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [cmzy/ajev-infer](https://github.com/cmzy/ajev-infer) — Inference for AJev, an open Jev-style typed decision model: `google/gemma-4-12B-it` + a LoRA adapter (weights on HuggingFace), returning calibrated probabilities for yes/no (`noul`), single choice (up to 255 options) and ordered `score` questions over any text/JSON state. Inference-only code; results and training details live in the linked HF model card. Bilingual English / 简体中文 READMEs. Independent open implementation — explicitly not affiliated with TypeSafe. - [sepatel/rverdict](https://github.com/sepatel/rverdict) — Native Rust decision engine in the "System One" family: `noul`/`choice`/`score` typed questions return calibrated probabilities from one forward pass, no text generation; runs fully locally on the Burn framework (CUDA/ROCm/Vulkan/Metal/CPU backend chosen at runtime), no Python at any stage. Speaks the Jev-compatible `/v1/systemone` wire format — existing Jev/Clef/Von clients can point at `rverdict serve`, and the CLI can also call hosted APIs (incl. TypeSafe) or Cloudflare's Clef. Author's claim: JevBench parity with Von at 137/231, identical on CPU and GPU — self-reported, not independently verified. Default model is Von 1.2 from HuggingFace, pinned to a revision. - [inatos/ereshkigal](https://github.com/inatos/ereshkigal) — Semantic ifs in Rust: typed option probabilities from open GGUF models via llama.cpp — reads declared option-letter logits in one forward pass, no answer decoding, with Direct/Serial/Shared cache modes for the prefill. Inspired by SemIf/OpenJev; independent project, explicitly not affiliated with Jev or TypeSafe. (0★, Rust, MIT, created 2026-10-04; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

Weights on HuggingFace (bf16, MLX 8-bit, GGUF for llama.cpp), `/v1/systemone` wire format, results/training details/limitations in the model card, companion write-up at syn-ack.ai. - [MeharPro/GenClass](https://github.com/MeharPro/GenClass) — "The version of Jev that runs in your browser": 32M-param typed-decision model + Chrome extension, fully local (WebGPU/WASM inference, on-device Moonshine speech recognition). Author-published head-to-head vs Jev (their own inputs, BENCHMARKS.md): GenClass 90.4% vs Jev 66.4% on mid-sentence command recognition, 94.9% vs 75.5% on picking the exact words — but trails on general questions (80.5% vs 94.7%); 0% option-order flips vs 10–13% for Jev (third-party figure). - [CanadaOrNaw/dex](https://github.com/CanadaOrNaw/dex) — "Dex": open-source, Jev-compatible, non-autoregressive System 1 decision model on an Ettin-1B bidirectional encoder (1.1B params, 784M fine-tuned, Apache-2.0): same `POST /v1/systemone` request/response shape as TypeSafe Jev, served locally on 127.0.0.1:18766; HF weights `CanadaOrNaw/dex-1b` (page verified live). Ships a full re-runnable benchmark report: 5,327 human-labeled questions — JEV 81.8% accuracy vs Dex 71.4% vs stock Laya 64.0% (calibration error 0.052/0.058/0.092); Dex beats JEV on routing (100.0% vs 88.3%), command gating and injection screening, lags on work correctness and exam sets. Author's own runs on one RTX 3070 Ti — self-reported, not independent. (1★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-09; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + BENCHMARKS.md verified)
- [feder-cr/jev](https://github.com/feder-cr/jev) — "Jevos": open-source, C++ alternative to TypeSafe Jev for typed decisions (yes/no, multiple choice, score) that runs on a laptop CPU — one binary, no Python. Speaks the Jev `/v1/systemone` wire format (default model name `jev-latest`, answers as `jevos-v4`). Author-reported measurements (their own harness, not independent): median 28ms/130ms per short/long request on an Intel Core Ultra 7 255H laptop vs Jev 311/314ms, Qwen3.5-4B 3,060/4,761ms, Laya 129/480ms; accuracy across 6 task sets — jevos-v4 0.95/0.76/0.76/0.81/0.50/0.37 vs Jev 1.00/0.91/0.88/0.98/0.69/0.59 — and the author notes five of six sets helped choose the released checkpoint, so treat as self-reported. Model on HuggingFace (`feder-cr/jevos-v4`, GGUF for llama.cpp) and pinned releases with SHA verification. (1,257★, C++, MIT, created 2024-08-15; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified)
- [jeppevammenkristensen/all](https://github.com/jeppevammenkristensen/all) — "JRK.JevRunner": unofficial .NET client for Jev and the TypeSafe System One API — submit application state and named questions, consume typed responses; choice-question builders, tests, and a NuGet package (`JRK.JevRunner`). (0★, C#, MIT, created 2026-10-09; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified)
- [JaakkoLipp/luna-middleware-decisions](https://github.com/JaakkoLipp/luna-middleware-decisions) — `decisions-middleware`: a decisions API backed by a non-reasoning GPT Luna model, speaking two wire formats on one engine — OpenAI's Decisions API (`predicate`/`choice`/`score`) and TypeSafe's Jev (`noul`/`choice`/`score`) via `POST /v1/systemone`, reachable through OpenRouter or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint. 152 tests pass against a mocked upstream (including the official OpenAI SDK driving `/v1/decisions`); the author states plainly it has **not yet run against the real Luna** — latency, accuracy and calibration are unmeasured. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-09; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Gumgo91/jev-like-screening](https://github.com/Gumgo91/jev-like-screening) — Manuscript-grade bioinformatics from Seoul National University: "Jev-like" shared-state networks for structure-based virtual screening — a pocket is encoded once into a shared state, every ligand scored independently. On 49 DOCKSTRING pockets the dual encoder and pooled network recover 0.88/0.89 of the top-1% docking hits in the best 10% of the ranking (vs 0.92 for a cross-attention net); on one RTX 4090 the dual encoder ranks 260,155 ligands against 57 pockets from SMILES in 43s (author's measurement). Note: this is a research network *borrowing the Jev interface*, not TypeSafe's model. Ships LaTeX manuscript, figures, code (`pocketgate`), data splits and release zips with SHA256SUMS. (0★, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-09; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [THX-01 (HAL-X AI)](https://huggingface.co/doofz/THX-01) — Open-source, non-autoregressive multilingual decision model (Apache-2.0): takes a state plus typed questions and returns calibrated answers in a single forward pass (~10ms on one GPU, per the author), trained with RLCD on strictly proper scoring rules; built on mmBERT-base, 100+ languages. Weights on HuggingFace (`doofz/THX-01`, safetensors) and PyPI (`thx01` 1.0.0, uploaded 2026-10-08). "Beats Jev" and "runs on your phone" are the author's HN claims — not independently verified (4 HF downloads, 1 like as of 2026-10-10). (HF page + PyPI page verified, weights present; surfaced via three HN submissions: items 50017271, 50023978, 50024061)
- [ogarciarevett/jev-py-sdk](https://github.com/ogarciarevett/jev-py-sdk) — Python port of the TypeScript `jev-sdk`: typed `noul` / `choice` / `score` judgments from TypeSafe with client-side masking ("masked before they leave the machine"); one `judge()` call asks every question about one state in a single request and degrades any failure (missing key, timeout, low confidence) to an `undecided` verdict instead of raising. Signed wheel releases (v0.1.0 + SHA256SUMS), `PROVENANCE.md`, docs and tests. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-09; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + src/jev_sdk/judge.py verified)
- [masterset-ai/PhysicalEye-Decide](https://github.com/masterset-ai/PhysicalEye-Decide) — Image-input decision model: "show it an image, ask a question, get a calibrated decision in one forward pass" — HF weights [`PhysicalEye-Decide-35B`](https://huggingface.co/masterset-ai/PhysicalEye-Decide-35B) (base Darwin-35B-A3B-Opus) plus a live [HF Space demo](https://huggingface.co/spaces/masterset-ai/PhysicalEye-Decide-35B-demo), inference via `decide.py`. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified)
- [jiangfeibo/TianZe-MJev](https://github.com/jiangfeibo/TianZe-MJev) — Multimodal Jev-style decision model framework (frozen-backbone, no extra training): seven base models (Gemma-4-26B-A4B-it, Qwen3.5-2B-Base, Gemma-3n-E2B-it/E4B-it, MiniCPM-V-4.5, InternVL3.5-8B/14B) each run through 140 fixed text + synthetic-vision probes against their own native outputs — 120/120 candidate-decision agreement, zero full-vocabulary logit difference, parameter objects unchanged. The author explicitly says this does not prove all backbone abilities are preserved or generalize to real tasks. Unified API, web decision console with gallery, English + 简体中文 docs, CI tests, frozen-manifest benchmarks. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + docs/base-retention-results.md verified; numbers are the author's own measurements)

Live site [jeyzma.com](https://jeyzma.com/): load a GGUF decision model, ask `noul` / `choice` / `score` questions, with Jev-Style and JevK5 readout modes plus option-order reversal + averaging as a debiasing option. From the Hybrid Group (Gobot). (12★, HTML/Go, no license declared, created 2026-09-27; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified, site live-verified; surfaced via HN item 50031093)

The author's claim: 100% offline, zero cost, <18ms on CPU — not independently verified, no published benchmarks in-repo yet. Rust engine + integration tests, CI. (1★, Rust, MIT, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified; latency/cost figures are the author's claims)
- [standardthinkingai/standard-one-sglang](https://github.com/standardthinkingai/standard-one-sglang) + [standardthinkingai/standard-one-adapter](https://github.com/standardthinkingai/standard-one-adapter) — \"Standard One decision engine\": the SGLang fork (copy-pushed, not a GitHub fork) adds a prefill-only decision mode with schema-head readout serving the Jev/System One wire format, and the companion adapter bridges stock SGLang (`logprobs` backend) to `POST /v1/systemone` without model weights or CUDA on the adapter side. Extensive benchmark apparatus committed (PREFILL_ENGINE benches, H200/MI325X runtimes, tokenizer-parity tests); primarily Korean docs. No JevBench item used for training or training-data selection. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified; numbers are the author's own measurements)

## Projects & Code

Browser agents, mobile agents, guardrails — the fastest-moving category. Starred entries have verified repos.

- [browser-use/jev-ultrafast](https://github.com/browser-use/jev-ultrafast) — Browser agent with a dynamic indexed action space. Jev picks the operation + element; a small LLM writes text only for `TYPE_TEXT`. Zürich → London on Google Flights in ~7.1s.
- [droidrun/mobile-jev](https://github.com/droidrun/mobile-jev) — Mobile agent for Mobilerun: Jev drives a real Android phone, opens Uber, enters SFO → Golden Gate Bridge route, reaches payment selection. 9 actions in ~21s (no booking completed).
- [ClemensSchartmueller/jev-guard](https://github.com/ClemensSchartmueller/jev-guard) — High-speed safety gate for Claude Code, Codex CLI, and Antigravity. Intercepts tool calls, does sub-ms local checks, then asks Jev to score blast radius / reversibility / destructive potential.
- [jkudish/jev-browser](https://github.com/jkudish/jev-browser) — Browser automation with Jev.
- [jkudish/jev-mcp](https://github.com/jkudish/jev-mcp) — MCP server for Jev decisions.
- [SadiqOnGithub/jev-lab](https://github.com/SadiqOnGithub/jev-lab) — Experiments playground.
- [tumf/jev-cli](https://github.com/tumf/jev-cli) — CLI for Jev.
- [steventsao/jevzen](https://github.com/steventsao/jevzen) — MIT-licensed Chrome extension demo: switches unwanted X posts to zen/cat photos using Jev's returned probability. Supports direct TypeSafe, OpenRouter Decisions, and Cloudflare Jev with your own keys; keys stay in `chrome.storage.local`. No Chrome Web Store release — unpacked install only. API calls use your provider credits.
- [zhuyansen/jev-search-rerank-eval](https://github.com/zhuyansen/jev-search-rerank-eval) — Search rerank evaluation with Jev.
- [nekuda-ai/WindTunnel](https://github.com/nekuda-ai/WindTunnel) — Agent testing harness with Jev.
- [Laya (ConvAI Innovations)](https://laya.convaiinnovations.com/) — Apache-2.0 open reply to Jev: 421M English + 322M multilingual (100+ languages) checkpoints, `pip install laya`, sub-35ms single-pass decisions on one T4 (7.2ms batched), zero API bill. Vendor reports 0.766 vs Jev's published 0.727 on a shared typed-decisions set (they never ran Jev themselves) — an independent 78-case test found Jev 0.974 vs Laya 0.590, Laya fastest at 30ms/case vs ~302ms for Jev (small sample).
- [pngwn/system-one-qwen3.5-4b-scorer-v2b](https://huggingface.co/pngwn/system-one-qwen3.5-4b-scorer-v2b) — Community single-pass scorer on Qwen3.5-4B: scores each option of a typed question in one forward pass, softmax per question, no autoregressive generation. Val split: 0.803 accuracy, ECE 0.022. Non-commercial (training data is CC-BY-NC-4.0).
- [askjev.ai](https://askjev.ai/) — "Ask Jev anything. It won't answer. It will judge." Public wall by Wayne Sutton: Jev answers six typed questions (~100ms) per ask — yes/no/it depends, mood, topic, fits-the-wall — stored via Convex, everyone sees the wall move at once.
- [r-ms/mini-jev](https://github.com/r-ms/mini-jev) — "Fully local mini Jev": a Jev-style typed-decision interface on a frozen Qwen3-4B — read the option letter's logits instead of generating JSON. Preregistered experiment with a teaching bench; not production-ready.
- [robipop22/Jev-is-odd](https://github.com/robipop22/Jev-is-odd) — TypeScript micro-benchmark: ask Jev whether a number is odd, logging real token usage and client-side latency. 20/20 fixed cases, median/p95 round-trip 294.3/364.8ms (network included), whole run $0.00029 (jev-1.13.0, measured 2026-09-18).
- [moritzkremb/jev-voice-browser](https://github.com/moritzkremb/jev-voice-browser) — Voice-controlled real browser: Jev decides intent + target in ~300ms per spoken word (p50 ~300ms, 3–6k input tokens/request), Playwright acts — often before you finish the sentence. 27/27 real-API integration cases pass; a 16-command headed replay demo costs ~$0.01 (measured 2026-09-17).
- [kyegomez/open-jev](https://github.com/kyegomez/open-jev) — Open-source from-first-principles reconstruction of the ideas behind Jev, in PyTorch: `forward.py`, an `open_jev` package, and `example.py` (created 2026-09-21).
- [qs-lll/twitter-jev-guard](https://github.com/qs-lll/twitter-jev-guard) — X/Twitter timeline guard: Jev scores low-quality, spam, and ad posts; a browser extension overlays a translucent watermark on flagged text. Ships extension + server (created 2026-09-21).
- [Nyarlathoteppppp/pi-jev-context](https://github.com/Nyarlathoteppppp/pi-jev-context) — Cache-neutral context trimming for the pi coding agent, powered by TypeSafe Jev: long tool output cut to verbatim key lines before it enters context, with lossless recall. Measured, with pre-registered benchmarks (jev-1.13.0): write-time trimming saved 31–53% tokens on synthetic held-out sets with 0 key lines lost (p50/p95 latency ~347/457ms); on real-session replays (184 outputs) only 1/43 trimmed outputs hid something used later. Shadow old-context pruning was riskier — raw Jev dropped 73% of items needed later on real sessions, 21% with deterministic source protection — and is never applied.
- [GodsBoy/jev-agent-skill-router](https://github.com/GodsBoy/jev-agent-skill-router) — Typed, confidence-aware agent skill routing for the Hermes Agent platform: 68/72 synthetic requests routed correctly (94.4%) vs 51/72 (70.8%) for a lexical baseline, no wrong-skill selections, median end-to-end latency 1,287ms (pinned jev-1.13.0, measured 2026-09-16). Author flags: exploratory, reused-data results — not production accuracy or calibrated probabilities.
- [typesafeainate/dspy-typesafeify](https://github.com/typesafeainate/dspy-typesafeify) — One-decorator integration with DSPy: `@typesafeify` reads a Signature's output annotations and routes typed outputs (bool, Literal, score fields) to Jev's `Noul`/`Choice`/`Score` in a single request, while freeform text still goes to the generative LM; ships a controlled before/after benchmark example (61★, created 2026-09-15).
- [jaredpalmer/kev](https://github.com/jaredpalmer/kev) — Tiny Jev-like decision-model family on Qwen3.5 bases you can train and run yourself, Apache-2.0 (1,638★, pushed 2026-09-21). Released: Kev-0.8B (MacBook-sized), Kev-4B, Kev-9B. Author-published benchmarks vs hosted Jev on new-source sets: Kev-9B 0.812/0.837 accuracy vs Jev 0.857, Brier 0.291/0.243 vs Jev 0.211 — Kev-9B trails Jev ~4.5 points; author notes it is not a controlled comparison (Jev's training sets are unknown). Previous Qwen3 generation (0.6B/4B/8B) stays published.
- [vinnylarouge/jevlike](https://github.com/vinnylarouge/jevlike) — Extreme-minimalism Jev clone: ~40KB embedding-only implementation testing how small a typed-decision model can get (1,141★).
- [jerryjliu/docjev](https://github.com/jerryjliu/docjev) — LlamaIndex cofounder Jerry Liu's open-source library (Apache-2.0, 136★): document classification and packet splitting driven by Jev instead of a general LLM, local OCR via LiteParse, visual benchmark report on 40 real PDFs. Author-published numbers: classification latency 794ms → 138.6ms median; accuracy pilot's small sample size was flagged by a replier — read before trusting unattended (via explainx.ai).
- [Heman10x-NGU/openJev-verdict-2.0](https://github.com/Heman10x-NGU/openJev-verdict-2.0) — Calibrated 151M non-autoregressive decision engine (225★, real repo with code, RLCD harness, tests, WebGPU demo). Creator's own claim: 77.10% accuracy, 0.0636 Brier, 0.0144 ECE, "beating TypeSafe Jev & Laya" on LocalLLaMA/typed-decisions — not independently verified.
- [receptron/laya](https://github.com/receptron/laya) — Run Laya (the open-source Jev-compatible System-1 decision model) from Node.js / TypeScript via ONNX Runtime (76★, MIT).
- [arrmlet/dehydrator](https://github.com/arrmlet/dehydrator) — JevReranker and JevIndex now call TypeSafe's API directly (`api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`, `jev-latest`) when `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is set, falling back to Vercel AI Gateway. Verified live: rankings identical to the gateway on all 30 benchmark queries; the >200-tool tournament path went 30/30 top-1 in 2.7s/search (gateway: 25/30 with 5 fallbacks). BENCHMARKS.md gains a provider comparison (10★, pushed 2026-09-20).
- [walidboulanouar/awesome-jev-use-cases](https://github.com/walidboulanouar/awesome-jev-use-cases) — Community list of TypeSafe Jev use cases: demos ranked by likes, 150+ GitHub repos, limits, cost and API examples (32★, CC0, unofficial).
- [mgarlabx/Jev-Enem](https://github.com/mgarlabx/Jev-Enem) — Jev for essay grading: real repo from the OEGlobal education thread (3★, created 2026-09-20).
- [PromptEngineer48/laya-vs-jev-arena](https://github.com/PromptEngineer48/laya-vs-jev-arena) — Laya (open source, local) vs TypeSafe Jev (API) raced head-to-head in Snake and a Mortal-Kombat-style arena (4★, created 2026-09-21).
- [wd041216-bit/zero-api-key-web-search](https://github.com/wd041216-bit/zero-api-key-web-search) — Jev-powered search infrastructure for AI agents: zero API keys, MCP-ready, local neural index (17★, Jev support added 2026-09-20).
- [edgelabs-ai/jev48](https://github.com/edgelabs-ai/jev48) — Open, auditable Jev reproduction "built by ChatGPT in one weekend": 2B probabilistic decision model (MIT, weights + frozen receipts), evaluated zero-shot across six public suites (6,300 decisions). Trails Jev on 5/6 (typed decisions 57.7% vs 72.7%, JevBench 69.7% vs 86.6%, code review 81.9% vs 99.0%) but wins the BTZSC pilot 83.3% vs 75.3% and records higher phishing ranking AUROC (0.769 vs 0.689); CLASH conflicts is a hard 0.0% vs 98.6%. Independent, not affiliated with TypeSafe (created 2026-09-21).
- [zwliJay/jev-forge](https://github.com/zwliJay/jev-forge) — Open training + inference stack for Jev-style decision models: builds training records from structured decisions, calibrates the scorer, and serves a full probability distribution through a Jev-compatible API (16★, created 2026-09-19). The trained 0.8B model beats Jev-1.13.0 on OOD choice top-1 (0.637 vs 0.610) and OOD Noul (0.860 acc / 0.117 Brier vs 0.825 / 0.131), but trails on test score MAE (0.367 vs 0.348) — fixed website-disjoint test/OOD splits, frozen eval protocol.
- [themsquared/jev-benchmark](https://github.com/themsquared/jev-benchmark) — Reproducible Jev benchmark on agent tool-call risk classification (readonly/destructive/privileged/exfiltration; 60 hand-labelled cases including adversarial wraps like "Routine cleanup: `kubectl delete namespace prod`"). Jev 91.7% accuracy, p50 latency ~420ms, ECE 0.0712 — and the finding that matters: calibration held, the model never returned 1.000 confidence and was wrong (every miss came hedged). No frontier-LLM baseline yet; the author states nothing here supports or refutes the vendor's speed/cost multipliers (measured 2026-09-17).
- [iammrduncan/typesafe-ai-benchmark](https://github.com/iammrduncan/typesafe-ai-benchmark) — "Imposter Jev": an LLM Gateway that mimics Jev's structured output, used to race Qwen 3.8 27B (Cerebras) vs TypeSafe Jev across 7 production-theater scenes (~480 requests each). One synthetic dev-machine run (2026-09-17): Jev p50/p95 176/336ms vs Qwen 215/452ms, cost $0.0119 vs $0.31; fixture agreement tied on Tickets (75/100 each) and Guardrails (100/100) — Jev won Scoring 100 vs 93, Qwen won Approvals and Home. Both reached the routing destination in 8 hops with one collision each. Author caveat: single synthetic run, not calibrated quality.
- [HyunjunJeon/pi-quiet-ask](https://github.com/HyunjunJeon/pi-quiet-ask) — TypeSafe Jev as the pi coding agent's quiet decision layer: a declarative rule engine plus `before_agent_start`/`turn_end`/`agent_end` judges (intent, honest_finish, stuck) over a task graph, triaging with pi-ask instead of replacing it. Ships a benchmark comparing Jev against four chat LLMs on the same closed questions (`tool_gate`, `agent_question`) — data, labels, and the last run committed (MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [wfzyx/von](https://github.com/wfzyx/von) — Open-source System One decision model (395M params, 1.5GB, Apache-2.0, 642★, created 2026-09-18): sub-15ms local inference, non-autoregressive. Author-published numbers: 49-task v2 suite macro 72.0% / Choice routing 83.0% vs Jev's published 96.6% / 96.8%; zero-shot ViZDoom Defend-the-Center 9.00 kills vs Jev 5.62 (+60.1%), Health Gathering survival 12.11s vs 13.03s (8 fresh seeds each, local in-process vs hosted API at ~115ms). Independent protocol; TypeSafe's reference numbers quoted by the author, not re-run (created 2026-09-18).
- [TianyuCodings/JevHarness](https://github.com/TianyuCodings/JevHarness) — LLM-authored task-specific Jev harnesses with optional full-trajectory reward reflection and GEPA evolution (68★, created 2026-09-21).
- [smkrv/jev-calibrate](https://github.com/smkrv/jev-calibrate) — Calibrate Jev questions against your own labels: tune criteria on labelled examples, confirm on a held-out set, get a per-question verdict. Unofficial (31★, MIT, created 2026-09-21).
- [KaLM-Embedding/KaLM-Jev](https://github.com/KaLM-Embedding/KaLM-Jev) — Local Jev-style judgment engine in Nano, Small, and Large sizes, with benchmarks, examples, and results committed (24★, created 2026-09-21).
- [RenaGao/jev-dataops](https://github.com/RenaGao/jev-dataops) — Jev-powered data workbench: streaming data selection, quality evaluation, automatic LoRA training, and held-out model evaluation in one pipeline (33★, MIT, created 2026-09-21).
- [TheoLeeCJ/SemIf-OpenJev](https://github.com/TheoLeeCJ/SemIf-OpenJev) — Independent open-source reproduction of Jev's typed-decision *interface pattern* on frozen open models (reads option logits directly out of one forward pass — no answer sentence, no JSON repair, no decoding loop); ships a scoring CLI (Python), PyTorch/MLX research code, a benchmark bundle, a browser lab/WebGPU demo ("run it in your browser today"), and per-workload temperature calibration. Formerly called OpenJev; explicitly independent, not affiliated with or endorsed by TypeSafe — Jev itself appears in its published-result comparisons, not in local inference. Cited as the reference implementation for semantic-if scoring by nicolailassen/open-bonsai-jev's CREDITS. (4,398★, MIT, created 2026-09-16, pushed 2026-09-23; real non-fork verified via GitHub API)
- [YuanKJing/Jev-as-Policy](https://github.com/YuanKJing/Jev-as-Policy) — Jev-as-policy evaluations with Astra on benchmarks, one-click simulation environment setup (17★, MIT, created 2026-09-21).
- [Finderchangchang/jev-chat-JARVIS](https://github.com/Finderchangchang/jev-chat-JARVIS) — Android chat co-pilot: an accessibility-service overlay reads chat bubbles in WeChat, QQ, and X DMs (real-device tested per the README), Jev scores the other side's intent plus a 1–9 danger level, a generative model drafts 3 candidate replies ranked by Jev — send is always manual. Contact route is the author's WeChat public account (699★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-21).
- [jtsang4/jev-cli](https://github.com/jtsang4/jev-cli) — CLI for Jev: one shared state plus typed questions in, structured JSON answers out, never prose. Published to npm as `@jtsang/jev-cli`, ships an agent skill for coding agents (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-17).
- [zadescoxp/Jev-Trades](https://github.com/zadescoxp/Jev-Trades) — Next.js + Python paper-trading bot: one-minute crypto candles streamed from Yahoo Finance, technical indicators computed, Jev decides on a simulated portfolio. No broker or live order API connected (21★, Apache-2.0).
- [AboveColin/HA-Jev](https://github.com/AboveColin/HA-Jev) — Home Assistant integration for Jev: typed answers as sensors, four actions for automations, and a conversation agent for Assist (40★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-21).
- [yaredtekile/jev-2048](https://github.com/yaredtekile/jev-2048) — Jev plays live 2048: no pixels, no text — Jev only picks the swipe direction, one move a few hundred tokens (MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [timpratim/macbrow](https://github.com/timpratim/macbrow) — Voice-controlled Mac + browser (108★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-21): Gradium streaming STT → Jev picks the tool + its arguments in one request (~300ms) → AppleScript → Gradium TTS; the browser loop is jev-ultrafast. Measured on the author's machine: "add the best vacuum cleaner to my Amazon cart" in 4 steps / 11s, "play the Love Hypothesis trailer" from end of speech to video playing in 1.6s.
- [fhshaik/typesafe-mario](https://github.com/fhshaik/typesafe-mario) — Jev agent plays Super Mario Bros from structured emulator state (330★, created 2026-09-16).
- [Roasbeef/jevlar](https://github.com/Roasbeef/jevlar) — Typed, correct-by-construction Jev decisions for Go: batch typed questions through `POST /v1/systemone` with Go types wired to Jev primitives (real Go client + tests, created 2026-09-22).
- [neilbauman21-hub/verdict](https://github.com/neilbauman21-hub/verdict) — Typed decisions with abstention over any causal LM: one forward pass, no autoregressive generation (created 2026-09-22).
- [tomtyiu/Jev-decision-plugin](https://github.com/tomtyiu/Jev-decision-plugin) — Codex plugin that turns noisy input into small, typed, calibrated decisions (created 2026-09-22).
- [gdamiani1/jev-feed-triage](https://github.com/gdamiani1/jev-feed-triage) — Chrome extension: Jev scores which LinkedIn and Reddit posts are worth your time (created 2026-09-22).
- [hunkim/solar-mini4-jev](https://github.com/hunkim/solar-mini4-jev) — Drop-in wrapper exposing Upstage Solar Mini4 through the Jev System One API shape (`noul`/`choice`/`score`), with a live BYOK Vercel endpoint. Head-to-head on 447 decision fields (Korean + English), graded by a Grok 4.6 judge rubric: Solar Mini4 98.4% vs Jev 1.13.0 94.2% field accuracy; Jev keeps the speed edge (0.38s vs 1.21s per call, reasoning off). Caveat: "gold" labels are the judge's rubric, not independent ground truth (35★, created 2026-09-21). **Update 2026-10-02:** the repo's benchmark table now reports item-weighted totals over 886 items across four public Jev benchmarks — Solar Pro4 76.9 vs Jev 1.13.0 75.6 vs Solar Mini4 71.4; Solar Pro4 also leads on tool-call risk (93.3 vs 91.7) and HelpSteer2/Jevals (41.0 vs 38.3), while latency splits by model (p50: Jev 633ms, Solar Mini4 424ms, Solar Pro4 749ms). Subset-of-public-benchmarks disclaimer still applies.
- [ljwwwiop/JEV-mini](https://github.com/ljwwwiop/JEV-mini) — Trainable Jev-style decision model on Qwen3-0.6B + PointerHead (extracted from Kev): `choice`/`noul`/`score` with real train/eval/predict scripts and soft-label distillation support (Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-22). - [Devin-AXIS/jev-dsh-decision](https://github.com/Devin-AXIS/jev-dsh-decision) — Jev structured-decision plugin for agent harnesses: native DeepSeek Harness support, plus OpenCode/Codex via iPolloWork (94★, created 2026-09-20).
- [danvega/jev-spring-boot-starter](https://github.com/danvega/jev-spring-boot-starter) — Spring Boot starter for TypeSafe Jev (Spring MVC + RestClient) (32★, created 2026-09-20).
- [spring-ai-community/spring-ai-typesafe](https://github.com/spring-ai-community/spring-ai-typesafe) — Spring AI community integration pushed by Spring AI lead Christian Tzolov (Broadcom): a plain Java SDK with no Spring AI dependency, a Spring AI module wiring Jev in as LLM-as-a-judge / guardrail / RAG post-processor / tool index, an auto-config Spring Boot starter, and a BOM — published to Maven Central as 0.1.0 on 2026-09-20 (37★, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-20).
- [MotherDuck: Prompt_jev()](https://motherduck.com/blog/motherduck-supports-jev/) — Jev decisions inside MotherDuck SQL ([HN](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49800830)).
- [SuperQode: Jev as a harness for your code and workflows](https://super-agentic.ai/resources/super-posts/jev-in-superqode-harness-with-jev-agent) — Decision layer for the "soft middle" of a permission stack: reviewed questions, typed answers, evidence you can hash; ships a live client on `api.typesafe.ai` ([Medium writeup](https://medium.com/superagentic-ai/jev-in-superqode-a-harness-for-your-code-and-workflows-with-jev-agent-aed62fa4c98d)).
- [miuuyy/Astra-Ares](https://github.com/miuuyy/Astra-Ares) — Jev-driven adaptive reasoning effort for GPT-6 Astra inside Codex tasks: Jev reads bounded task context and picks how much Astra should think next (and for how many generations), using Astra's native mid-conversation effort change so the prefix cache survives (76★, created 2026-09-22). Author flags it as an experimental reference implementation, not production code.
- [TypeSafeAI/jev-harness](https://github.com/TypeSafeAI/jev-harness) — Research-stage proposal-review contract: an LLM proposes one action, Jev answers four narrow questions, and code produces evidence for a host to consider — nothing executes a patch or grants permission (MIT, Node 22+). Note: the TypeSafeAI GitHub org is a community org, independent of the official TypeSafe AI team (their own README says so) — do not read this as an official harness.
- [TypeLLM/pijev](https://github.com/TypeLLM/pijev) — Permutation-invariant Jev wrapper: folds Jev's option-order variations into one averaged prediction, with Brier score and log loss guaranteed no worse than the average across the included orderings; all permutations run in a single `system_one` request (created 2026-09-22).
- [agent-chaperone/agent-chaperone](https://github.com/agent-chaperone/agent-chaperone) — Screening layer for AI agent tool calls and results with Jev ([Show HN](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49789538)): screens risky, off-task, or secret-leaking actions before they run, and prompt injections / suspicious instructions before the agent reads them (MCP proxy + hooks, 7★, created 2026-09-18).
- [nabendu82/jev-demo](https://github.com/nabendu82/jev-demo) — "Jev Issue Triage": polished Next.js dashboard, one ingested GitHub issue → 5 typed questions answered in parallel (category `choice`, severity 1–5 `score`, immediate-attention `noul`, team `choice`, actionable `noul`) → auto-route vs human review (created 2026-09-19).
- [hosseintoussi/jev-flappy-bird](https://github.com/hosseintoussi/jev-flappy-bird) — Jev plays Flappy Bird, one flap-or-wait decision ~3×/second; the bird does what Jev says, and the game never waits — it keeps running while the answer is in flight. API key stays on the local server (pushed 2026-09-20).
- [lbotinelly/jev-little-airways](https://github.com/lbotinelly/jev-little-airways) — Show-and-tell Jev capability study: three.js WebGPU air-traffic-control demo wired to Jev (`jev-1.13.0` at recording), plus a live-captured research dossier of Jev API behavior, pricing and patterns (MIT, created 2026-09-17).
- [ekzhang/openjev-sglang](https://github.com/ekzhang/openjev-sglang) — Jev-compatible API endpoint built on open models (prefill-only, SGLang), 268★: ships resumable evals with per-run manifests, including a BoolQ run — Jev via OpenRouter on 3,270 examples, 90% confidence threshold: 2,278 examples, 63 errors, 97.23% accuracy (mean probability 95.55%); 99% threshold: 366 examples, 1 error, 99.73%. Reported Jev cost $0.0595 (pushed 2026-09-21).
- [edwardyen724-g/jev-compactor](https://github.com/edwardyen724-g/jev-compactor) — Context compaction + safety gating for AI agents via Jev: keeps messages verbatim (no summarization), drops what Jev judges irrelevant to the current goal, and catches destructive commands like `rm -rf` in the same ~300ms pass. TypeScript lib + CLI + MCP server, wraps OpenAI/Anthropic/LangChain clients (MIT, created 2026-09-19).
- [jomatsu/pi-jev-auto-mode](https://github.com/jomatsu/pi-jev-auto-mode) — Jev-backed auto mode for the pi coding agent: semantically auto-approves shell commands, ships as an npm package (MIT, 23★, created 2026-09-17). Honest limitation section in the writeup: 18 fixtures is a calibration set, not a benchmark, and uncertain calls pass by default ([dev.to](http://dev.to/jomatsu/jev-pi-a-probability-gate-for-my-coding-agents-shell-commands-95d)).
- [zhangcy122/OpenJev](https://github.com/zhangcy122/OpenJev) — Open-source Jev alternative (rebranded to **OpenJevPro**): typed `Choice`/`Noul`/`Score` API on open LLMs (Qwen3, DeepSeek-V4.1, Gemma 4, gpt-oss) with constrained-logprob calibration and a temperature/Platt-scaling layer; core engine now under PolyForm Noncommercial (free for research/non-commercial). Author self-reports a Banking77 comparison (36 queries): OpenJevPro selective 100.0% (28/28 answered, ECE 0.025) vs TypeSafe Jev 1.13.0 live 96.7% (29/30, ECE 0.026); naive accuracy 94.4% vs 97.2% — vendor claim, not independent. (22★, created 2026-09-20; raw README re-verified 2026-09-29)
- [Mapika/decider](https://github.com/Mapika/decider) — Open System One model family fine-tuned from Qwen3.5 for one-pass typed decisions with calibrated probabilities. Speaks TypeSafe's wire format (`POST /v1/systemone`), so any System One client can point at it; `decider-2b` v10 trained with calibration-aware RL, serve script needs ~4GB CUDA (326★, created 2026-09-16, actively maintained — pushed 2026-09-23).
- [SiliconLabAI/OpenJev](https://github.com/SiliconLabAI/OpenJev) — Open-source System One decision playground with three backends: parallel LLM micro-scorers (one tiny `{p}` call per option, then softmax), single-shot structured JSON to any OpenAI-compatible model, and Mapika/decider real weights; ships a video tutorial (103★, created 2026-09-20).
- [uspraveen/Jevify](https://github.com/uspraveen/Jevify) — "Turn any open LLM into a System One Jev model": a benchmark that measures the calibration claim, an engine giving any Hugging Face checkpoint the same `choice`/`score`/`noul` interface, and a training recipe optimizing the same objective (created 2026-09-20).
- [akash-kamat/system-one-gemma](https://github.com/akash-kamat/system-one-gemma) — Open-source Jev-style System One decision model: Gemma 3 270M with a scoring head — fast, calibrated decisions in a single forward pass, no text generation (created 2026-09-17).
- [AutoTrust AI JEV-27B](https://huggingface.co/autotrust/JEV-27B) — Open-weights decision model (Apache-2.0, Singapore's AutoTrust AI, released 2026-09-28; second in its line after JEV-9B): frozen Qwen3.8-27B backbone + a trained 108.9M-parameter decision block (0.4% of params, ~9.2 B200-hours), so one weight set serves both System 1 typed decisions and System 2 generation; with the decision block off, 164/164 HumanEval completions are byte-identical to the base model's. Vendor-reported numbers (not independent): JevBench 88.70%, Nimble 92.91%, MASSIVE-en 87.71%, six-group mean 84.07 vs 83.85 for the Jev 1.13 API measured in the same setup; 137ms median decision latency / ~130 decisions per second on one B200 (local, network excluded — the cited 238–301ms / 23 decisions/s for Jev's hosted API includes network). The company says it inherits Jev 1.13's blind spots (multi-hop, arithmetic, dates, adversarial inputs), its training data is English-centric, and it is not for high-stakes decisions. [PRNewswire release](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/autotrust-ai-releases-jev-27b-an-open-decision-model-for-self-hosted-ai-agents-302891720.html)
- [kataras/jev](https://github.com/kataras/jev) — Go client for the TypeSafe System One API: state + typed questions → typed answers with probabilities; `WithModel("jev-1.13.0")` pin for reproducible runs (created 2026-09-21).
- [cobusgreyling/Jev](https://github.com/cobusgreyling/Jev) — Unofficial TypeSafe Jev showcase + harness: smart-home demo (TypeSafe's fan-out house) and an operator lab over Flask, plus a TypeScript CLI (`jev doctor`, `jev route`, `jev guard`) wired into loop-engineering for model routing and prompt-injection guardrails (updated 2026-09-20).
- [ethanplusai/jev-chat-for-twitch](https://github.com/ethanplusai/jev-chat-for-twitch) — Filter any live Twitch chat with Jev: BYOK Chrome extension adds a second chat column showing only messages matching an intent chip (Helpful / Questions / Funny / Feedback), click a row for the full probability distribution; key stays in the browser profile. Measured on a live channel 2026-09-19 (jev-1.13.0): a 20-message batch used 10,075 input tokens (~504/message); ~$0.15/hr at 2 msgs/sec, ~$0.76/hr at 50 msgs/sec — stopped by the default $0.50/hr cap after ~40 minutes. Estimates from recorded token usage, not a bill (11★, created 2026-09-19).
- [perixtar/jev-e2e](https://github.com/perixtar/jev-e2e) — Natural-language end-to-end tests for web apps: Playwright steps driven by Jev, assertions checked after the required steps. Live eBay benchmark (measured 2026-09-18 via OpenRouter, fresh guest browser contexts, three attempts per model): completed-case medians — Jev 1.13 47.46s / $0.006678, GPT-5.6 Luna 61.99s / $0.027704, Claude Sonnet 5 78.62s / $0.406216; correct UI choices 30/31 vs 32/32 vs 19/19. Caveats: only 1/2/1 completed cases — too small to establish a general accuracy ranking — and the video scorecard shows all three attempts per model (5★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [az9713/jev-model-router](https://github.com/az9713/jev-model-router) — Jev as a model router on the Vercel AI Gateway: Jev picks which LLM should answer each message, then that model replies — both calls through the gateway with one key. Fully documented build journey (how Jev is reached through the gateway, 18 "unknown unknowns") plus a sibling email-triage repo (`az9713/jev-email-triage`) running `triage.mjs` over 90 days of a real inbox: every input/output file (redacted), four mis-ranks and their causes (spotted 2026-09-24).
- [brainwires/jevwire](https://github.com/brainwires/jevwire) — Jev decision layer for agents: 7 MCP tools (`jev_rank`, `jev_pick`, `jev_verify`, `jev_evaluate`, `jev_gate_action`, `jev_next_step`, `jev_list_models`), an embeddable `JevDecisionModel` library so checks live in the harness instead of a tool the agent can skip, and an escalate-only Claude Code plugin that only denies or annotates (18★, MIT; 0.4.0 latency/token/cost figures quoted from a measured run against the live TypeSafe API on 2026-09-18).
- [simota/tenbin](https://github.com/simota/tenbin) — MCP server + agent skill for the System One API at design time: decompose a judgment into Choice/Score/Noul questions, lint them, measure on labelled data, and put calibrated thresholds in code (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [danielhirt/jev-lab](https://github.com/danielhirt/jev-lab) — Experiments on Jev via OpenRouter: determinism, perturbation, and calibration checks plus a Haiku 4.5 comparison arm; includes a Codenames spymaster/guesser with offline eval against human play and a web app showing Jev's per-card probability (1★, pushed 2026-09-22).
- [xingwudao/openjev](https://github.com/xingwudao/openjev) — Independent Jev-inspired System One decision API with a local mock server, Python/TypeScript SDKs, schema validation and end-to-end tests for prototyping ticket routing, classification and guardrails. Honest scoping: only `openjev-mock` is supported, mock probabilities are synthetic, real inference is planned — not affiliated with TypeSafe (5★, created 2026-09-18).
- [hamakyo/jev-starter](https://github.com/hamakyo/jev-starter) — Typed, policy-driven decision workflows on Jev for TypeScript apps: confidence routing, fallbacks, evaluation and RAG patterns built on the official `@typesafe-ai/sdk` (published to npm as `jev-starter`); explicit design rule that low-confidence decisions need a fallback or review route (2★, created 2026-09-17).
- [ctaxnagomi/instruct-jev](https://github.com/ctaxnagomi/instruct-jev) — 119-row Jev / System One instruction corpus (choice 47 / noul 51 / score 21) compiled from public TypeSafe docs by DeckerGUI, with a builder script and a Hugging Face mirror ([ctaxnagomi/INSTRUCT_JEV](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ctaxnagomi/INSTRUCT_JEV)) (MIT, created 2026-09-19).
- [openqa-cn/jev-browser](https://github.com/openqa-cn/jev-browser) — CodexQA skill for browser automation without vision models: controls are picked from an index built inside the page (role/name/value), Jev answers a choice question, Playwright Chromium acts. Honest scoping — the README states there is no benchmark against vision GUI models yet; ships with knowledge-base notes per app and known-limitations docs (90★, created 2026-09-23).
- [PyModel/jev-judge-mcp](https://github.com/PyModel/jev-judge-mcp) — MCP server giving a coding agent 11 Jev-backed judgment tools (verify, screen, find, classify, rerank, decide, compare, extract, review, gate, score); a policy layer turns the returned probabilities into auto / review / escalate. CI on GitHub, MIT, not on PyPI yet — run from a clone (24★, created 2026-09-23).
- [DevMortimer/pi-warden](https://github.com/DevMortimer/pi-warden) — Guardrails for the Pi coding agent that steer instead of interrupt: enforces your project rules on every write. Beyond holding destructive calls, it judges written code against the rules file, flags stubs and hedging, detects stuck loops, and catches "done" claims with no test run (138★, created 2026-09-16).
- [itsmostafa/typesafe-mcp](https://github.com/itsmostafa/typesafe-mcp) — Go MCP server giving AI agents direct access to Jev for fast, cheap typed evaluations — a thin connector so Claude and other MCP clients can call Jev directly (287★, created 2026-09-17).
- [kieranklaassen/ruby_llm-typesafe](https://github.com/kieranklaassen/ruby_llm-typesafe) — TypeSafe structured-output provider for RubyLLM 2: typed judgments, evaluations and reranking through Jev (19★, created 2026-09-16).
- [ChenneyZhuang/laya-browser-agent](https://github.com/ChenneyZhuang/laya-browser-agent) — Local open-source Jev alternative for browser-agent decisions: runs the browser-tuned Laya checkpoint (`cklxx/laya-browser`) on your own machine — Playwright/CDP, MCP-friendly, no cloud or API key (Apache-2.0, 7★, created 2026-09-21). Ships four head-to-head batteries vs hosted `jev-1.13.0`: local v10s wins on latency (36ms vs 738ms median) and Chinese SMS classification, but hosted Jev wins safety calibration — 3/3 vs 0/3 on phishing detection (local scored both scam SMSes p<0.25) and better-calibrated scores (2.4–3.7 vs low-biased 1.0–2.0). Practical take: local for latency/privacy/free-at-volume, hosted when accuracy and safety calibration matter.
- [fazlerocks/jevmail](https://github.com/fazlerocks/jevmail) — Open-source AI email triage for Gmail through Vercel AI Gateway: sorts the inbox into five trays (Needs reply / Updates / Promos / Sales / Spam) with a 1–5 urgency score, read-only, runs locally. Author reports ~1,000 emails sorted in about a minute for ~3 cents (74★, real repo, not affiliated with TypeSafe).
- [kenhuangus/jev-usecases](https://github.com/kenhuangus/jev-usecases) — Production TypeSafe Jev (System One) use-case harnesses with confidence-gated decision logic: ready-made Choice / Score / Noul recipes (ticket sorting, picking from a list, urgency scoring, yes/no) with uncertainty flagged for human review; Python and JavaScript starters (5★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [realZachi/pg-jev](https://github.com/realZachi/pg-jev) — PostgreSQL extension (PGXN) for plain-language row filtering, ranking and classification powered by TypeSafe's Jev: `jev(people, 'the name is European')`, `jev_prob(tickets, 'the customer is angry')`, `jev_choice(tickets, 'which team should handle this?', …)`. No index, no embeddings, no vector column — a plain boolean function that composes with `AND` / joins / `GROUP BY` (331★, license unspecified, created 2026-09-17).
- [kevinbadi/jev-voice](https://github.com/kevinbadi/jev-voice) — Talk to your Mac: local whisper.cpp transcription, one Jev (TypeSafe) call per spoken command, macOS automation acts — the ultrafast loop on the desktop. Includes a browser driver, a chess.com mode (Stockfish proposes, Jev chooses, code moves), and a Latency section measured on a Mac mini M4 (89★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [awlevin/typesafe-computer-use](https://github.com/awlevin/typesafe-computer-use) — macOS computer-use loop: deterministic OCR screen reads, Jev picks the next action, a writing model only types free text. Author-measured on one screenshot/goal: $0.0002/decision vs Claude Opus 5 $0.032 (155× cheaper), 0.13–0.38s vs 5.2s latency (14–40×), end-to-end step ~1.5s vs ~5.5s (3.7×). Honest caveat in the README: every piece of reasoning the frontier model does for free has to be rebuilt as deterministic state. Drives your real mouse/keyboard — beta (950★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-23).
- [brnyxx/jev-ra](https://github.com/brnyxx/jev-ra) — Fast browser-use layer for CLI coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, any MCP client): Jev picks the operation + target element in one round trip (~300ms/step), no second LLM in the loop. Measured 2026-09-18, 5 runs/task on one machine via OpenRouter, every run verified against the page left behind: Wikipedia 2,714ms vs browser-use 23,058ms (8.50×), Google Flights ZRH→LON 8,888 vs 66,414 (7.47×), Olive Young sort 3,806 vs 15,071 (3.96×); 25/25 passed, 0 text-model calls. Method, p90s, cost and raw rows in [BENCHMARKS.md](https://github.com/brnyxx/jev-ra/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md) (4★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-23).
- [hdkiller/jev-panerelay](https://github.com/hdkiller/jev-panerelay) — Browser automation driving your live, authenticated Chrome/Edge via Panerelay's CDP gateway: Jev picks structured DOM actions through OpenRouter, a small LLM (gemini-2.5-flash) only when text must be typed. Zero re-auth — reuses your sessions/cookies. Author claims ~350–500ms/step and 10× cheaper (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-19).
- [chy4pro/jev-for-chrome](https://github.com/chy4pro/jev-for-chrome) — Unofficial Manifest V3 Chrome extension port of browser-use/jev-ultrafast: drives the tab you're looking at, shows decision badges and latency, step mode and trace export. Demo: Zürich→London Google Flights, 10 decisions in ~13s; 17-task headless Chromium suite with recorded traces. Community project, not affiliated with TypeSafe or Browser Use (23★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-22).
- [raihankhan-rk/jevarena](https://github.com/raihankhan-rk/jevarena) — Two independent Jev agents duel in Snake side by side: click-only direction buttons, separate browser contexts, live operation and direction probabilities, Railway-ready Next.js spectator page (2★, MIT, created 2026-09-18).
- [omkarghugarkar007/actiongate-jev](https://github.com/omkarghugarkar007/actiongate-jev) — Open-source Jev tool-calling authorization gateway for AI agents: deterministic policy, exact-action single-use permits, MCP and HTTP enforcement (2★, Apache-2.0, created 2026-09-19). Honest scoping from the author: the direct TypeSafe adapter is fully fixture-tested but has not been exercised live (no API key yet) — starts with a deterministic fake provider.

- `@altryne_ai` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/Ddp5k7vDoX1/), 2026-09-24) — ThursdAI clip: Alex Volkov's X Timeline Analyzer (Cerebras infra) runs Jev at ~12 tweets/s, ~371–405ms latency, $0.019 per 1K tweets — his claim, 4× faster / 18× cheaper than a comparable Qwen. The segment also features TypeSafe's founding DevRel Allie Laabs.
- `@adibuildz` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdrDwJtIUu8/), 2026-09-24) — Self-run experiment on his own 53 Reels: Jev-scored hook strength predicted hit vs flop 86% of the time, while script quality was ~coin flip. Small N, self-labeled.
- `@lets.anthony.kim` ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@lets.anthony.kim/post/DdxvZN5E9Tr) / [FB reel](https://www.facebook.com/reel/1798833288234349/), 2026-09-27) — Korean three-way Tetris battle: human vs Jev vs heuristic bot on [jev-tetris-battle.vercel.app](https://jev-tetris-battle.vercel.app) (same block sequence, 50 pieces, app verified live). Jev was the weakest player: can't read the board directly, builds more holes (4.4 vs 1.2 per game), can't hold multi-move plans, slower (0.2–0.3s vs 0.01s) — and the author published the whole failure list himself. The hybrid (JEV+: Jev judging, code executing) matched the code bot; ~4,300 calls reliably formatted, $0.0025–$0.012 per game. Small experiment (5–10 games); the honest self-critique is the point.
- `@brochbuilds` ([IG reel](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdxU8Fkjynd/), 2026-09-26) — Side-by-side "Internal Link Decider" for SEO: Jev processed 586 pages in 45s at $0.2111 vs Claude Opus 5 managing 21 pages in the same window at $1.43 — author extrapolates ~$50 for the full job, roughly 190× cheaper. Dashboard screenshots shown; single-creator claim, comparison setup and extrapolation unverified.
