Author claims Jev beat span-01 on accuracy, latency and cost in all three (Jev above average student level on the Korean SAT, span-01 barely above guessing) and concludes current Jev users have little reason to switch; notes span-01 is pitched as a behavior-detection model for agent contexts, with a free lite version for easy experimentation. Single-author self-reported numbers, no public repo — recorded as seen, not verified.

Ships a replayable benchmark (79 scripted sessions, 261 labelled decisions, offline cassettes): v0.8.0 + Jev reaches 98.5% recall with 0.0% false blocks at ~$0.000058/task, vs 90.9% recall rules-only. - [kushals256/jevcache](https://github.com/kushals256/jevcache) — OpenAI-compatible local cache proxy: instead of picking a model it decides *whether to call one* — Jev judges "same intent" between differently-worded requests so repeats reuse the cached answer (fails open to a real call if Jev errors). Author's real-app numbers: MISS 3244ms → HIT 394ms (intent 0.93), est. $0.000137 saved per hit. `npx @kushalicious/jevcache`, node:sqlite, no native SQLite compile. (9★, MIT, created 2026-09-19)
- [nadeem4/jev-demo: agent-watchdog](https://github.com/nadeem4/jev-demo) — Claude Code pre-tool hook: before every tool call, Jev answers four typed questions (how reversible the call is, whether it matches what you actually asked for, whether the content looks like prompt injection, whether the agent is looping) and thresholds map the four probabilities to allow / ask / deny. Hard rules run first and never reach the model; on Jev error or >3s latency it asks the user instead of guessing. Same repo as the Decision Arena entry in Benchmarks below.

## Benchmarks & Evaluations

Numbers with sources. Vendor claims are labeled as such.

- 📊 [RoboKrunch: 10,000-robot fleet triage](https://github.com/robokrunch/jev-physical-ai) — 300 real decisions API calls against a simulated warehouse AMR fleet (bilingual CN/EN incidents), 3 simultaneous judgments per call (human escalation, owning team, urgency 0–2). **300/300 succeeded, p50 0.53s, p95 0.81s, $0.0000246/decision ($24.57 per million)**. Fleet-scale model (10K robots × 48 decisions/day × 30d): **$354/mo vs $1,814/mo for GPT-4o-mini — 5.1×**. Caveats: incidents are simulated (template agreement 91.3%, not production accuracy); GPT-4o-mini cost is estimated, not measured.
- 📊 [RoboKrunch: Jev vs self-hosted ModernBERT](https://github.com/robokrunch/jev-physical-ai) — `ModernBERT-base` (149M params) on a 2-core CPU: p50 169ms vs Jev's 527ms (~3× faster) but one label vs three judgments. Infra crossover ≈ **977K decisions/month** (~678 robots at 48/day) before self-hosting beats Jev on cost. Conclusion: Jev's edge is zero training, zero labeling, zero ops — not raw speed.
- [Aman Kumar: "Testing Jev on public and private data — classifier or filter?"](https://amankumar.ai/blogs/jev-measured) — ~16,000 real calls over two days. On short-text classification, level with or ahead of gpt-5.4-mini and gpt-5.6-luna on 3 of 4 public sets, at **5–56× lower cost, median under a second**. Probabilities hold near 0 and 1 (confident answers right 90–100%); the middle is a coin flip. Verdict: *"a filter, not a replacement"* — Jev takes the calls it's confident about, the rest fall back to your existing model. Every case, per-item answer and the scoring code published: [onlyoneaman/jev-eval](https://github.com/onlyoneaman/jev-eval) (1★, MIT, created 2026-09-18; real non-fork verified via GitHub API).
- [TypeSafe's own workflow evals (via MarkTechPost)](https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/09/19/typesafe-ai-releases-jev/) — Jev 0.114s / $0.000081 vs GPT-5.6 Terra 8.566s / $0.013880 (claimed 193.6× faster, 444.6× cheaper). Fine print: reference answer is the average of GPT-6 Astra and Fable 5.1; TypeSafe's own team wrote the workflows; TypeSafe says these gains sit at the high end and it cannot prove the price is unsubsidized.
- [Vercel: command safety](https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/09/19/typesafe-ai-releases-jev/) — Guillermo Rauch reported Jev up to **18× faster at p95** than GPT Luna for command-safety review, and more accurate (via engineer Pranit Sharma's benchmark).
- [Bryo AI: email triage](https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/09/19/typesafe-ai-releases-jev/) — CTO Nikhil Mudholkar found Gemini slightly more accurate but **10–20× more expensive** than Jev.
- [@ebrain.lab: Jev vs Claude Opus on 40 Korean sentences](https://www.threads.com/@ebrain.lab/post/DddGgXuoLlL) — One API call per sentence: Jev 40/40 correct in 1.9s for $0.0012; Claude Opus also 40/40 but ~24× more expensive. Caveat: prompting Jev like an LLM with the whole document dropped accuracy to 62% — commenters note batching increases drift.
- [@simplifyinai: 27 questions, one parallel request](https://www.threads.com/@simplifyinai/post/DdeAkQ9ksP9) — Jev answered 27 typed questions in a single parallel request in 0.11s for $0.000083, head-to-head vs GPT-5.6-texta on the same task (community test).
- [anisselbd/jev-phishing-bench](https://github.com/anisselbd/jev-phishing-bench) — Jev (jev-1.13.0) vs Claude Haiku 4.5 on 2,000 phishing emails (results 2026-09-17; reproducible repo with calibration audit). Jev: 62.6% accuracy, 43.2% recall on phishing, ECE 0.154, p50 239ms, $0.038 per 1K emails. Haiku 4.5: 81.3% accuracy, ECE 0.097, p50 687ms, $0.462 per 1K. Verdict: Jev loses on accuracy, wins on speed and cost.
- [OpenRouter: Jev vs 4 LLMs on a 30-class task (via TipRanks)](https://www.tipranks.com/news/private-companies/benchmark-highlights-performance-edge-for-decision-model-on-openrouter) — OpenRouter's own benchmark, 200 synthetic cases: Jev >5× faster than the next fastest model, matched top LLMs on accuracy, second cheapest behind Qwen3.8 Flash.
- [OpenRouter docs: "Jev vs LLM: When to Use a Decision Model Instead of Generating Text" (2026-09-19)](https://github.com/openrouterteam/docs/blob/HEAD/content/blog/2026-09-19-jev-vs-llm-when-to-use-each.md) — OpenRouter's own measured tutorial (vendor-measured): Task A — 60 support tickets across 5 intents: Jev 59/60 intent, 60/60 escalation flag, p50 194ms, $0.0248 per 1,000 tickets vs GPT-5.6 Luna 59/60 @ $0.0921 and Claude Opus 60/60 @ $2.88. Task B — 40 messages, prompt-injection screen: Jev 40/40 (injections scored 0.86–0.99, ordinary 0.01–0.20). The authors' own caveat: "small sets, 60 and 40 examples on a single day, one set of prompts — the shape of the tradeoff, rather than a leaderboard." Full text verified.
- [Matthew O'Riordan's Pong latency showdown (via RuntimeWire)](https://runtimewire.com/article/diogo-almeida-typesafe-jev-40m-seed-pong) — Four lanes, same game state, recorded 2026-09-17 via Vercel AI Gateway: Jev averaged **227ms/decision (p95 400ms)**; Gemini 3.8 Flash 3.2s, Claude Haiku 4.5 2.5s, GPT-5.6 Sol 3.5s. In the first 12s Jev returned 47 decisions vs 3/2/2 for the chat models. Caveat: a latency demo, not a strategy test — the chat models picked the correct move 95–100% of the time, and every model got structured state (no vision).
- [FazalAAli/jev-robotics-demo](https://github.com/FazalAAli/jev-robotics-demo) — Jev vs Claude Opus 5 driving a simulated Franka arm + Allegro hand in MuJoCo (stack a blue cube on a red one): one recorded run each — Jev 19.1s / $0.0006 vs Opus 158.8s / $0.75 (clean first-try Opus runs took ~55s / $0.19). Design: code owns the physics, Jev owns the judgment.
- [anessbelbati/jev-rerank-bench](https://github.com/anessbelbati/jev-rerank-bench) — Can a decision model beat dedicated rerankers? Jev vs Cohere Rerank 4 vs ZeroEntropy zerank-2 vs a chat-model baseline: 14 datasets, every raw API response saved, bootstrap ranges on every gap (MIT). Ranking average: Jev 0.692 vs Cohere Pro 0.691 (no winner declared); equal query weighting puts Cohere ahead; Jev better on the NevIR negation test. Measurements began 2026-09-16.
- [simonmesmith/jev-banking77-experiment](https://github.com/simonmesmith/jev-banking77-experiment) — Reproducible BANKING77 classification eval: Jev 92.40% accuracy vs 93.66% for a fine-tuned BERT (−1.26 pt), US$0.44 total test cost (created 2026-09-18).
- [JevBench (benchmarkheaven.com)](https://benchmarkheaven.com/jev-models) — Reproducible benchmark for typed decision models ([HN](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49800574)).
- [gemanor/jev-code-review-benchmark](https://github.com/gemanor/jev-code-review-benchmark/blob/HEAD/README.md) — Jev (jev-1.13.0) vs Gemini Flash vs Claude Fable (medium reasoning) on rule-based Python code review, run 2026-09-17 (1,080 calls): per 1,000 reviews Jev $0.043 vs Flash $1.94 vs Fable $11.78; median 0.75s vs 3.59s vs 4.31s; correctness 98.0% vs 100% for both. Caveat: small constructed examples with explicit rules — not production PR review.
- [@DanRWilloughby "Sniff Test" (via doco.page)](https://doco.page/s/CtgDAZsecX1tr3y8XMA7pNnpDedJCPzs) — Jev-as-judge AI-flavor linter raced against mainstream models on writing checks: Jev 78.8% accuracy / 182ms median / $0.013 per 1K checks vs Claude Haiku 4.5 82.5% / 1,971ms / $0.43, Sonnet 5 90% / 6,083ms / $1.34, Opus 5 96.3% / 6,532ms / $3.08, GPT-5.6-sol 91.3% / 4,428ms / $1.64. 10.8× faster and 33× cheaper than the closest rival, 3.7pp less accurate — third-party test reported via the article, protocol not published.
- [Norwegian hearing-documents test (via doco.page)](https://doco.page/s/CtgDAZsecX1tr3y8XMA7pNnpDedJCPzs) — Independent developer test on 24 Norwegian hearing documents + 11 questions: Jev $0.22 per 1K documents vs DeepSeek V4.1 Flash $1.31; median 0.32s vs 2.7s; calibration direction right (0.9–1.0 confidence band: 98% agreement with the reference model, 0.1–0.3 band: 4%). Counterintuitive finding: stricter question wording made calibration worse (ECE rose 0.040 → 0.116).
- [TrueStandard independent test (reported via Karmactive, 2026-09-22)](https://www.karmactive.com/typesafe-jev-ai-decision-model-explainer-193x-speed/) — Third-party numbers with tempered framing: a single decision ~1.7× faster than a comparable LLM call; a multi-step workflow ~100× in one test configuration — the 193× figure is TypeSafe's own best case, not universal. Calibration held (accuracy rises in higher-confidence buckets); on a 77-class intent task Jev scored ~73% vs OpenAI's ~85%. Test protocol not published.
- [scienthoon/jev-ood-calibration](https://github.com/scienthoon/jev-ood-calibration) — Independent calibration audit on an unseen rule-generated task (900 items) plus three public benchmarks (3,721 items): Jev is well-calibrated in-domain (ECE near the noise floor), but confidently wrong where the rule is unknowable OOD (avg 0.74 probability on the chosen level, refit temperature 3.4) — same overconfidence magnitude as a fine-tuned classifier OOD. Miscalibration sign flips by question type (Choice/Score overconfident, Noul underconfident on the same inputs): calibrate per question, not per model. Every raw response published, ~$0.06 of API calls (6★, created 2026-09-19).
- [RINNECODER/jev-behavior-study](https://github.com/RINNECODER/jev-behavior-study) — Independent Jev 1.13.0 behavior study with controlled prompt experiments, raw results, and interactive web demos (3★, created 2026-09-16): central finding is framing sensitivity — correct prerequisite answers don't always produce correct decisions, and answer order moved arithmetic accuracy 95/108 (answer first) to 62/108 (answer last). Snake-game demos included; author stresses results don't establish independent Jev planning.
- [jmanhype/jev-dspy-lab](https://github.com/jmanhype/jev-dspy-lab) — Reproducible calibration and selective-risk benchmarks for Jev/TypeSafe decisions inside DSPy workflows (3★, created 2026-09-17).
- [Daniele Teti: "Jev, the AI that decides without writing: I put it to the test against GPT" (2026-09-22)](https://www.danieleteti.it/post/jev-typesafe-delphi-benchmark-en/) — 105 business emails × up to 25 questions per email (grouped 3/10/25), six models via OpenRouter, balanced accuracy with 95% bootstrap CIs. Jev: 94.3 / 97.2 / 94.8 at 3/10/25 decisions; no LLM beats it at 3 or 10, only Gemini 3.1 Pro does at 25 (+2.3pp). Against the cheapest LLM as accurate as Jev: **4.1–8.5× faster, 2.8–14.0× cheaper**; against Gemini 3.1 Pro ~21× faster at 25 decisions — "a long way from 193". Confidence: right 96.5% of the time above 0.95, but 77.6% when claiming ~0.90 (optimistic in the middle — calibrate thresholds on your own data). Full Delphi code, dataset, CSVs and methodology published. Caveats: one run, hard emails deliberately overrepresented, and the expected answers were written/reviewed by Claude Opus 5 without every label hand-checked (hence no Anthropic models in the comparison).
- [Zyte: "Jev, the model that cannot write a word, and where it fits in web scraping" (2026-09-21)](https://www.zyte.com/blog/jev-the-model-that-cannot-write-a-word-and-where-it-fits-in-web-scraping-does-it/) — Hands-on: Jev as a data-quality *gate*, six typed questions in one API call over a scraped book record. Jev 0.92s / $0.000033 vs GPT-5.6 Luna 3.8–8.9s / $0.000232–0.000262 (~7× cost gap, much tighter latency spread). Planted-description-swap test: `category_right` fell 0.96 → 0.04; across 59 correct records and the same 59 with descriptions shifted by one, it caught 46 crossings at a 0.5 threshold (zero false alarms), 52 at 0.7 (one false alarm). Caveats: Jev is **not deterministic** — confident answers hold across repeats but borderline ones drift by several points; and without an explicit "other" option it labeled a category listing page poetry at confidence 1.00 (type guaranteed, meaning not). Author's verdict: ship it for the narrow gate job, don't replace a null check with a model call.
- [omarmujahid/jev-decision-bench](https://github.com/omarmujahid/jev-decision-bench) — Independent benchmark: Jev `jev-1.13.0` (run 2026-09-18) vs gpt-5.6-luna baselines on 49 decision tasks. Jev matched or beat the LLM on 42/49: median 105ms vs 710–808ms per item, $0.04 vs $0.16–0.19 per 1K items, calibration ECE 0.07 vs 0.14–0.18. Leads on reasoning, knowledge and reranking; loses on counting and a 77-option classification task. Repo ships its own caveats; author unaffiliated with TypeSafe.
- [yibie/laya-jev-lab](https://github.com/yibie/laya-jev-lab) — Independent Jev (TypeSafe API) vs Laya (Convai, local MLX) measurements on an Apple M4 Max with raw results in `results/`: on 40 Chinese support-ticket classifications Jev 31/40 = 78% (mean confidence 0.88, 588ms) vs Laya 23/40 = 57% (0.71, 7.6ms); plus a local-first cascade that matches Jev's accuracy at ~1.8× the speed. Jev perfect on clear inputs (confidence often exactly 1.00); both struggle on genuinely ambiguous boundary cases, which the author flags as a task-design problem, not a model one.
- [flyersworder/agent-contracts](https://github.com/flyersworder/agent-contracts/commit/744d67b9ded2ce16ca040a0f5b6946cbd90cc9b7) — System-One probe of `typesafe/jev-1.13` (~750 calls, $0.074) for an AAMAS resubmission: ranking the menu by informativeness gives rho −0.443 ± 0.029, but asking which variables are connected gives AUC 0.879 ± 0.012 / AP 0.718 in the source→sink stratum — "it knows the physics and not the judge". Seven contrasts, both caps, zero cap flips; three retractions and two method corrections recorded in the results doc.
- [denser-org/rerank-bench-jev](https://github.com/denser-org/rerank-bench-jev) — Production reranker shootout: Jev (Noul) vs Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B on BEIR SciFact + NFCorpus, all 100 candidates scored in one request, every raw API response shipped for recomputation. Quality is indistinguishable (SciFact nDCG@10 Jev 0.7699 vs Qwen 0.7481, +0.022, p=0.011; NFCorpus 0.3623 vs 0.3569, tie) — but Jev is ~2× faster (p50 0.64–0.65s vs 1.11–1.22s; p95 0.76–0.78s vs 1.69–2.10s) while Qwen is ~4× cheaper per query ($0.00045 vs $0.00178 at depth 100). Repo verdict: decide on latency or cost, not nDCG. From the denser.ai team.
- [TokenTrim/jev-agent-failure-benchmark](https://github.com/TokenTrim/jev-agent-failure-benchmark) — Jev on the text subset of Who&When Pro (6,257 failed multi-agent traces), scored with the official `whowhen_eval` scorer: responsible agent 73.4 vs 55.7 (GPT-5.4), decisive step 76.4 vs 72.3, error-type F1 23.7 vs 15.3 (next best GLM-5 22.2), joint 31.3 vs 21.3 — for ~$1.28 total (Jev bills input only). Caveats: LLM baselines are the arXiv:2607.09996 paper numbers, not a same-run retest; Who/When are constrained-choice for Jev but free-generate for LLMs, so the like-for-like axis is error-type.
- [4esv/jev-eval](https://github.com/4esv/jev-eval) — Jev `jev-1.13.0` vs GPT-5.6 Terra (via OpenRouter) vs three open Jev-shaped models (Laya 421M, open-jev 435M, Kev-0.8B) on 5 tasks × 300 items. Jev wins clinc out-of-sample 0.897 and ties Terra on polarity-as-noul 0.970; open-jev takes Banking77 intent 0.873 (Jev 0.780) and leads calibration on intent (ECE 0.047) and SST-5 (0.052 vs Jev 0.200); Terra leads polarity ECE 0.020. Latency: Jev p50 0.17–0.20s vs Terra 1.04–1.06s (~5× faster). Bench ships per-task latency and cost.
- [erendikmenn/jev-rag-benchmark](https://github.com/erendikmenn/jev-rag-benchmark) — Reproducible RAG rerank benchmark (Turkish XQuAD, 1,044 unique questions, 240 passages, benchmark status 2026-09-20): hybrid BM25 + bge-m3 fusion gives 99.521% candidate recall at top-20 vs 96.648% for BM25 alone (+2.87pp), the prerequisite ceiling before reranking is scored; Jev was requested as `typesafe/jev-1.13` (OpenRouter resolved `jev-1.13-20260917`) and the stated premise is "Jev wins is not an assumption" — quality, latency and cost can each stay flat or get worse (14★, MIT, created 2026-09-19).
- [harrymunro/jev-laya-benchmark](https://github.com/harrymunro/jev-laya-benchmark) — Jev 1.13 (API) vs Laya 421M (local MLX on Apple M3 Pro): 1,470 synthetic items across eight typed-decision tasks. Judgments correct: Jev 92.9% vs Laya 65.3%; choice/noul/score 99.3/98.6/76.1 vs 68.3/71.3/51.5. Single question p50: 136ms vs 42ms; 50 questions in one request: 170ms vs 1,002ms; independent-item throughput 72 items/s (32 in flight) vs 24 items/s sequential. Full calibration analysis and report in REPORT.md (3★, MIT, created 2026-09-21).
- [alperenerol/jev-1.13-mini-benchmark](https://github.com/alperenerol/jev-1.13-mini-benchmark) — Mini benchmark of `typesafe/jev-1.13` (OpenRouter Decisions API) on labeled support-triage cases (labels are author-assigned; case content and raw responses committed). Dataset v1.2 (pushed 2026-09-29, supersedes the earlier 34-case numbers): 100 cases (96 English / 4 Turkish, 68 agentic-routing cases rt_33–100) — choice 14/14 exact, score perfect within tolerance, noul urgency 0.917 on clear-cut cases, latency p50 0.41s, ~$0.0011 per 68 requests. Turkish cases: no degradation. (1★, MIT, created 2026-09-19; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [simonmesmith/jev-bbq-experiment](https://github.com/simonmesmith/jev-bbq-experiment) — Reproducible eval of Jev 1.13.0 on all 58,492 public BBQ bias-benchmark questions: 56,900 correct (97.28%) — 99.96% when the passage didn't give enough information, 94.60% when it did. Bias scores 0.04 (ambiguous) / 0.34 (informative). Whole run cost US$0.3429 in 7.75 minutes. Caveat from the author: a benchmark this shape can't certify a model as generally unbiased.
- [Aitejiu: "Benchmarking Jev: what a decision model can (and can't) do in an agent harness"](https://dev.to/aitejiu/benchmarking-jev-what-a-decision-model-can-and-cant-do-in-an-agent-harness-20po) ([code](https://github.com/Aitejiu/jev-harness-lab)) — Black-box engineering evaluation: 10 public datasets, ~22,500 API calls, 52.2M input tokens, **$2.19 total** — Jev as the "fast-think layer" for an agent harness at ~$0.00004 and ~0.3s per call (1★, created 2026-09-19; dev.to post 2026-09-21).
- [myc0576/smartmoney-cub](https://github.com/myc0576/smartmoney-cub) — Trading journal + review harness with Jev as optional typed-judgment layer, shipping `finance-jev-v1` (240 frozen cases across four tracks: trading-review, financial-filings, industry-events, macro-policy). Reference run: deterministic rule baseline 83.33% vs live TypeSafe Jev (`typesafe_direct`, `jev-1.13.0`) 78.43% overall, macro F1 0.7319, Jev better calibrated (ECE 0.1464 vs 0.1667), p50 1,034ms. Author caveat: frozen toy suite — figures do not imply generalization to production market regimes (27★, MIT, pushed 2026-09-25).
- [scarif-labs/jev-software-decision-benchmark](https://github.com/scarif-labs/jev-software-decision-benchmark) — Independent benchmark of JEV as a software decision primitive for dependency-update automation under distribution shift. On 1,102 in-distribution cases (551 breaking / 551 control): JEV AUROC **0.851** vs static rules 0.602 vs DeepSeek Flash 0.585. On a 185-case out-of-distribution set (103 independent repos): JEV retains the highest AUROC (**0.605**, 95% CI 0.525–0.685) but the frozen in-distribution threshold **0.62 did not transfer safely** — 30 auto-merges at 50.0% precision, 15 unsafe. Author caveat: this does not establish general superiority or inferiority of JEV as a decision primitive (2★, created 2026-09-18).
- [nadeem4/jev-demo: Decision Arena](https://github.com/nadeem4/jev-demo) — Zero-training game benchmark pitting Jev (via Vercel AI Gateway) against open-source Laya (multilingual checkpoint, CPU), 10 episodes/agent on 2026-09-22, replays and scorecard at [arena.codewithnk.com](https://arena.codewithnk.com): highway-env crash rate Jev 0% (95% CI 0–28%) vs Laya 90% (60–98%); Blackjack basic-strategy match Jev 77% (73–81%) vs Laya 50% (42–57%); Snake food eaten Jev 1.8 with 0 deaths vs Laya 0.5 with 90% deaths — but a hand-coded greedy baseline ate 17.3: "neither model plans." Author caveats: n=10, treat under-50-episode results as a first look; Jev was safe but passive (kept lane on 94% of decisions, circled in Snake), Laya often didn't adapt (chose "stick" on all 200 Blackjack decisions). Via [@codewithnk](https://www.instagram.com/p/DdrDQWzE0xm/) IG carousel (2026-09-24).
- [nadeem4/ai-experiments: RAG re-ranker](https://github.com/nadeem4/ai-experiments) — Measured RAG rerank on all 323 BEIR NFCorpus test queries (top-20 candidates each, official qrels, 6,460 scoring calls per re-ranker, one typed `score` question per passage): Jev nDCG@10 0.3414, +0.0347 vs the BM25 floor 0.3067 (paired 95% CI [+0.0218, +0.0476]); MS MARCO cross-encoder +0.020; both Laya checkpoints lose to BM25 (−0.0219, −0.0360). Zero-shot by design — Laya's own model card says its base checkpoints are near chance zero-shot; every interval clears zero at n=323. Code, results and method notes published (created 2026-09-24).
- [Jev Does Not Play Dice: 83% probability, 19% accuracy on a hidden fair die roll](https://kantahayashiai.github.io/posts/jev-does-not-play-dice/) — Independent calibration check where the true probability is known exactly: a fair die, 400 trials — Jev picked "1" every time at 82.9% mean reported probability, 19.0% actual accuracy (chance); same pattern on a colored die, four-way spinner and coin (200 trials each). Forecast-document test: a stated 30% shortage risk came back as 5.3% via `Choice` (stated 45% → 6.6%, stated 55% → 95.9% — an abrupt threshold behavior around 50%). Lesson: "a fast and cheap decision maker" and "a number you can trust unconditionally" are two different things. Code + raw responses on GitHub; originally written in Japanese on note.com.
- [softpudding/jev-frontier-100](https://github.com/softpudding/jev-frontier-100) — 100 original tasks comparing Jev 1.13.0 with Qwen3.5 0.8B/2B/4B across three reasoning budgets (100 questions × 3 trials per condition, reproducible, MIT). Jev: **77.0%**; Qwen3.5 4B with a 2,048-token thinking budget **96.7%** (+19.7pp, 95% bootstrap CI +12.7–26.7), 2B/2,048 82.0% (+5.0, CI −2.3–12.3), 0.8B off 38.0%. Budget matters: 4B scores 56.0% with thinking disabled. Similar totals don't imply similar skills: Jev scores well on short code semantics and formal logic, 2B/2,048 stronger on relationship tracking and algorithm tasks. Caveats: synthetic, English, four-choice task set; near-ceiling 4B result exposes the small dataset's limits (1★, created 2026-09-19).
- [baibizhe/jev-decision-benchmarks](https://github.com/baibizhe/jev-decision-benchmarks) — JEV decision benchmarks on MetaTool, When2Call and BFCL V4, with bilingual tables and reproducible reports. MetaTool (JEV 1.13 via Choice adapter): similar-tool 0-shot **77.79%** vs ChatGPT 69.05%, abstain 0-shot **87.04%** vs 50.35%; at "exactly two" JEV 88.33% vs Vicuna-33b 91.15%. Caveats: paper baselines generate tool names + answer extraction, not Choice; public similar-tool/abstention sets contain 995 examples each vs 975 in the paper. No license declared (1★, created 2026-09-19).
- [Entagl: "TypeSafe Jev: 5x Faster, 25x Cheaper, 98.5% Correct"](https://www.entagl.com/blog/typesafe-jev-benchmark-ai-decision-models) — Two-round independent evaluation (Sept 2026): 1,357 hand-labeled decisions across 13 tasks in 13 languages, then 402 real routing decisions replayed from two client workspaces. Jev 98.5% vs a frontier Gemini model 99.0%; median 329ms vs 1,598ms; ~25× cheaper. Calibration held: 986/986 correct at ≥97% confidence, 73.2% below 60%. On real traffic Jev beat the incumbents at "what to do with the message" (94.0%/93.0% vs 91.2%/87.4%) but lost at "which specialist answers" (91.4%/83.7% vs 98.1%/97.8%) — misses came from context it was never given. One 0.95-confidence miss survived. Method published in full.
- [Jev Decision Index](https://huggingface.co/spaces/multimodalart/jev-decision-index) — Community leaderboard aggregating open Jev implementations' benchmark results (public HF Space). Author's posted chart (2026-09-24, via [@jstnimo](https://www.threads.com/@jstnimo/post/DdqVF_cgKDf)): Jev reference 59.5 leads, JefFine Qwen3-8-27B 55.7, diffgemma 2B 55.6, Decider 358-43B 54.3, Verdict 151M 13.4. Methodology not published — treat as directional.
- [wondertwins/jev-benchmark](https://github.com/wondertwins/jev-benchmark) — Benchmarks + interactive playground for chess and NPC addressee detection (6★, MIT, created 2026-09-16; model served as `jev-1.13.0` on 2026-09-16). Chess: no better than random from a raw board; ~65% over random with code-supplied facts, ~78% with one-ply tactical facts; mate-in-one 24% (6/25, random baseline 3%) — performance rating ~950 on a Stockfish-anchored ladder (11 games; author caveats the extrapolation). NPC addressee detection: F1 0.96 clean / 0.93 noisy transcripts, precision 1.0, exact-set accuracy 92% vs 64% for a fuzzy name-matching heuristic. Latency medians 0.2s / 0.17s per call. Lesson in one line: "Jev is a judgment engine, not a calculator." All code, labeled data and every raw request/response in `runs/raw/`.
- [AY Automate: "Jev vs GPT and Claude: Independent Benchmark (2026)"](https://www.ayautomate.com/blog/jev-vs-llm-benchmark) — 791 labeled decisions (Banking77 8-way/77-way intent + deepset prompt-injection), run 2026-09-19 on one client and one OpenRouter billing meter, 3,955 calls for $1.53. Jev (`typesafe/jev-1.13-20260917` via the decisions endpoint) was 2.0–3.6× faster at median and 4.7–7.5× cheaper than the two cheapest small models (GPT-5.4 nano, Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite); accuracy was level with those small models but behind GPT-5.6 Terra on 77-way intent (Terra +5pp, paired McNemar says the gap is real). Actionable finding: a Jev-first cascade (0.80 confidence gate, rest to Terra) matched Terra-alone accuracy at 26–28% of Terra's cost and ~half its mean latency. Prompt-injection detection: Jev AUROC 0.990, best of the five. Full repro harness plus every call in `results.jsonl`. Caveats: one run on public data, options passed as labels only, and the LLMs ran minimal reasoning + a strict JSON schema (favors their speed/cost).
- [Back Not Prop: "Jev Jailbreak Benchmark" (2026-09-20)](https://backnotprop.com/blog/jev-guardrails/) — Jev (`jev-1.13.0`, one `Noul` question per item, wording fixed before the first run) vs four open prompt-injection classifiers (PIGuard, Prompt Guard 2, ProtectAI v2, deepset) on 7,803 labeled messages + 296 conversations with shared scoring code. Curated set (1,688 items): Jev AUC 0.937 — best of the five. Real attacks (6,115 items): Jev AUC 0.844, behind ProtectAI v2 (0.882) and PIGuard (0.866) — Jev overflags wild benign items (median score 0.06–0.24 on WildGuard/in-the-wild benign vs 0.02–0.05 on cleaner sets). Multi-turn jailbreaks: 100% caught at a 0.18 threshold with 0.7% benign flagged. Speed/cost: median 211ms per call, $0.17 for all 6,115 items. Honest limits section: the classifiers were trained on injection data, Jev was not; Jev's question wording was never varied.
- [iApp OpenThai-SystemOne (v0.3, 2026-09-22)](https://iapp.co.th/docs/llm/openthai-systemone) ([code](https://github.com/iapp-technology/openthai-systemone), [weights](https://huggingface.co/iapp/OpenThai-SystemOne)) — Apache-2.0 Thai/English 0.8B System-One decision model from iApp/OpenThai: HTTP contract mirrors Jev's `/v1/systemone` (Jev SDK code points at it unchanged), free hosted preview (1,000 decisions/day/key). Zero-shot on Bespoke Labs' public 13-subset System One benchmark (vendor's own measurement, run with Bespoke's released eval script): macro 74.3 vs Bespoke Nimble-9B 74.8 vs Jev 1.13.0 76.0 — ahead of the 9B on 8/13 subsets (PAWS 94.0 vs Jev 89.2, MASSIVE-en-US 88.3 vs 87.4), behind on summary relevance (21.7) and PubMedQA (64.0); median calibration ECE 0.07. Docs list the weak spots up front (5-level rating tasks, banking77 77-way intent 45.4, Thai social sentiment 51.6). Real-time Doom demo: [iapp-technology/openthai-systemone-doom](https://github.com/iapp-technology/openthai-systemone-doom).
- [instax-dutta/sysone-bench](https://github.com/instax-dutta/sysone-bench) — First independent head-to-head of System One decision models on byte-identical inputs (same states, same question dicts, same seed; question SHA hashes verified identical before comparing): v2 run 2026-09-21, 751 states, 9 suites. Jev (`jev-1.13.0` pinned, full run cost $0.008) wins 6/9 — triage 0.888, guardrails 0.967, moderation 0.989, banking77 0.906, sst5 0.617, multilingual intent 1.000; Laya (local M2 CPU) wins agnews 0.940 and mnli 0.983; emotion is a weak tie (0.54–0.55, ECE ~0.3). Secondary baseline: stock Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct with parallel constrained decoding. Latency per 5-question call: Laya 180–660ms local vs Jev 925–1068ms over the API. Raw and temperature-fitted ECE reported separately, results append-only, plus a `FEEDBACK_REPORT.md` benchmark-cum-feedback report for TypeSafe (4★, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API).
- [dhruvmehra/jevbench](https://github.com/dhruvmehra/jevbench) — Small reproducible benchmark: six classifiers — Jev 1.13, Laya, cheap LLM (gpt-5-mini), frontier LLM (Claude Sonnet 5), fine-tuned DistilBERT (per-dataset, up to 10K train examples), zero-shot BART-NLI — over three public datasets (sst2, agnews, banking77; n=500 seeded each), reporting accuracy, calibration (ECE), latency, throughput and cost side by side. 31 offline tests, no-network repro. Per the Fractional research piece citing this run, fine-tuned DistilBERT beat Jev on topic and intent tasks, most sharply on Banking77 (88.0% vs 76.4%) — the "train once you have labels" argument in concrete numbers. Jev ran via OpenRouter Decisions API; all datasets and local models public, no HF token needed (6★, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API; full results table in `docs/results/2026-09-22-n500-summary.md`).
- [@dmckinno: Jev vs GPT-nano document-classification benchmark (Threads, 2026-09-25)](https://www.threads.com/@dmckinno/post/DdsWtjZDe37) — Same 200 PDFs through classification and extraction+classification: identical results (TP=6, FP=20, TN=174, FN=0 → precision 23.1%, recall 100%). Jev ~5× faster (16.7s vs 84.1s classification; 20.6s vs 89.6s end-to-end) but slightly more expensive ($0.0344 vs $0.0272 per 200 PDFs). Author built an OPT-175B first-line classifier at Meta in 2022 and argues a fine-tuned small model is the likelier buy; the reply thread doubles as peer review — Jev fits a narrow window of structured generation + latency sensitivity + scarce training data, a plain classifier may beat it otherwise. Caveats: author's own task, single run, figures read from posted terminal screenshots, not independently verified.
- [desquaredp/jev-loop-benchmark](https://github.com/desquaredp/jev-loop-benchmark) — Reproducible JEV-led coding-agent repair loop: cheap-only vs JEV-led (cheap implementation + bounded strong-model diagnosis) vs strong-only on five SWE-bench tasks with official grading, plus two scikit-learn regression probes. JEV-led and strong-only both resolved 5/5; JEV-led cost **$2.001 vs $6.385** for strong-only (68.7% reduction), cheap-only resolved 3/5. Author's own note: "an illustrative five-case experiment, not a statistically significant benchmark"; sanitized receipts committed under `results/shareable-01`. (0★, MIT, created 2026-09-22)
- [dchristopoulos/jev-aita](https://github.com/dchristopoulos/jev-aita) — Jev vs Sonnet 5, GPT-5 nano and two local LLMs on 770 r/AmItheAsshole posts: every model assigns a probability to each of four verdicts (YTA/NTA/ESH/NAH), scored by Brier score against the subreddit's verdict. Jev came second (Brier 0.369 vs Sonnet 0.344); Jev's median call 6.3× faster than Sonnet's and 62× cheaper — author notes that's fast, but not TypeSafe's claimed "40×–200×". No affiliation with TypeSafe; ~$2.75 of the author's own API spend. 52 commits, full method + "What this does not show" section. (3★, no license declared, created 2026-09-22)
- [jujumilk3/jev-calibration-audit](https://github.com/jujumilk3/jev-calibration-audit) — Independent API-only calibration audit (run 2026-09-18 from Seoul against `jev-1.13.0`, every number backed by `results/raw/*.jsonl`): on KoBBQ's 300 ambiguous items Jev chose "unknown" 95% of the time when that option existed — but with the gold answer removed from the options, accuracy on those items was necessarily 0% and 79% of answers picked the dataset's stereotype. Read every ECE against the noise floor printed beside it — a perfectly calibrated model would still show a nonzero value at these sample sizes. (1★, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API)
- [FirasSX914/Janus](https://github.com/FirasSX914/Janus) — "Measure when to use Jev and other models on your data, then route accordingly": [RESEARCH.md](https://github.com/FirasSX914/Janus/blob/main/RESEARCH.md) runs 500 Banking77 items + 500 Web of Science abstracts. Jev is right 95.8% of the time it says 100% confident on Banking77 but only 76.7% on Web of Science; a tuned Jev-to-DeepSeek cascade reaches 80.2% at $0.1033/500 decisions vs 78.8% at $0.2207 for DeepSeek V4-Pro alone — yet on Web of Science the same routing ties Jev alone (52.8% either way) for 47% more money. Author's conclusion: no routing parameter measured on the first dataset held on the second. (3★, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API)
- [nikkoxgonzales/jev-certify](https://github.com/nikkoxgonzales/jev-certify) — Finite-sample guarantees for Jev via conformal risk control: [results/REPORT.md](https://github.com/nikkoxgonzales/jev-certify/blob/main/results/REPORT.md) shows 460 calibration queries and a 5% target on silently misrouted queries picking confidence threshold 0.831, certifying expected per-query loss 0.0499. On 400 held-out human-labeled queries the threshold auto-routed 84.75% of traffic with 2.65% wrong — certificate held. Separate scope gate missed its 5% target 3.6× when out-of-scope prevalence rose: the bound does not cover a shifted population. (1★, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API)

Scripts, raw data and the hook in the author's jev-testlabor repo on Codeberg — whole measurement reproducible, total API spend just under $0.80.

Cost: IDR 11 (~$0.00066) per check; latency 656ms vs 128ms. Every number produced by the repo's code, raw API responses in `data/`, and 14 of the 24 test cases were written after the decision policy was frozen. Per-case score variance: Jev 0.0000149 (Luna 433×, Terra 913×, Claude 92× higher). Jev averaged 0.44s and $0.00035/call ($0.34 total vs $28.17 for Claude). Project repo published. Caveats from the authors: one public dataset per question type, LLM probabilities are verbalized rather than read from token probs, latency measured from one residential connection, and the Gateway doesn't report which Jev version answered. The insight nobody else wrote down: **choice questions come back bimodal** — Jev is either certain or visibly lost, so gating on a 95% confidence threshold escalates only 4/40 and leaves 36 at 100% correct (9.7× cheaper than all-Fable); but the same gate collapses on soft questions (38/40 escalated on urgency) — "gate on the sharp questions, and on the soft ones take the probability as the answer." Full measured replay (latency + price, recorded not live) at [wiz.jock.pl/experiments/reflex](https://wiz.jock.pl/experiments/reflex); receipt published: 49 Jev calls $0.00095 vs 51 Gemini/Haiku/Fable calls $0.161. Yes/no: Jev 94%, AUROC 0.970, ties. Companion Medium write-up ("I Tested a 478x Cheaper Decision Model…"). (1★, Python, MIT, created 2026-09-19; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [0xmdinc/jev-medical-bench](https://github.com/0xmdinc/jev-medical-bench) — Medical decision benchmark: TypeSafe `Jev 1.13` vs Qwen3.8-27B (self-hosted vLLM on one RTX 4090) vs DeepSeek V4 Pro, on 50 basic + 52 hard synthetic clinical cases (triage ESI scoring, ED referral, specialty choice, rx safety, diagnosis, management — each mapped to Jev's three question types). Measured 2026-09-27, hard set: DeepSeek V4 Pro + reasoning 98% (8.28s p50, $0.645/1K), Qwen3.8-27B 92% (6.83s, $0 own GPU), Jev 1.13 90% (**0.38s p50, $0.018/1K**), DeepSeek V4 Pro 90%, Qwen + thinking 90%. By task, Jev's weak spot is triage (62% vs 88–100% elsewhere); as a confidence gate (≥0.6), Jev keeps 92% of cases at 96% accuracy, escalating 4 to a reasoning model. Honest caveats: all cases synthetic, gold answers not reviewed by an independent clinician; results charts + runner in repo. - [vintasoftware/medplum-provider-jev](https://github.com/vintasoftware/medplum-provider-jev) — Medplum Provider demo detecting conflicts between clinical notes and discharge summaries with Jev (`agreement` / `potential_conflict` / `insufficient_information` review cards); re-measured on a 100-case benchmark 2026-10-01: hosted Jev 98/100, Jebadiah 27B 92/100, AutoJev-27B 84/100, Jebadiah 9B v2 82/100 (every Jebadiah-27B miss an acknowledged dose change); `measure-cases.json` committed, synthetic data only — not clinical validation. (4★, TypeScript, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Eileenes/DecideLint](https://github.com/Eileenes/DecideLint) — The missing CI layer for System-1 decision models (Jev, Kev, Laya, SemIf, Von, ...): declare a decision contract (state + typed questions + candidate criteria + threshold policy, versioned like source code), run it against a built-in simulated model (offline, zero dependencies) or any Jev-compatible HTTP endpoint, and score answers with accuracy, Brier, log loss and the reliability diagram behind ECE. Also grid-searches the single temperature that minimises NLL and reports ECE before/after. English + 中文 docs, backend/frontend + Docker Compose. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified)
- [truongxxxx/jev-polymarket-test](https://github.com/truongxxxx/jev-polymarket-test) — Can TypeSafe Jev (`jev-1.13.0`) beat the Polymarket price? On 26,174 resolved Yes/No markets (≥$10k volume): on the 9,138 markets resolved after Jev's release the market's own price wins decisively — AUC 0.844 vs Jev-blind 0.615 (Brier 0.152 vs 0.220, accuracy 77.0% vs 65.8%); telling Jev the market price lifts it to AUC 0.794. Median latency 284ms, the whole blind run ≈$0.79. Memorization check across six resolution windows plus a companion write-up ([moonthetrain.com](https://moonthetrain.com/lab/can-jev-beat-polymarket)); frozen report (`jev/report.md`, regenerated by `evaluate.py`), charts and data-fetch scripts committed. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + jev/report.md verified; numbers are the author's own measurements)
- [WuXixiong/jev-poisoning-bench](https://github.com/WuXixiong/jev-poisoning-bench) — Experimental framework for measuring how poisoned contextual evidence affects Jev-style fixed-choice decisions and their downstream execution: retrieval + typed decisions + local filesystem sandboxes, wrong-decision / wrong-execution / policy-violation / unavailable / correct-execution coverage measured separately, attack search, frozen contamination sets (SHA256 manifests) and six controlled studies (CS1–CS6) over synthetic document-release and incident-response workflows. English + 中文 docs, offline CI, reproduction inputs committed; the author flags it as experimental with no manuscript. (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified)
- [kartikanand73/decision-api-benchmark](https://github.com/kartikanand73/decision-api-benchmark) — Narrow, reproducible head-to-head of the three decision APIs on the same bank-withdrawal routing question, all through OpenRouter with byte-identical requests apart from the model id (500 cases × 6 option orders plus repeats, 9,165 calls per run, two full runs on 2026-10-10, every call committed as JSONL): route macro-F1 **89.2%** (86.1–92.1, 95% case-resampling bootstrap) for TypeSafe Jev (`typesafe/jev-1.13-20260917`) vs **87.6%** for Microsoft Decision-1 vs 71.4% for OpenAI's Luna Decisions; route accuracy 92.8% / 91.0% / 77.4%. Latency p50: 233ms Jev / 214ms OpenAI / 303ms Microsoft; cost per 1,000 decisions: $0.0324 / $0.0611 / $0.0178. Same route in all 6 option orders: Microsoft 97.2–97.6% (its "zero flips" vendor claim holds), Jev 91.6–91.8%, OpenAI 64.2% (option-order sensitivity confirmed); Brier score 0.117 / 0.112–0.115 / 0.296. Grown out of the author's Jev Decision Gateway; run-to-run comparison with drift analysis committed. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified; numbers are the author's own measurements)

- [r4fo/prompt-injection-detector](https://github.com/r4fo/prompt-injection-detector) — Binary prompt-injection classifier on email: E2A prompt-injection emails plus matched benign controls, SpamAssassin ham and NotInject hard negatives, with leakage-safe train/validation/test splits. Benchmarks classical ML, neural nets and Transformers with committed results (`results/final_benchmark.csv`) and error analysis; JEV is in the author's stated comparison pipeline but its numbers are not in the committed results. (0★, Jupyter Notebook, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + results CSV verified)

- [DarioFontanel/jev-decisions-d1-prompts](https://github.com/DarioFontanel/jev-decisions-d1-prompts) — Three prompts for replicating the Jev vs OpenAI Decisions API vs Liquid d1 bake-off: each prompt has a coding agent build a complete application around one model under identical rules and identical time/cost measurements. Italian docs. (0★, MIT, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified; methodology only — no measured results yet)
- [QuizPilot: 90-question Jev benchmark (dev.to/jack9999, 2026-10-06)](https://dev.to/jack9999/how-we-use-jev-to-answer-quiz-questions-a-90-question-benchmark-7ho) — Author-run benchmark on 90 self-written quiz questions (not a real exam): Jev 1.13 (via OpenRouter) answered 85–86/90 (basic set 59/60, hard set 26–27/30), one request covering 60 questions in 0.17–0.44s vs 2.6s for a lightweight LLM (89/90) vs 8.5s for a frontier reasoning LLM (90/90). All nine misses were single-choice questions needing multi-step arithmetic; 7 of 9 wrong answers came back with confidence below 0.6 against 0.93 average for right answers. Adapter open source: [jelly-ham/quizpilot-extension](https://github.com/jelly-ham/quizpilot-extension). (full text verified; numbers are the author's own measurements; the re-check combination is computed from separate runs, not a live run)
- [etsabary/luna-decisions-benchmark](https://github.com/etsabary/luna-decisions-benchmark) — Independent bake-off of OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna Decisions (`openai/gpt-6-luna-decisions-20261006` via OpenRouter, 2026-10-10) against the recorded Jev September 22 provider mix (`jev-latest` → `jev-1.13.0`) on the same 1,000 deterministic five-option logic questions: **Jev 817/1,000 (81.7%) vs Luna 612/1,000 (61.2%)**; Luna answered 983 (17 API refusals, refusal follow-up analysis committed) and hit 62.3% among answered, but its high-confidence slice was strong — 199/201 correct at confidence ≥0.8. Methods, per-question data (2.2MB questions CSV, Jev results CSV, Luna attempt JSONL, checksums) and refusal diagnostics all committed. The author explicitly caveats that this evaluates a particular API snapshot on a generated logic benchmark and \"does not establish a general ranking of either product\". (0★, Python/Jupyter, MIT, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + tree verified; numbers are the author's own measurements)
- [\"If LLMs Can Decide Without Fine-Tuning, Do We Still Need Models Like Jev?\" (itsodeleo.github.io, 2026-10-08)](https://itsodeleo.github.io/posts/local-decision-workflow/) — Independent local bake-off (M2 Max, 300 self-constructed questions, not the models' training tasks) asking whether direct logit-scoring on existing LLMs beats purpose-built decision models: Gemma 4 26B-A4B direct-scoring 75% / 259ms, Qwen 3.5 9B 61% / 217ms vs Jeff 2B v1.1 65% / 295ms, Jeff 0.8B v1.1 60% / 128ms, OpenDecider small 8-bit 77% / 707ms, Laya multilingual 37% / 17ms and Laya Typed Decisions 51% / 65ms — but the same Laya config scores ~77% on the public Typed Decisions set, which the author reads as task-overfitting (with the caveat that tasks, languages and input conditions changed together). TypeSafe's Jev itself was **not** directly tested; the author stresses this is not a controlled comparison of fine-tuning effects. Blog write-up only — no code published. (full text verified 2026-10-11; numbers are the author's own measurements)
- [ashish24142/jev-clm](https://github.com/ashish24142/jev-clm) — Pre-registered probability-native contract-screening test on the CUAD test split (102 contracts, 41 clause categories, 4,182 yes/no decisions): five predictions registered in `PREREG.md` before the first API call — three hit, two missed, all published. Jev-1.13.0 (pinned): AUROC 0.942, ECE 0.045, auto-clears 73% of clauses at 96.0% precision. The gpt-5.4-mini baseline wins on raw accuracy (88.8% vs 86.5%) but returns yes/no only, so it cannot say which answers to trust; the gpt-6-luna arm (3× price, added post-registration and disclosed) matched Jev's accuracy with worse calibration (ECE 0.092). Every raw API response archived under `results/raw/`; companion essay [migrationassurance.com](https://migrationassurance.com/probabilities-not-prose/). (0★, Python, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README + results verified; numbers are the author's own measurements)
- ["A lifejacket for scientists drowning in papers" (sina.bio, 2026-10-08)](https://sina.bio/posts/decision-language-models.html) — Can decision LMs rank preprints by relevance to a scientist's stated interests? The author's own two benchmarks: SinSciRelBench (66 papers — 22 author-coauthored, 22 keyword-matched controls, 22 unmatched controls, as high/medium/low relevance proxies) and the human-judged RELISH dataset. Jev (`jev-1.13.0`, `noul`) vs OpenAI's Decisions API (`gpt-6-luna`): on SinSciRelBench the two largely agreed on ordering (Spearman 0.945); Jev AUC 0.981 vs OpenAI 0.915 separating the author's papers from unmatched controls (0.938 vs 0.893 with keyword-matched controls included). On RELISH both beat the TF-IDF baseline: Jev average AUC 0.893 vs OpenAI 0.872 vs TF-IDF 0.721 for relevant-vs-irrelevant separation. Cost on SinSciRelBench: Jev $0.042/M input tokens vs OpenAI $0.10 — OpenAI ≈1.87× Jev's total. Author's caveats: SinSciRelBench is small and single-person, and RELISH tests paper-to-paper rather than person-to-paper relevance. Companion live tool: [author.link](https://author.link). [HN discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50013328). (full text verified 2026-10-11; numbers are the author's own measurements)

- 📊 [RoboKrunch: 300 real Jev decisions on a simulated 10K-robot fleet](https://github.com/robokrunch/jev-physical-ai) — 60s, watch the cost ticker.
- [Jev by TypeSafe AI: Real-Time Decision Making for Agent Assist](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9gPTHWYwuqU) — A human agent-assist application in production shape: customer–agent voice over WebRTC, Deepgram live transcription, Jev evaluates the transcript against predefined questions, the assist panel updates in real time (~1 day old).
- [Gary Explains: "Jev From TypeSafe is a New Class of AI Model that is FAST and CHEAP — But There is a Caveat!" (YouTube, 2026-09-19)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qdji39XXgEY) — Technical explainer on what Jev is and isn't: a model that understands natural language but answers with structured responses + confidence instead of text (the caveat: it's not an LLM).
- [YouTube: “Jev’s 444x Claim: What TypeSafe’s Own Benchmark Actually Says” (2026-09-24)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SlS21dJW4cI) — Claim audit with chaptered sources: the 444.6× figure is a self-run benchmark scored against the average of two other frontier models rather than ground truth — which TypeSafe states in its own launch post; the one public outside measurement cited, from a Vercel engineer, is 5–18×. Description explicitly disclaims any use/avoid recommendation. Title + description verified.
- ["Jev AI Explained: TypeSafe's New AI Is 99% Cheaper Than GPT" (YouTube, The Invisible Economist, ~2026-09-23)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Eqh6A-2mz7o) — Stickman-animated explainer of what Jev is and how it works (typed questions in, one answer plus a confidence score out; the "bouncer" trick for cutting frontier-model costs), with the numbers the channel quotes: Jev on OpenRouter $0.042/M input, output free (~99.6% cheaper than frontier list price); TypeSafe's own benchmark 67.8% at $0.0004/task vs GPT-5.6 Terra 67.9% at $0.0304 vs Claude Opus 5 73.1% at $0.1761. Credit where due: the description flags these as TypeSafe's own numbers, not independently verified. Title + description verified.
- [9592 Solutions: "Is Jev actually good? We tested TypeSafe's classifier on 3,080 bank support messages" (YouTube, ~2026-09-26)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MwGATggBw-c) — The team behind the M37 app runs Jev against hosted LLMs, two local classifiers and one open-weight model on the Banking77 test set, with the acceptance cutoff (0.99 on Jev's chosen-label probability) fixed on 573 dev messages first: Jev accepted 84.5% of test messages with 2.04% error (95% CI 1.56–2.65%), matching the LLMs at 93.6–94.0% overall. They disclose their commercial interest (the app's "Choose Category" runs on Jev) and note TypeSafe neither sponsored nor reviewed the video. Title + description verified.
- [Hyperautomation Labs: "Jev vs OpenAI Decisions — 505 real decisions head-to-head" (Facebook, 2026-10-08)](https://www.facebook.com/reel/4505677659686846/) — Live head-to-head of 505 real-world decisions across 8 jobs (support tickets, tool picks, prompt attacks, fact checks, star ratings, banking intents, dates, invoice math): Jev 385 correct vs OpenAI Decisions (presented as launched Oct 6, 2026, running on GPT-5 Luna) 378 — near tie. Presenter's findings: Jev ~2.4× cheaper per token on paper ($0.042 vs $0.10/M input) but actual cost gap only ~1.33× due to token usage; only OpenAI handles images directly; their recommended trick is asking both models and auto-acting only on agreement (94% agreement, 93% right). All numbers are the presenter's own measurements. Post link verified.
- [Benchmark Heaven: "Does JevBench's new #1 really beat Jev?" (YouTube, ~2026-10-06)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nyl9IQbk5Zk) — Covers JevBench v1.6.0 (published 2026-10-05, every ranked system re-measured): new #1 Quyet-1.0-Large (Capability 81.7), ahead of deck-31B (77.6) and Jev (76.5) — v1.6.1 (Oct 6) re-ranks with Levanto Labs' Sage 1.3.0 first. Their own independent check: the top three plus plain Gemma-4-31B-it and Cloudflare's Clef-Flash on 3,080 bank support messages (10-option questions, run 2026-10-05 from Berlin on rented Modal GPUs) — Quyet 94.5%, Jev 93.9%, deck-31B 94.0%, Gemma 93.8%, Clef-Flash 95.3%; at a 2%-error cutoff Quyet auto-answered 92 of 100 messages vs Jev's 84 and Gemma's 64. Description is fully sourced with methods, version notes and corrections. Title + description verified.

## Tutorials & Guides

Hands-on, not hot takes. Entries here were checked for real code or real runs.

- [Avi Chawla: "Build your own Jev (100% local)" (X article, 2026-09-20)](https://x.com/_avichawla/status/2101563610644496464) — Recreates Jev's fixed-answer scoring on a local open LLM via SGLang's `/v1/score`: read the option letter's logits, softmax over the labels, zero text generation. Real run on Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct (logits 25.28/24.50/21.19 → 0.68/0.31/0.01), plus a 100-case benchmark app pitting the scoring lane against a generation lane. ~5,000 words, 107K views.
- [Movez: "Jev Engineering: how to build the fastest AI Agent Brain in 10 Steps" (X article, 2026-09-19)](https://x.com/0xMovez/status/2101007482919227841) — Ten-step guide to wiring Jev in as an agent's decision brain: Playground, SDK, a `chief.py` decision router, dynamic menus, parallel questions, guardrails, cost math, five production use cases. Caveat: most headline numbers (flights in 7s for $0.0039, 1,018 papers for $0.08) are quoted community experiments, and "200× faster, 400× cheaper" is the author's claim — not an independent measurement. 302K views.
- ["Jev AI Full COURSE 1 HOUR (Build & Automate Anything)" (YouTube, 2026-09-21)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hz8tobAFBVM) — One-hour build course: ten Jev use-case builds (inbox sorting, ticket triage, slop filter, browser agent, lead scoring), Jev vs ChatGPT speed tests, cost math and confidence limits. Covers what the course claims to build; we have not run its code.
- [Agentium: "Jev (TypeSafe)" framework docs](https://docs.agentium.in/models/jev) — JS agent framework's integration reference: `model: jev("jev-latest")` makes the agent itself a decision, JevToolkit lets chat agents consult Jev, Jev as an eval judge in CI, and `planner: "jev"` for browser agents; notes `jev-latest` currently resolves to `jev-1.13.0`. Runnable examples included (e.g. `jev-triage.ts`).
- [dev.to: "Jev, the model that cannot write a word, and where it fits in web scraping" (2026-09-21)](http://dev.to/extractdata/jev-the-model-that-cannot-write-a-word-and-where-it-fits-in-web-scraping-does-it-45jb) — Practitioner test of Jev as a post-extraction data-quality gate: one batched call with six typed questions over scraped book records; across 59 correct + 59 planted-mismatch records, a single `category_right` question caught 46 crossings at a 0.5 threshold with zero false alarms. Honest about jaggedness: a Choice labeled a category-listing page "poetry" at confidence 1.00.
- [yibie/jev-engineering-zh](https://github.com/yibie/jev-engineering-zh) — 《Jev 工程学：为 coding agent 而作》完整中文翻译 — TypeSafe 创始人那篇 "JEV ENGINEERING FOR CODING AGENTS" blueprint 的中文版，保留原结构与 7 张插图 (30★ in under a day, posted 2026-09-22).
- [Bite Code: "What's all this noise about Jev?" (2026-09-22)](https://www.bitecode.dev/p/whats-all-this-noise-about-jev) — Hands-on walkthrough with a real Python script: Jev classifies the author's own last-10 blog posts' tags, 8/10 out of the box → 9/10 after prompt tweaks, with a cost comparison against Claude. Also flags the fake-demo accusations around launch demos.
- [codaaiteam/jev-ai](https://github.com/codaaiteam/jev-ai) — Community-maintained Jev quickstart & FAQ: 30-second version, API guide (curl/Python/TS), pricing notes, and free no-waitlist playground links. README-only repo; states it is not affiliated with TypeSafe AI. (4★)
- [Preporato: "Learn Jev: Hands-On Tutorial, Live Demo and Free Lab"](https://preporato.com/jev) — Honest interactive walkthrough with live API examples (raw request/response, 399ms shown) and a free 45-minute guardrail lab. Best part: a production comparison inside Preporato's own study tutor, where Jev replaced an LLM agent loop — Jev 20/20 vs LLM loop 13/20 on decisions that follow written rules, 10/10 vs 7/10 on giving the same move when a state repeats, median latency 520ms vs 18s. Equally honest about the failures: 32/64 predicting a learner's next move (worse than the 59% most-common-move baseline), no threshold that separated answer-leaking hints from safe ones, and one route given 0.94 confidence when wrong. API spec checked against the live API 2026-09-24. Full page verified.
- [Laravel News: "Decide with Jev: Laravel AI That Answers with a Probability" (mini course, EP01, 2026-09-26)](https://laravel-news.com/decide-with-jev-laravel-ai-that-answers-with-a-probability) — Builds a "content preflight checker" in Laravel: Jev scores whether a draft matches its brief — 0.96 for a complete draft, 0.02 for a thin one — with mid-range scores routed to a human editor. Uses the Laravel AI SDK (1.x dev branch at record time, since merged) plus a 9-minute video; full article verified. Note: Laravel AI SDK 1.0 adds Jev classification (announced the same week, per Laravel News headlines).
- [Laozhang.AI: "Jev AI Model: What It Is, How It Compares, and How to Use It" (2026-09-24)](https://blog.laozhang.ai/en/posts/jev-ai-model-guide) — Long-form tutorial with request anatomy (state/model/questions), cost arithmetic (1M ticket classifications at 400 input tokens ≈ $16.80), and a retest of Emil Lindfors's DeepSeek V4.1 Flash comparison on 24 Norwegian documents: agreement statistically tied (stance 20/24 vs 22/24 with reasoning on), but $0.22 vs $1.31/$3.08 per 1K docs and 0.32s vs 2.7s/26s median latency; Jev answers at ≥0.9 probability agreed with the reference 34/35 times, while DeepSeek's self-reported confidence did not separate right from wrong. Full text verified.
- [hirosichen/jev-workshop](https://github.com/hirosichen/jev-workshop) — 繁中工作坊学员资源：可打印讲义 PDF + 可编辑 docx + 可直接复制的代码示例（短网址 tinyurl.com/jevtw2026），每个灰色代码框右上角带一键复制按钮。 (0★, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [ShunsukeTamura06/jev-engineer-briefing](https://github.com/ShunsukeTamura06/jev-engineer-briefing) — 日语工程师勉強会约 15 分钟幻灯片（jev_briefing.html 浏览器直接打开）：以日银声明文"タカ・中立・ハト"三分类为例，用"記述式（LLM）vs マークシート（Jev）"的比喻讲清机制、适用场景与注意事项。 (0★, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [fangqiank/JevDemo](https://github.com/fangqiank/JevDemo) — 中文 README：基于 TypeSafe Jev 的 TypeScript 最佳实践 demo，13 个可运行场景覆盖 state 准备 / question 编写 / answer 使用三件事，全部调用真实 Jev API。 (0★, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [dev.to: "Laya a decision making system one model" (Shariful Islam Sourav, 2026-09-26)](http://dev.to/sharifulislamsourav/laya-a-decision-making-system-one-model-41fb) — Weekend build: an order-risk fraud detector on Laya with a GitHub repo and a live demo (order-risk.netlify.app). Self-reported speeds: 5s+/request on a laptop CPU, <1s on Colab T4, ~4s/request on a 2vCore/4GB VPS. Methodology lesson: let the model output the risk score, keep business rules in the application layer; semantic bias noted (`failed_payment_attempts: 0` read as a risk signal → reworded in plain language). Full text verified.
- [pcbrom/laya-jev](https://github.com/pcbrom/laya-jev) — 葡萄牙语大学课程材料（PPGI/UnB）：Colab notebook 从架构讲到校准，对比开放权重的 Laya 与 API 访问的 TypeSafe Jev——固定维度输出的 MLP 分类器如何变成在选项间共享评估器的 decisor。 (0★, Jupyter Notebook, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Real Python: "How to Get Started With Jev in Python"](https://realpython.com/jev-python/) — Hands-on tutorial with free sample code: a train-station visitor scenario replaces rigid `Y`/`N` checks with a Noul, then asks Choice + Score + Noul in one request; setup via `uv` + TypeSafe SDK + OpenRouter key, plus an honest troubleshooting section (bad instruction wording dropped a "yes" from 0.98 to 0.31). Full page verified 2026-09-30.
- [dev.to: "What Is Jev AI? A Practical Getting-Started Guide to TypeSafe's Decision Model" (Charlie Chen, 2026-09-29)](https://dev.to/charlie_chen_d661f49e69cb/what-is-jev-ai-a-practical-getting-started-guide-to-typesafes-decision-model-2gaf) — Accurate getting-started walkthrough: the three question types, the Jev-vs-LLM decision-layer framing, honest production habits (pin `jev-1.13.0`, measure your own traffic, vendor latency figures are vendor figures). Disclosure: the code walkthrough routes through jevmodel.org, an independent paid proxy (higher per-token than going direct) — convenient, not neutral. Full text verified.

## News & Articles

- [The Register: "Cloudflare tries to outplay Jev with open-weight Clef models" (2026-10-01)](https://www.theregister.com/ai-and-ml/2026/10/01/cloudflare-tries-to-outplay-jev-with-open-weight-clef-models/5300649) — "Two weeks after the Jev model took the AI world by storm": Clef has an LLM backbone — specially post-trained frozen Qwen3.8-27B (Clef) / Qwen3.5-9B (Clef-flash), prefill-only pass then parallel choice scoring. Cloudflare ran its tests against the HF Jev Decision Index and its own suite: self-reported scores are not yet reproduced on the official index; it claims beating Jev in three of four TypeSafe benchmark areas, losing on agent trace observability. Cost catch: open weights and more capable, but $0.24/M input vs Jev's $0.042/M.
- [AI Daily Post: "Cloudflare's Clef AI Models Win 7 of 10 Benchmarks" (2026-10-02)](https://aidailypost.com/news/cloudflares-clef-ai-models-top-7) — Recap of the Clef launch with the honest caveat stated: Cloudflare built both the benchmark and the model tested against a format it is also compatible with — "founders should pull the weights and run their own schema-specific tests rather than taking the 7-of-10 headline at face value."
- [The Information (via Techmeme): TypeSafe in talks to raise $1B+ at a $10B+ valuation (2026-09-24)](https://www.varindia.com/news/typesafe-10b-jev-bet) — Nine days after a $40M seed at a ~$200M valuation (DCVC-led), investors are reportedly offering to lead a round at $10B+; talks remain preliminary. The thesis rests on adoption: Jev reportedly reached ~13% of Vercel paid teams within 24h of launch — the gateway's fastest-adopted model (vendor/agency-reported; free-access window, not necessarily recurring paid demand). Sober framing: a $10B valuation is a bet that "typed decision" models become an important automation layer, which commercial evidence has yet to catch up with.
- [ByteIota: "Jev Is Not an LLM — and That's the Point"](https://byteiota.com/jev-is-not-an-llm-and-thats-the-point/) — Honest explainer with a real critique section: TypeSafe's own disclosure that its model-capabilities team designed the workflow evals (treat peak 193.6×/444.6× as ceiling estimates), the VentureBeat injection finding (a fabricated authorization message in the state dropped Jev's block probability for a destructive shell command 0.76 → 0.48 — confirmed by TypeSafe's own limitations page), and the HN "frontier model" framing debate (CEO conceded the zero-shot-classifier characterization is accurate). Third-party numbers quoted: Vercel 5–18× faster and more accurate (Vercel's own report), Bryo AI 10–20× cheaper than Gemini on business email classification.
- [Bart Collet: "The last signature" (2026-09-23)](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/last-signature-bart-collet-gjr5e) — Long-form essay on what cheap machine checking means for healthcare and accountability: Robert Scoble's Sully.ai team was building with Jev <48h after launch; Yohei Nakajima called hospitals the better showcase. Sharp on the failure mode — "zero hallucinations" means zero *malformed* outputs, not zero errors; a well-formed wrong answer routed in a tenth of a second fails silently and at volume. Jevons paradox for verification: cheaper checking invites more decisions, while the number of humans who can underwrite them doesn't scale.
- [Codecaf: "What Is Jev? TypeSafe AI's New 'Decision Model' Explained" (2026-09-24)](https://codecaf.com/what-is-jev-typesafe-ais-new-decision-model-explained/) — Dev-oriented explainer with verified curl/JS/Python samples; keeps the vendor claims honest ("the benchmark is the vendor's own methodology on workflows the vendor chose, with no independent reproduction yet") and documents the no-waitlist access paths (Vercel AI Gateway `typesafe-ai/jev`, OpenRouter `typesafe/jev-1.13`, Cloudflare Workers AI, Netlify, LiteLLM).
- [Jock: "Jev Benchmark: TypeSafe's Model That Cannot Write a Word" (2026-09-21)](https://thoughts.jock.pl/p/jev-typesafe-system-one-model-benchmark-2026) — Independent hands-on benchmark: 40 support tickets through Jev vs four text-generating models. Jev was ~10× faster than Claude Fable 5.1 and ~329× cheaper, and beat Fable at guessing customer annoyance. Includes a real terminal transcript (403ms, $0.0000172 per call) and a fractional anger score (2.07 on calm→furious) used to sort queues.
- [Doomers: "How Doomers Launched TypeSafe AI and Jev on X" (2026-09-21)](https://doomers.ai/work/typesafe-ai-case-study) — Launch-agency case study with third-party launch metrics: the Jev launch post reached ~38.5M views, 74K likes, ranking 4 of 675 tracked launches (Doomers' largest); Vercel reported Jev as the fastest-adopted model in AI Gateway history — ~13% of paid teams within 24h, 2× the GPT-5.6 family, 6× Fable 5.1. Vendor/agency figures, not lab benchmarks.
- [Creative AI News: "Six Open Jev Clones, Four Different Jev Scores" (2026-09-21)](https://www.creativeainews.com/articles/open-jev-clones-benchmark-disagreement-2026/) — Six open Jev reproductions shipped within 48h of launch (Laya, SemIf, Bespoke Nimble, Kev-0.5B, Jevlike, DiffusionGemmaJev) — but four independent evaluations of the same Jev on the same public Banking77 benchmark put it at 87.0%, 83.2%, 77.8% and 76.3%: "The clone wave is not a race to reproduce a model. It is six teams reproducing six different targets."
- [Indian Express: "Meet Jev" (2026-09-21)](https://indianexpress.com/article/technology/artificial-intelligence/meet-jev-new-ai-model-from-chatgpt-inventor-10887591/) — General-availability announcement: waitlist dropped, access starts at $5 in credits (~120M tokens); Vercel, Cloudflare, LangChain and Langfuse have added Jev to their stacks.
- [MarkTechPost: "TypeSafe AI Releases Jev" (2026-09-19)](https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/09/19/typesafe-ai-releases-jev/) — Best single roundup: pricing fine print, community projects, Vercel/Bryo/Droidrun data points.

RLCD & Parallel Inference Explained"](https://www.explainx.ai/blog/how-does-jev-work-rlcd-system-one-model-explained-2026) — Architecture question treated honestly: RLCD objective + single parallel forward pass are confirmed; encoder-only transformer / text-diffusion / purpose-built are labeled reader speculation, not confirmed fact.
- [Gadget Pilipinas: "TypeSafe Jev: System One Model, answered by Laya" (2026-09-20)](https://www.gadgetpilipinas.net/2026/09/typesafe-jev-system-one-model-laya/) — Laya's prior-art dispute (March 2025 papers, arXiv:2503.23303) plus an independent 78-case test: Jev 0.974 vs Laya 0.590, Laya fastest at 30ms/case vs ~302ms for Jev.
- [BuzzRAG: "TypeSafe's Jev Bets on Faster Decisions for AI"](https://buzzrag.com/article/typesafe-jev-faster-decisions-ai-agents-7vnwji) — Cites an Every/Forkast extraction test (~25× faster, 580× cheaper than Claude Fable 5.1 on one task) and notes no named production customers or disclosed revenue yet.
- [Cherry Creek News: "Jev evals measure agreement, not correctness"](https://thecherrycreeknews.com/typesafe-jev-system-one-model-claims-evals-independent-tests-cherry_creek/) — Methodology critique of TypeSafe's workflow evals (reference answer is the average of GPT-6 Astra and Fable 5.1), with footnotes on the Doom and Wikiracing demos.
- [Foundermag: "TypeSafe AI's Jev Becomes the Fastest-Adopted Model in Vercel AI Gateway History"](https://foundermag.co/launches/jev/) — Per Vercel's September AI Gateway Production Index, Jev reached 13% of paid Gateway teams within 24h of launch — 2× the GPT-5.6 family, 6× Fable 5.1. Third-party usage data, not a lab benchmark.
- [Manj Chenna: "Jev AI, stripped down"](https://manjchenna.com/essays/jev-typesafe-system-one-model) — Long-form honest analysis: probabilities are facts about your question, not the world; "zero hallucination" means zero out-of-schema answers, not correct answers; calibration is the claim that would actually matter and is still unproven; notes TechCrunch reporting that outside observers suspect Jev is built on an open-weight LLM.
- [Arize AI: "TypeSafe Jev: Can Decision Models Replace LLM Judges?"](https://arize.com/blog/typesafe-jev-llm-judge/) — Independent eval-company analysis with fresh third-party numbers: Every's head of evals ran 777 judgments in under 0.7s for ~$0.0025; UK events site NearHere got 96% from Jev vs 86% from Gemini Flash-Lite on listing moderation, 58× cheaper per decision; a developer ran Jev zero-shot over 18,514 spam emails to 98.3% vs 98.4% for a TF-IDF logistic regression trained on 14,800 labeled emails (statistical tie), with a calibration curve that routes the uncertain 4.6% of emails to humans for 99.5% on the rest. Arize says it will run its own benchmarks, and calls the "can't hallucinate" claim an overreach — it's a schema guarantee, not correctness.
- [Flowtivity: "Jev by TypeSafe AI: Is the 200x Faster Decision Model Too Good to Be True?" (2026-09-16)](https://flowtivity.ai/blog/jev-typesafe-ai-decision-model/) — Claim-by-claim audit with verdict "the speed and price are real as published, the intelligence comparison is vendor-graded homework": Doom runs at ~10 queries/sec (~$7/hr on published prices); own cost model — 40K classification decisions/mo costs ~$1.34/mo on Jev vs ~$900 on a frontier model at $10/M input tokens (modeled from published prices, not a live benchmark; disclosure of no TypeSafe relationship included).
- [FoundeReview: "The model that won't write" (2026-09-17)](https://foundereview.com/r/typesafe.ai/chokt310055) — Evidence-only review from outside the waitlist (no API key issued): Every's 777 judgments in <0.7s survived contact; Dan Shipper's writing checks — Jev 0.35s median vs Claude Fable 5.1's 8.83s at ~1/580th the cost, catching 6/7 planted defects (it missed, three times, the defect that needed a beat of reasoning); Discord-sourced tests — 96.5% over a 662-message prompt-injection corpus, 6M tokens of word-game judging for $0.14, while one tester fed 2,900 trading days for under a cent and found no predictive signal. Conclusion: speed and price have survived third-party contact; the calibration claim has not.
- [LumaDock: "What is Jev? The AI model with no text output that plays Doom"](https://lumadock.com/blog/what-is-jev-typesafe) — Long explainer with the full workflow-eval table (Jev 67.8% at $0.0004/0.4s vs GPT-5.6 Terra 67.9% at $0.0304/10.1s; security-incidents row: Jev 61.7% at $0.0001/0.3s vs Sol 62.5% at $0.0295/8.5s — same call, 300× cheaper); also surveys self-host routes for the same pattern: GLiClass (Apache 2.0, CPU-fast, scores not calibrated) and Qwen 2.5 logprobs. Written by a GPU-VPS vendor.
- [explainx.ai: "6 Jev Clones in 2 Days" (updated 2026-09-21)](https://www.explainx.ai/blog/six-jev-clones-two-days-2026) — Six independent open-source re-implementations within 48h of launch (Laya, Bespoke Nimble, Jevlike, Kev, OpenJev, DiffusionGemmaJev) via Latent.Space's catalogue, with diversity-of-approach analysis. 2026-09-21 correction: Kev's real lineup is 0.8B/4B/9B on Qwen3.5 (not "8B"), with published benchmarks vs Jev and an HN thread.
- [mer.vin: "Laya: The 33ms Open-Source Decision Model Beating Jev" (2026-09-21)](https://mer.vin/news/laya-the-33ms-open-source-decision-model-beating-jev/) — Practical Laya deep dive: `Router(preload=True)` is the one production detail that matters — lazy loading rebuilds the checkpoint on every language switch (7.4s median reload on CPU), and the headline 0.766 accuracy belongs to the benchmark-fine-tuned checkpoint (zero-shot is ~0.36, near random). Kaggle 2×T4 fine-tuning notebook included.
- [AI Weekly: "Convai ships Laya" (2026-09-19)](https://aiweekly.co/alerts/convai-ships-laya-a-421m-modernbert-decision-model-apache-20) — Model-card honesty rundown: zero-shot 0.362 vs 0.318 random baseline and 0.461 majority-class; refitting one temperature per question type moves mean ECE 0.466 → 0.081; multilingual macro-average on MASSIVE Intent (51 languages) 0.227 — "100+ language coverage buys reach, not accuracy."
- [The Unwind AI: "Get started with Jev for free" (2026-09-20)](https://www.theunwindai.com/p/get-started-with-jev-for-free) — LocalJev: Jev-compatible API served on local models via oMLX, tested across 1,200 requests on five 4-bit models on an M5 Max (prompted JSON probability output, not direct-logit — check calibration); official TypeSafe Agent Skill for Claude Code to find Jev-shaped holes in your codebase; kev's six-questions-in-~160ms claim with Jev leading ~19 points out-of-domain.
- [scriptbyai.com: "The Ultimate Jev Resource List 2026" (2026-09-20)](https://www.scriptbyai.com/jev-resource-list/) — Competing resource catalogue: open System-One implementations (choosekit, Decider, Jeff, LitJev, LocalJev, NanoJev, jevmlx, OpenDecision, openjev-sglang, Jev Visual) — repos unverified by us, listed as discovery leads.
- [aliaihub/awesome-jev-usecases](https://github.com/aliaihub/awesome-jev-usecases) — Evidence-backed use-case catalogue for building with Jev: design patterns, a question catalog, failure modes, and a model-selection guide — every claim labeled and sourced (15★, active on 2026-09-21).
- [Pranay Suyash (Medium): "Jev's 193.6× Faster, 444.6× Cheaper Claim: What TypeSafe's Workflow Eval Actually Measures" (2026-09-21)](https://pranaysuyash.medium.com/jevs-193-6-faster-444-6-cheaper-claim-what-typesafe-s-workflow-eval-actually-measures-68b8529e822b) — Takes the headline multipliers apart: the benchmark decomposes the task before calling the model, so it tests a way of building software as much as a model. Walks through the four published workflows (security-incident, agent-trace, invoice, customer-service) and the three primitives (Noul, Choice, Score).
- [Mathrubhumi English: "Jev AI Model Explained: Why Developers Choose Decision-Making AI" (2026-09-22)](https://english.mathrubhumi.com/technology/jev-ai-model-explained-decision-making-ai-fajsf1e6) — Mainstream-India explainer: three primitives, RLCD training, $0.042/M input with free output, and the no-waitlist GA move on 2026-09-21.
- [GrowthR: "What Is Jev? TypeSafe's New AI Model"](https://growthr.com/resources/what-is-jev/) — Practitioner explainer with a trimmed `POST /v1/systemone` example; useful line that calibration is a claim about groups of predictions — "saying 0.8 means right ~80% of the time" says nothing certain about any single answer.
- [dev.to: "Jev Does Not Replace the LLM. It Changes Who Owns the Decision" (2026-09-19)](https://dev.to/miruky/jev-does-not-replace-the-llm-it-changes-who-owns-the-decision-3n6) — Architecture piece: "System One" is TypeSafe's product term, not a standardized model taxonomy; notes `jev-latest` resolves to `jev-1.13.0` and the docs list a 64k request budget.
- [dev.to: "TypeSafe AI Jev: An AI model that doesn't generate text – and is 200x faster" (2026-09-18)](https://dev.to/saaro_net/typesafe-ai-jev-an-ai-model-that-doesnt-generate-text-and-is-200x-faster-1ggo) — Community explainer on the three primitives (Choice / Score / Noul) and the parallel-forward-pass speed story.
- [Kingy AI: "TypeSafe Jev Review: The AI Model That Doesn't Generate Text" (2026-09-17)](https://kingy.ai/blog/typesafe-jev-review-the-ai-model-that-doesnt-generate-text/) — Document-and-eval audit with explicit scope limits (no live API key at publication): reconstructs the workflow-eval table (Jev 67.8% aggregate at $0.0004/0.4s vs Sol 74.1% at $0.0836/23.3s over 711 cases) and shows the homepage "193.6× faster, 444.6× cheaper" headline cannot be reconstructed from the rounded table — quote as TypeSafe's workload-specific claims, not universal multipliers. Also frames "zero hallucinations" as a type-safety guarantee, not a correctness one.
- [matt-harness: TypeSafe Jev claim-by-claim research note (2026-09-18)](https://github.com/wasikarn/matt-harness/blob/HEAD/docs/research/typesafe-ai-system-one-jev-2026-09-18.md) — Research verdict: the company, docs site and people are real (live docs with ~35 fetchable pages, open-source MIT Python adapter) — but the performance numbers are self-reported, with no independent benchmark or third-party citation on the docs site. Treat "does the product exist" and "are the claims verified" as separate questions.
- [Zentor: "What Is TypeSafe Jev? Typed Agent Decisions" (updated 2026-09-21)](https://zentor.ai/blog/what-is-typesafe-jev) — Vendor explainer (Zentor sells workflow automation) walking through the request shape — state plus typed questions (`choice`, `score`, `noul`) evaluated in parallel, "adding questions barely changes the response time" — with TypeSafe docs quotes checked 2026-09-18.
- [jeviguide.com: "Is Jev an LLM?" (independent FAQ, checked 2026-09-19)](https://jevaiguide.com/faq/is-jev-an-llm/) — Architecture question, honestly scoped: RLCD objective + single parallel forward pass are confirmed; TechCrunch reports it is transformer-based; base model, size, training data all undisclosed. - [SaaSCity: "Jev by TypeSafe AI: System One Model Guide for Startups (2026)" (updated 2026-09-19)](https://saascity.io/blog/system-one-models-jev-typesafe-ai-2026) — Founder-oriented guide: unit-economics math, five high-value queue use cases, the hybrid "traffic cop + specialist" stack. - [AI Weekly: "UT Dallas' Jev-Mem cuts agent query latency 36.7% on LoCoMo" (2026-09-22)](https://aiweekly.co/alerts/ut-dallas-jev-mem-cuts-agent-query-latency-367-on-locomo) — First Jev-flavored research preprint (indexed on Hugging Face): System-One control plane, full LLM only for answer synthesis.
