- [dev.to: "Fast Decisions in Agent Workflows: Laya vs TypeSafe Jev" (2026-09-22)](http://dev.to/x_z_e87b809fe996bc463fe4a/fast-decisions-in-agent-workflows-laya-vs-typesafe-jev-3ago) — Independent JevLab write-up comparing open-source Laya to hosted Jev: Laya ~7.8× faster locally (32.8ms vs Jev's published 236–276ms) with tighter calibration after a temperature refit (ECE 0.081 vs 0.246). Honest about the setup: these are vendor/third-party tables, not a controlled A/B — "accuracy without thresholding is an illusion"; the shipping question is confidence thresholds and fail-closed handoffs.
- [Doco: "An AI That Doesn't Write a Single Word—How Is It 193x Faster and 444x Cheaper" (2026-09-20)](https://doco.page/s/CtgDAZsecX1tr3y8XMA7pNnpDedJCPzs) — 12,000-word independent deep dive: reconstructs the four workflow evals from TypeSafe's own data (Jev 67.8% avg vs frontier 68–74%), quotes 36Kr/Tencent's verdict that the real comparison should also include Flash models + constrained decoding and traditional classifiers ("no such test exists yet"), and reports two third-party tests not covered elsewhere — @DanRWilloughby's Sniff-Test linter table (Jev 78.8% / 182ms / $0.013 per 1K vs Haiku 82.5% / 1,971ms / $0.43) and a 24-Norwegian-hearing-document test (Jev $0.22/K docs, median 0.32s, stricter question wording worsened ECE 0.040 → 0.116). - [dev.to: "Jev vs Laya: The Same AI Idea, One Closed and One Open" (2026-09-22)](https://dev.to/jamilxt/jev-vs-laya-the-same-ai-idea-one-closed-and-one-open-3c6e) — Honest benchmark reading guide: Laya's fine-tuned 0.766 vs Jev's 0.727 comparison is specialization, not superiority; realistic independent gains for decision-in-pipeline workloads land ~7–25×, not the vendor "ceiling" numbers; Jev wins out of the box, Laya wins when the task matches its training. Includes Laya's Bengali-script trap (0.080 accuracy at 0.945 confidence — calibration that lies) and a zero-shot RAG routing test where untuned Laya hit 0% against hosted Jev's 61.4%.

- [Apex36: "What Is TypeSafe Jev? - [OffGrid Studio: "What Is Jev? - [Firecrawl: "What Is Jev? - [Drawpie: "Jev, TypeSafe AI's Model: Specs, Price, Access"](https://drawpie.com/blog/what-is-jev-typesafe-ai-model/) — Honest spec/price audit (figures read from TypeSafe's docs on 2026-09-22): catches the GA-to-signup-pause gap at 32.8h and flags the 64K-request-budget vs 32K-context inconsistency across hosts (reported, not reconciled); keeps the headline multiples next to TypeSafe's own caveats (evals score against the Astra+Fable average, "0% hallucination" is definitional, not empirical).
- [Terence Luk: "TypeSafe Jev: The System One Model" (2026-09-24)](https://blog.terenceluk.com/2026/09/typesafe-jev-system-one-model.html) — Honest hands-on tutorial through Vercel AI Gateway: a claims table separating TypeSafe's launch-post figures (40–200× faster, up to 444.6× cheaper) from what the author could verify, a real first-call walkthrough, an Azure Monitor alert-triage example, and caught docs discrepancies (Vercel model page says 32K context vs KB guide's 64K request / 32K state). - [Eastern Herald: "TypeSafe AI's Jev Skips Language, Makes Decisions Instantly" (2026-09-21)](https://easternherald.com/2026/09/21/typesafe-ai-jev-decision-model-developers/) — Narrative piece with the sharpest calibration critique among explainers: TypeSafe has published no RLCD preprint and no standard benchmarks vs Anthropic/Google/Meta, so "calibration quality, not just speed, is what makes classification models trustworthy in production"; notes the API was briefly unreachable within hours of launch from demand.
- [The Register: "Shut up and calculate: Jev's new AI primitives for coders" (2026-09-23)](https://www.theregister.com/devops/2026/09/23/shut-up-and-calculate-jevs-new-ai-primitives-for-coders/5298431) — Week-one developer adoption roundup: Nikunj Kothari (FPV Ventures) set up jevable.com to collect Jev prototype apps posted on X; the gaming-hack wave (Doom, Tetris, chess — where Jev lost to the open-weight GLM 5.3 model but was far cheaper to run); a $0.0011-per-decision live outfit-mockup app at ~620ms. - [GuardingPearSoftware: "How to use Jev in game development" (updated 2026-09-23)](https://www.guardingpearsoftware.com/blog/how-to-use-jev-in-game-development-55682) — Honest independent-latency aggregation with named sources: WotAI p50 455ms (fastest of 16 models); Benchmark Heaven 242 decisions, median 0.65s / p95 0.72s measured from Germany; Sprite Fusion live platformer level-gen 319–375ms/request; TrueStandard single classification 1.7× faster than cheap chat models, but replacing six sequential judgments with one batched Jev call 100.7× faster / 7,499× cheaper; Paul Wei (X) Slay the Spire 2 moves at 0.7s/move. Conclusion: genuinely the fastest thing to call, 1.5–2× on single small questions vs cheapest chat models, 100×-scale gains only when a batched call replaces a chain of sequential LLM calls. TypeSafe's own launch numbers (70–500ms, 193.6×/444.6×) cited as vendor-run high-end.
- [Hugging Face blog: "Jev ai vs djev vs Laya vs OpenJev vs SemIf: Which Decision Model Should You Use?"](https://huggingface.co/blog/sora-2/jev-ai-vs-djev-vs-laya-vs-openjev-vs-semif-which-d) — Selection guide grounded in the JevBench v1.3.0 shared-evaluation snapshot (52 systems, 534 decisions; composite = intelligence + calibration + speed + cost): Jev 1.13.0 74.4 (#1), SemIf 73.1 (#2), djev 73.0 (#3), OpenJev 66.4 (#11), Laya 54.4 (#33). Jev leads calibration (82.7) and hard-tier accuracy (74.1%); djev leads the speed axis; SemIf leads the judge tier (95.2% vs 94.5%). Advice is constraint-first ("choose by what would be most expensive to change later") with an honest walkthrough of how to run your own evaluation. Caveats spelled out: a composite score hides trade-offs, and the snapshot is dated — not a product guarantee.
- [@conanssam: Korean hate-comment detection benchmark (Threads, 2026-09-24)](https://www.threads.com/@conanssam/post/DdsRqkIn_hA) — Same 471 cases, three systems on a Korean hate-speech validation set (none/offensive/hate): Jev 1.13.0 (63.0% 3-class accuracy, 95.1% hate precision, 82.5% recall, F1 0.883, $0.023/1K) vs local CLM-8B INT8 (39.9% accuracy, F1 0.776, $0.000) vs Claude Haiku 4.5 (67.3% accuracy, 95.7% precision, F1 0.875, $3.621). Median latency on a log scale: decider-2b (warm) 17ms, OpenJev 85ms, SemIf 110ms, CLM-8B 325ms, jev-1.13 356ms, Haiku 8,073ms. Key finding: CLM binary collapse — 434/471 flagged hate, zero 'hate' predictions, mostly 'offensive'. Caveats: author's own labels, numbers read from the infographic, not independently verified.
- [B.AI: "B.AI Adds TypeSafe AI's Jev Model to Its API" (2026-09-23)](https://www.binance.com/en/square/post/09-23-2026-b-ai-adds-typesafe-ai-s-jev-model-to-its-api-369782022358093) — API gateway adds Jev (via Binance Square, citing ChainCatcher): full-access section, callable as `Jev-1.13.0` or `Jev-Latest`; positioned for ticket routing, content moderation, risk scoring, agent branching. Company announcement, not a measurement.
- [dev.to (gde): "Jev After Eight Days of Independent Tests: Level With Mid-Price LLMs, Behind the Frontier" (2026-09-24)](https://dev.to/gde/jev-after-eight-days-of-independent-tests-level-with-mid-price-llms-behind-the-frontier-1kln) — Meta-review of the independent evidence eight days after launch: 14 arXiv preprints read in full, 104 GitHub repos and 33 dev.to/Medium posts, every figure traced to a primary source and re-scored from committed per-item outputs where published (studies graded A/B/C; "Reported" marks what can't be checked). Findings: Jev accuracy sits level with mid-price LLMs and 6.5–11.5pp behind the frontier in the cleanest comparisons (Janardhan's 200-item six-model run re-scored exactly: Fable 84.0% vs Jev 72.5%; arXiv 2609.24574's 7,977-item social-science replication: Jev behind the best of 19 LLMs on 14/15 tasks, median 11.6 macro-F1). Calibration: Jev's own probabilities are the best-measured out-of-the-box on familiar English tasks (median ECE 0.071 on the Bespoke 13-subset suite), but miscalibrate off-distribution with the error direction flipping; one temperature fitted on 50 to a few hundred labels fixes most of it (civil_comments ECE 0.16–0.21 → <0.025). Speed/cost vs LLMs runs 0.5×–12.1× / 0.6×–478× depending on comparator — one fitted temperature also brings a local Gemma 4 26B's calibration to 0.080 vs Jev's 0.071 as shipped. Also tracks which studies have conflicts of interest (launch partners, gateway vendors, replica authors). Full text verified.
- [Fractional (research.fractional.company): "Jev Tested: 2–5x Faster and 5–50x Cheaper, Not 193.6x"](https://research.fractional.company/articles/jev-typed-decision-models/) — Research article (crawled 2026-09-25) dissecting the headline multipliers with cited sources: TypeSafe's own launch notes admit the workflow evals "were made by individuals on our model capabilities team, so some bias could exist", comparators ran through TypeSafe's slower/cheaper probability wrapper, and the comparator is never named — checked against published summary points, 193.6× fits Jev-vs-Sonnet-5 timing and 444.6× fits Jev-vs-Opus-5 cost, "an inference, not TypeSafe's". Aggregates four independent testers measured Sept 18–22 (ayautomate, jujumilk3, AnthusAI, jevbench): median latencies 0.24–0.39s, confirming the Jev half of the speed claim; LLMs configured for a decision leave Jev only 2.0–3.6× faster, and on billed cost per decision Jev is roughly 5–50× cheaper. Also notes "schema-valid is not correct": LLMs have had constrained decoding since Aug 2024, so Jev's real differences are native per-option probabilities, parallel questions, input-only billing and a sub-second floor. Full text verified.
- [Thunders: "What Jev Changes in AI Test Automation"](https://www.thunders.ai/articles/jev-system-one-ai-test-automation) — A test-automation vendor's own Jev tests and integration notes: Jev answers in roughly 100–200ms vs 2–10s for the large language models in the same comparison; Thunders routes first runs / cache-invalidated steps to Jev behind a deterministic cached-selector replay layer, and gates the provider per customer because TypeSafe publishes no EU region. Honest limitations list: text only, 32k state budget vs DOMs that often exceed it (25% of enterprise apps cross 32k), no EU region, rate limits that "can move without notice", no independent benchmark they've seen, and Jev can't count, calculate or compare dates; calibration is a group property that tells you how to route, not whether an answer is right; the `jev-latest` alias moves with releases, so pin the version. Vendor blog — its measurements, not neutral coverage. Full text verified.
- [Silverthread Labs: "Jev AI Limitations: What the 193x Benchmark Doesn't Measure"](https://www.silverthreadlabs.com/blog/jev-ai-benchmark-limitations) — Claim audit (crawled 2026-09-25): TypeSafe's workflow evals have no ground truth — the reference answer is "the average of GPT-6 Astra and Fable 5.1", so the score is agreement, not correctness (TypeSafe says this in its own notes, which the piece respects as unusual honesty). Security demos: a VentureBeat prompt-injection demo moved a `rm -rf ~/.ssh` block probability from 0.76 to 0.48 with a fake tool-output field, and Pydantic's docs note that the order of a `Literal`'s options "is part of what Jev sees" — reordering can move the answer. On Laya: its 0.766-vs-0.727 headline comes from a checkpoint fine-tuned on that benchmark's own training split (zero-shot base 0.362); on Banking77 Convai's own model card puts Jev 0.870 vs Laya 0.425. Conclusion: a fast, cheap, well-calibrated opinion — use it for routing/triage/scoring/flagging, never treat confidence as a security boundary. Consultancy blog with cited sources; full text verified.
- [ActionBox: "TypeSafe AI Jev Review: Tested vs LLMs, API & Benchmarks"](https://actionbox.cloud/blog/typesafe-ai-jev-review/) — Independent hands-on review: early-access test verified the Playground, real `jev-1.13.0` API responses and the official Python/TS SDK patterns (snippet type-checked against public SDK v0.6.0). Separates the product idea (typed results, parallel questions, low published price) from what still needs evidence: accuracy on independent datasets, calibration after shift, rate limits, architecture. Verdict: test Jev in shadow mode against historical human decisions before letting it touch a live workflow. Full text verified.
- [explainx.ai: "LangSmith Jev Evals: Score Every Production Trace" (2026-09-22)](https://www.explainx.ai/blog/langsmith-jev-production-trace-scoring-2026) — LangChain made Jev a first-class judge inside LangSmith Evals (Evaluators tab → TypeSafe provider): attach typed Choice/Score/Noul questions to production traces, online evaluators score live traffic; Jev Gateway passthrough adds token usage + cost lines on traces, guard policies on nested state, nested PII redaction, and model access policies. Same week TypeSafe dropped its waitlist (2026-09-21, $5 credit) — friction down for BYOK Gateway setups. Setup is evaluators-tab + feedback keys to dashboards/webhooks; framework-agnostic (any instrumented app sending runs to LangSmith). Honest limits listed: domain transfer unproven (benchmark ran on a five-example weather agent), typed questions need upfront design work, alpha `langchain-typesafe` packaging, LangSmith-stack concentration. Full text verified.
- [explainx.ai: "SemIf Free on LangSmith Gateway" (2026-09-22)](https://www.explainx.ai/blog/semif-langsmith-gateway-free-week-decision-ai-2026) — LangChain added a Decision models category to the LangSmith LLM Gateway: hosted SemIf (`semif-qwen3.5-4b`) free through 2026-09-28 for US Free/Developer/Plus workspaces, no Gateway Credits consumed, same System One `/v1/systemone` interface (noul/choice/score, up to 32 named questions, no streaming); Jev on the same gateway is BYOK (`typesafe/jev-1.13.0`). SemIf is independent of TypeSafe (repo formerly OpenJev, carries an explicit "not affiliated with TypeSafe" disclaimer). Benchmark lineage: Kev's external eval on SemIf's authored144 rows shows Jev 0.965 vs Kev-9B 0.917 (domain-dependent). Full text verified; promotion window is time-boxed.
- [RuntimeWire: "TypeSafe's Jev Router picks models for free, with a claimed million-token window" (2026-09-25)](https://runtimewire.com/article/typesafe-jev-router-openrouter-launch) — TypeSafe's "Jev Router" listed on OpenRouter Sept 25: Jev picks the model and reasoning effort per request, listed price $0 with a claimed 1M-token context window — versus the separate `typesafe/jev-1.13` listing at $0.042/M input, 32K context. OpenRouter has published no usage data or routing comparisons; the article notes the 1M-window scope (routed model vs routing process) is unexplained, eligible models and selection rules are undisclosed, and there is no head-to-head vs OpenRouter's own usage-based Auto Router. Full text verified.
- [Testomat.io: "Deterministic AI in Testing: What It Is & Why It Matters"](https://testomat.io/blog/deterministic-ai/) (2026-09-25) — Testomat's open-source Explorbot browser-testing agent now uses Jev for closed decisions (which element matches the intent, is the run healthy, is a result already verified) behind a 70% confidence threshold, with fallback to previous behavior on low confidence, timeout or failure. Their own measurements: supervisor decisions answered in ~0.3–0.4s with Jev vs ~2.5–2.8s with the larger agent model — but small page-reading questions were as fast or faster with a cheap non-reasoning model. Honest read of the vendor numbers: "Expect smaller gains in real tasks than the 193.6× peak" (notes TypeSafe's workflows were built by its own capabilities team and scored against frontier-model averages rather than ground truth). Vendor blog — their measurements, not neutral coverage. Full text verified.
- [JVM Weekly vol. 193: "Thinking, fast… and typed: Java & Jev" (2026-09-24)](https://www.jvm-weekly.com/p/thinking-fast-and-typed-java-and) — Documents the JVM wave: a GitHub search turned up 23 Java repos mentioning Jev by Sept 22 (22 created within the launch week), including the danvega starter and the spring-ai-typesafe integration above — plus langchain4j's "JudgeModel" typed-batch-judgment abstraction proposal (issue #6468, PR #6469 opened the same minute by Red Hat's Stefano Maestri, who argued the abstraction should be provider-neutral, not a TypeSafe-tied API). Newsletter essay; the repo/proposal facts above are author-reported. Full text verified.
- [t-klug: "Code Enumerates, Jev Judges" (2026-09-17)](https://github.com/t-klug/t-klug.github.io/blob/HEAD/_posts/2026-09-17-typesafe-jev-slopcheck.md) — First-person engineering write-up of slopcheck, a CI tool that reads every pull request and judges AI slop with Jev: 18 typed patterns, several hundred questions per change in one request, verdicts (MERGE READY / POLISH THEN MERGE / NEEDS REWORK / NEEDS A HUMAN) with exit codes CI can gate on. Whole project (build + every debug run) cost $0.91; shipped fixture: 9,629 input tokens, 0.7s round trip. Field-order and wording lessons included: "Literal means literal — the question was the bug." - [Filipe Motta (ITNEXT): "Your LLM Is Overqualified for Half Its Job: Jev for Cloud, DevOps and SRE Pipelines" (Sep 2026)](https://itnext.io/your-llm-is-overqualified-for-half-its-job-jev-for-cloud-devops-and-sre-pipelines-06ae80c77652) — Long-form mapping of where a decision model fits in cloud/SRE work: ten pipeline slots (incident triage, CI/CD failures, agent action gating, Terraform plan review, k8s troubleshooting, runbook selection, model routing, alert noise, security findings, cost anomalies), with a companion repo simulating three (incident triage, CI failure, action gate) — offline stub by default with a `--live` flag. Disclosed up front: the author has not run Jev on production traffic (TypeSafe early access Sep 15, public Sep 20 with $5 credit, signups paused Sep 22 under "immense swell of demand"), so the seven cases are architecture designs, not production reports; every number is checked against the API reference. Keeps vendor figures honest: 40–200× faster and "193.6× faster, 444.6× cheaper" came from TypeSafe's own capabilities team, labeled as claims to verify on your own traffic. Full text verified.
- [Mithil Maske (Medium): "What Is Jev? TypeSafe's System One Model, Explained" (2026-09-26)](https://medium.com/@mithilmaske/what-is-jev-typesafes-system-one-model-explained-b0a4d98ec263) — Synthesis of eight days of independent tests: six-model comparison has Jev at 72.5% vs Claude Fable 5.1 84.0% / GPT-6 Astra 79.0%; narrow binary work shines (98.33% on 18,514 spam emails, 91.7% classifying agent tool-call risk); calibration ECE 0.024–0.032 on public benchmarks, overconfident out-of-distribution (ECE 0.107). The technique the author says to internalize: decomposition — "is this phishing?" on 2,000 emails went 62.6% → 95.0% after splitting into five atomic questions plus regression, beating Haiku 4.5's 93.2% (splitting didn't help Haiku). Fair summary: "level with mid-price LLMs, behind the frontier, at a fraction of the cost." Also records the access rollercoaster: waitlist dropped Sep 20 with $5 credit, paused Sep 22 after rate-limit-abuse signups; Vercel AI Gateway noted as the practical free-tier side door. - [luisacsfreitas (Towards AI): “Jev Doesn’t Write, It Decides: Games Today, Company Data with Care, Computer Vision Next” (2026-09-24)](https://pub.towardsai.net/jev-doesnt-write-it-decides-games-today-company-data-with-care-computer-vision-next-21178d277475) — 10-min analysis: games are the natural home (bounded choices, engine-owned rules, latency-critical) — cites community numbers: jev-benchmark (jev-1.13.0) F1 0.96 vs 0.82 fuzzy-match heuristic on 79 tricky NPC-addressee utterances; Jev-as-chess-engine ~37% best-move accuracy, ~950 Elo, 166ms median/move; jev-unreal-statetree C++ plugin for UE 5.8. Honest enterprise caution: API-only, no public weights; zero-retention only via enterprise agreement; pin the model version; option-order position effect (same option’s probability 0.84–0.96 depending on list position, per archerhume.com’s 1,200-item MMLU analysis). Vision outlook: jev-eyes (OCR → spatial char grid → Jev) and jev-multimodal; chained-probability warning. Full text verified.
- [aidiveyt (dev.to): "Jev did not make Claude Code cheaper. Its own benchmark says so." (2026-09-27)](https://dev.to/aidiveyt/jev-did-not-make-claude-code-cheaper-its-own-benchmark-says-so-1abm) — Code-reading audit of [vinilana/jev-gateway](https://github.com/vinilana/jev-gateway): one ternary in `src/adapters/messages.ts:99` decides the whole story — with thinking or prompt caching on, the gateway is only allowed to *hint*, and every real Claude Code request carries both, so "hint mode is the only mode there is"; the hint is a `<system-reminder>` the model can ignore, and the 47,000-token tool roster gets re-sent on every call to return four tokens. The gateway author's own bench (chess engine): routing paid on bugfix (Sonnet 5 −48% input tokens, −25% time) but cost +61% tokens / +83% time on Opus 5 feature work — numbers the six hype videos never quoted, nor the README's own line: "Expect better tool picks on large tool lists, not lower cost or latency." Verdict: tool routing in Claude Code — no; Codex (where the tool is forced) — yes. Author discloses AI-draft assistance but "the runs, the numbers and the opinions are mine". Full text verified.
- [Wikipedia: "Jev (AI model)"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jev_(AI_model)) — Jev got its own encyclopedia entry (live as of 2026-09-26): TypeSafe AI founded 2024 by Diogo Almeida, Erik Gafni and Sasha Sheng; limited early access on Sept 15, 2026 alongside the $40M DCVC-led seed round at a Forbes-reported ~$200M valuation; returns typed values with probability estimates and confidence scores instead of generated text; TypeSafe frames Jev as the first of a class called "System One models". Page verified live.

Vendor claims only: OpenAI says 150ms vs GPT-6 Luna's 1.6s; pricing unknown; limited preview now, broad release "in the coming days". The article contains zero comparative numbers vs Jev and quotes no Jev metrics at all. Disclosure on page: TNS owner Insight Partners is an investor in OpenAI. Full text verified via browser read.
- [neuroflash: "Jev AI Model Explained: What It Means for Market Research" (2026-09-25)](https://neuroflash.com/blog/ai-news/jev-ai-model/) — Explainer that separates Jev fact from marketing (flags 40–200× speed figures as unverified, no published paper yet); the market-research angle: calibrated probabilities predate Jev in surveys, and Jev's synthetic-only RLCD calibration vs neuroflash's 1M+ real survey profiles. Read with the source in mind — the last third is a Digital Twins product pitch. Full text verified.
- [Farit Galeev (Medium): "How Might TypeSafe's Jev Work? Rebuilding a 'System One' Decoder on a Laptop" (2026-09-28)](https://medium.com/@f.galeev.business/how-might-typesafes-jev-work-rebuilding-a-system-one-decoder-on-a-laptop-48a7c104220b) — Reverse-engineering deep-dive with real experiments and code ([HiGal/parallel-constrained-decoding](https://github.com/HiGal/parallel-constrained-decoding), six notebooks): parallel constrained decoding over an off-the-shelf Qwen2.5-1.5B (4-bit, MLX, M1 Pro) turns a 28-field ticket-triage schema's 276 sequential forward passes (~2.4s) into 4 warm passes, schema-valid by construction. Key invention: "candidate mass" — the raw probability that the next token starts any allowed label — flags answers that look confident after renormalization (one fraud field: winner at 0.551 with only 24% of belief on allowed labels). Tokenizer traps documented with numbers: splitting at the colon gives median P(true)+P(false) 1.5e-5 vs 0.89 when cutting at real divergence points; quoted booleans flip 23 of 28 fields. Author's conclusion: the decoding trick explains single-query/free-output/no-type-errors and plausible latency (640–690ms on a laptop), but not calibrated confidence — "that's the real moat." Explicitly an independent reconstruction, not affiliated with TypeSafe. Full text verified.
- [Simon Willison: "Jev introduces a new shape of LLM—System One, aka Decision Models" (2026-09-21)](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Sep/21/jev/) — Independent deep read from the Datasette author: sides with "decision models" as the better name, walks the three primitives, and pushes the sharpest honest question on opacity — "If Jev marks something as spam, which content signals tipped it off?" — plus a self-run bias probe (rated Cupertino top, East Palo Alto bottom as a "Good city?"). Updated Sept 22 with his own `llm-typesafe` plugin. Full text verified.
- [Crypto Briefing: "Jev gains popularity as a faster, cheaper alternative to major AI models"](https://cryptobriefing.com/jev-ai-model-faster-cheaper-alternative/) — Launch recap framed honestly: Vercel-measured 5–18× faster than OpenAI models on classification, 10–20× cheaper than Gemini on email triage; median latency 0.44–0.48s; $0.042/M input tokens, output free; founders Diogo Almeida, Erik Gafni, Sasha Sheng; $40M DCVC-led seed. Notes Jev trains exclusively on synthetic data via RLCD and that open-weight "Jev-type" adapters may emerge; caveat: "doesn't always outperform on raw accuracy." Full text verified.

Runnable notebooks at `DataDog/llm-observability` (`typesafe-jev/`). Honest engineering notes: pin the model version, keep composite verdicts/thresholds in code, re-run calibration checks whenever the rubric or Jev version changes. Full text verified.

Full text verified.

- [HackerNoon: "JEV-27B-VL: Can AI Make Better Decisions From Images?" (2026-10-07)](https://hackernoon.com/jev-27b-vl-can-ai-make-better-decisions-from-images) — Deep write-up on autotrust's Apache-2.0 27B vision-language decision model: System 1 typed decisions (yes/no, 2–256 options, 0–5 ratings, 256K-token prompts) plus System 2 as the unmodified Qwen3.8-27B; reported AgentRewardBench AUROC 0.91, VL-RewardBench 78.3%, six text-decision benchmarks mean 84.07% vs Jev 1.13's 83.85% — all vendor-reported figures, not independently verified. Full text verified.
- [AICoderScope: "Jev and System One Models: What's Real in TypeSafe's 193x Claim — and Where a $0.042/M Decision Model Fits Your Coding Agent" (2026-10-05)](https://aicoderscope.com/blog/jev-system-one-models-structured-ai-decisions-coding-2026/) — Independent deconstruction of the 193.6×/444.6× headline: real arithmetic against TypeSafe's slowest/priciest baselines, graded against frontier-LLM labels rather than human ground truth. Two reproducible community benchmarks (arifulislamat/jev-benchmark, themsquared/jev-benchmark — both already listed) give the honest multiple: ~equal accuracy on single-shot classification, 3–5× faster, 30–65× cheaper than mid-tier frontier models; jev-guard's tool-call gating publishes the same ≈$0.00004-per-call figure. Practical read: at 300+ decisions a session the latency win is immediate; below ~100 decisions/day, skip the extra dependency. Full text verified.
- [OrcaRouter: "Why Cloudflare's Clef Deliberately Copies Jev's API" (2026-10-03)](https://www.orcarouter.ai/blog/clef-decision-models-release) — Reads the Clef release as product strategy: Clef speaks Jev's POST /v1/systemone wire format, so swapping decision models is a config change rather than a migration. Quotes Cloudflare's own (unreproduced) figures — BANKING77 macro-F1 94.2 vs Jev's 79.7 on the win side; GPQA Diamond 78.3 for Jev vs 48.0 for Clef on the loss side; 209.3ms vs 524.1ms median latency. Honest verdict: a real, fast, permissively-licensed decision model whose benchmark story is, so far, entirely the vendor's own account. Full text verified.
- [Startup Fortune: "OpenAI's new Decisions API looks a lot like its answer to a rival's Jev model"](https://startupfortune.com/openais-new-decisions-api-looks-a-lot-like-its-answer-to-a-rivals-jev-model/) — Frames the Sep 29 DevDay Decisions API (a constrained GPT-6 Luna; 150ms vs ~1.6s for a standard Luna call — OpenAI's own figures) against TypeSafe's Jev, which shipped Sep 15 after two years in stealth from ex-OpenAI researcher Diogo Almeida. Cites TechCrunch reporting that monitoring agents with Jev costs ~$2.94 vs ~$372 with a frontier LLM — the difference between a check you can run on every agent action and one you can only afford to run occasionally. Full text verified.
- [MarkTechPost: "Decision AI Models Explained: TypeSafe Jev vs Fastino GLiDE, GLiNER2.5-Decide and Open-Source Competitors" (2026-10-02)](https://www.marktechpost.com/2026/10/02/decision-ai-models-explained-typesafe-jev-vs-fastino-glide-gliner2-5-decide-and-open-source-competitors/) — Category explainer with a 7-model comparison table (Jev, GLiDE, GLiNER2.5-Decide, Laya, JevK5, OpenJev, kev-0.5b) plus case studies: Vercel adoption, Simon Willison's reranking, Arize/Langfuse judges, 6G edge orchestration, TypeSafe's Doom demo. All head-to-head numbers are the vendors' own benchmarks, which the article explicitly says not to compare across rows: Fastino chose both the tests and the opponents for its GLiDE/GLiNER2.5-Decide claims, and its "Fast Decisions" row pits GLiNER2.5-Decide against JevK5 — an open reproduction, not TypeSafe's Jev. Full text verified.
- [Christopher Lynch: Jev vs frontier LLMs on transaction tagging (LinkedIn, ~2026-10-02)](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/cl96_theres-been-a-lot-of-chatter-about-jev-activity-7510234086188199936-T4MV) — Risk-decision use case (transaction tagging): Jev 1.13.0 89.60% at $0.044 / 41.72s, vs Claude Opus 5.5 97.35% at $2.579 / 500.10s, Claude Sonnet 5 95.50% at $1.461 / 746.81s, GPT-6 Astra 97.27% at $4.203 / 1,169.76s. Read: Jev takes ~92% less time than the next-fastest and costs ~97% less than the next-cheapest — but frontier accuracy is clearly ahead, and Jev was confidently wrong on 1.6% of transactions. Author's own evaluation, 126 comments. Post snippet verified.
- [AutoTrust AI (PR, 2026-10-08): "JEV-27B-VL Tops Hugging Face Global Trending List"](https://www.channelnewsasia.com/media-release/autotrust-ais-jev-27b-vl-tops-hugging-face-global-trending-list-open-decision-models-gain-momentum-6442756) — Company press release: AutoTrust says its open-weight visual decision model JEV-27B-VL ranked #1 on Hugging Face's global Models trending list (rankings captured Oct 8), with GEV-26B-Decide at #3; it also leads the vision board of the public Jev Decision Index with a Full score of 69.82 — 6.4 points above a 397B-parameter reference — and claims the open portfolio recorded 2.98 million downloads in 30 days. All figures are the company's own PR claims, not independently verified.

- [cellcog.ai: "OpenAI Decisions API: Price, Question Types, vs Jev"](https://cellcog.ai/blog/openai-decisions-api/) — OpenAI's Decisions API went public beta on 2026-10-06: dedicated `POST /v1/decisions` endpoint, `gpt-6-luna` only, predicate/choice/score question types, $0.10 per 1M input tokens (no output/cache charges), text plus inline-base64 images; OpenAI claims ~10× faster than the Responses API and expects GA "in the coming weeks". Jev's first big-tech competitor — $0.10/M vs Jev's $0.042/M on input, but with native image input and zero-data-retention/HIPAA options Jev lacks. (figures from OpenAI's docs; vendor claims flagged)
- [Red Hat Developer: "Benchmarking AI decision models against traditional guardrails" (2026-10-02)](https://developers.redhat.com/articles/2026/10/02/benchmarking-ai-decision-models-against-traditional-guardrails) — Red Hat's head-to-head finds decision models like Jev don't beat LLM-as-a-judge or traditional classifiers. ([HN discussion, 166 points](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49933476))
- [HackerNoon: "Inside Jev: How Decision Models Trade Text Generation for Typed, Calibrated Answers" (2026-10-04)](https://hackernoon.com/inside-jev-how-decision-models-trade-text-generation-for-typed-calibrated-answers) — Long-form explainer: the Jev-vs-frontier-LLM contrast table (parallel vs sequential sampling, RLCD vs RLHF, $0.042/M input with free output, 70–500ms), the System One/System Two framing, and an honest cost-savings rule of thumb (~73% on agent workloads where decisions dominate). Full text verified.
- [dev.to: "Open Source Jev Alternatives: Five Self-Hosted Decision Models to Run Yourself" (2026-10-02)](https://dev.to/saptadev27/open-source-jev-alternatives-five-self-hosted-decision-models-to-run-yourself-28gh) — Guide to five self-hosted decision models (Kev, SemIf, Von, Laya, GLiNER2.5-Decide) with local-deployment notes. Reports a "jabr classifier benchmark" (49 tasks, 869 cases): TypeSafe Jev 0.966 macro accuracy, Von 0.704, GLiNER2 0.698, Laya 0.583 — a ~26-point zero-shot gap, with distinct failure modes noted per model (Von collapses on unfamiliar domains, GLiNER2 over-triggers on keywords, Laya compresses rating scales). (benchmark figures as reported by the article, not independently verified; page 200 verified)
- [Techstrong.ai: "Jev Makes Intelligence Cheaper. Who Gets to Keep the Value?"](https://techstrong.ai/features/jev-makes-intelligence-cheaper-who-gets-to-keep-the-value/) — An economist's skeptical take on decision-model economics: illustrative 1M-request math puts 1,000-token requests at $42 for Jev vs ~$108 for Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite (a ~2.6× gap, not the "hundreds of times" vendor-framing suggests). Cites two preprints with specifics: a classifier-first pipeline escalating hard cases to Jev matched Jev's accuracy at 43% of its cost; an agent-harness eval's 23.9% saving from putting Jev ahead of an LLM reviewer shrank to 4.3% once Jev's own screening cost was included. Also JevAdvBench (812 questions, an unverified opinion changed 12.1% of decisions) and a Decision Hijacking study (adaptive attacks lifting attacker-target success 1.8% → 3.5%) — both flagged as early preprints, specific workloads. Notes Cloudflare's Clef pricing ($0.24/M input, $0.09/M flash) sits above Jev's $0.042. Full text verified.
- [RuntimeWire: "Bloomberg reports TypeSafe raised $870M at a $7.5B valuation" (2026-10-09)](https://runtimewire.com/article/typesafe-jev-reported-870m-financing) — Bloomberg reports a ~$870M financing led by Andreessen Horowitz (closing date not reported), following the Sep-15 $40M DCVC seed. The same piece repeats TypeSafe's unattributed claims — Jev hit ~1M users within days, ~1/3 of Fortune 500 "use" it, and the "193.6× faster, 444.6× cheaper than LLMs" figures — all flagged as vendor claims, not independently verified. Full text verified.
- [dev.to/intercode: "Jev AI by TypeSafe: What a Decision Model Does Better Than an LLM, and What It Can't" (~2026-10-09)](https://dev.to/intercode/jev-ai-by-typesafe-what-a-decision-model-does-better-than-an-llm-and-what-it-cant-4in4) — Technical explainer: prefill-only architecture (all questions scored in one transformer pass, 70–500ms), the three primitives (Choice/Score/Noul), RLCD training that penalizes miscalibration instead of rewarding likable answers, $0.042/M input with free output. (page 200 verified; figures from TypeSafe's docs, vendor claims flagged)
- [johnnys.news: "Jevfree – repurposing EmbeddingGemma 2 for Jev-like decisions" (2026-10)](https://johnnys.news/2026/10/Jevfree-repurposing-an-embedding-model-for-decisions/) — Browser-local Jev-style decisions with Google's open EmbeddingGemma 2 via transformers.js: state and options embedded, dot-product + low-temperature softmax to probabilities (option whitening, boolean-as-choice, score-as-choice), no server or API key. Live demo at nor0x.github.io/Jevfree (~1.5GB first download, WebGPU recommended). The author's own caveats: rough "vibe" probabilities, no real reasoning — negations and numbers can fail — not a drop-in Jev replacement. ([HN discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50024668); the nor0x/Jevfree repo itself is 0★, no license declared, <7 days old — held as draft per the quality rule)
- [Microsoft launches Microsoft-Decision-1 (2026-10-09)](https://www.testingcatalog.com/microsoft-launches-decision-1-model-in-foundry/) — Microsoft's answer to Jev: a decision model (Qwen3.5-9B post-trained for single-pass scoring) that takes a fixed set of options and returns a calibrated probability for each — yes/no, multiple-choice, rating, plus rubric-based grading of AI responses and agent actions. Available now in Microsoft Foundry (OpenRouter "soon"), priced identically to Jev at $0.042/M input with free output. Microsoft's claims (announced by Satya Nadella, Oct 9): top accuracy across a 36-benchmark, ~150K-question evaluation withheld from training; 4.5× faster than Quyet-1.0-Large and 35× faster than GPT-6 Sol at p50 latency; decisions flip only 1.3% of the time across paraphrased/shuffled options; Xbox Research reports comparable quality to GPT-6 Sol at >14× speed and 200× lower cost on 10K gaming-feedback items. All figures are Microsoft's own claims, not independently verified. ([runtimewire writeup](https://runtimewire.com/article/microsoft-decision-1-achint-srivastava-agent-workflows))
- [ABTdomain: "Using Jev in Domain Abuse Review: A Workflow Design" (2026-10-10)](https://abtdomain.com/blog/2026/10/using-jev-in-domain-abuse-review-a-workflow-design/) — Brand-protection case study around a three-layer review (Release/Watch/Flag bins at 0.3/0.7 thresholds): on 100 domains containing "apple", Jev (`jev-1.13.0`) released 18, flagged 24 and passed 58 on; the Layer 3 UDRP-style use test released 13, watched 12 and flagged 1 of the 26 active domains. Complete requests shown (three runs averaged per score), the 100-domain list on [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ABTdomain/domain-tech-meta). The authors stress this is decision support, not a real-judgment tool. ([HN discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50035784); full text verified)
- [GC AI: "What we learned building 38 projects with Jev in 12 hours" (2026-10-09)](https://gc.ai/blog/what-we-learned-building-38-projects-with-jev-in-12-hours) — Internal hackathon report with concrete limits: Jev found a needle sentence in 30k tokens of boilerplate and classified movie titles 46/50 across 253 options — but calendar arithmetic failed ~4 times out of 5, and packing tabular rows into one prompt dropped accuracy 96%→72% from answer cross-contamination (the lesson: fan out separate calls, not arrays). Also covered: a ToS Trap Spotter Chrome extension (Spotify's 110-paragraph agreement scanned in 1.9s, 89% F1 vs 57% keyword baseline), an on-call triage bot replaying 136 alerts (every incident caught, one false page), PR review gating where Jev works only on narrow factual questions (AUC 0.54 on "is this test useless?" vs 0.72 on eight factual sub-questions), and the Jeventus Slack sloth that won the hackathon. 16 reusable design patterns catalogued; the team's internal `jev-design` skill guide is included. (full text verified)
- [cellcog.ai: "Jev Alternatives: Clef, Perplexity and Strands Decider" (updated 2026-10-10)](https://cellcog.ai/blog/jev-alternatives/) — Side-by-side of the decision-model wave: seven models with hosted price per 1M input tokens (Clef-flash $0.038 after the Oct 9 cut, Perplexity $0.04, Jev and Microsoft-Decision-1 $0.042, OpenAI Decisions API $0.10, Clef $0.24), licenses (Clef / Clef-flash / Strands Decider 2B Apache 2.0, Perplexity's model with an Apache-2.0 tag on HF, Jev closed) and bases (all open ones sit on Qwen3.x). Reproduces Cloudflare's own head-to-head table (Clef family leads most rows; Jev still wins When2Call and BRIGHT) and flags the sourcing: the benchmark table is Cloudflare's, Perplexity's 85.71% appears only in its X post, no independent all-model comparison exists yet. (full text verified)

## Papers

TypeSafe has not published a formal paper on Jev or RLCD as of 2026-09-27. This section will not be padded with loosely-related arXiv links — but the first full third-party papers treating Jev as the measurement subject are below:

- [arXiv:2609.29429 — "Just Ask Jev: Reinforcement Learning for Calibrated Decisions as a Zero-Shot Detector of AI Alignment Failures" (Guo et al., submitted 2026-09-25)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29429) — RLCDAlignBench benchmarks Jev on ten alignment failures (sycophancy, jailbreaks, deception, prompt injection, hallucination, privacy violation, social bias, reward hacking, concealing uncertainty, power seeking) across 44 benchmarks and five target models. A single generic question reaches median AUROC 0.886 zero-shot, beating supervised baselines on most benchmarks; question wording matters little, the encoded context (fields that reveal the reference label) matters more; Jev also surfaces label defects in existing benchmarks and costs 63× less than LLM-judge scorers. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. ([Code & data](https://github.com/sumleo/RLCDAlignBench), 15★, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API, created 2026-09-24)
- [arXiv:2609.36399 — "Calibrated to Whom? Persona and Language Effects on Cultural Values in JEV" (Asseri & Asseri)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36399) — Asked Jev the 24 items of the VSM 2013 values survey as 12 matched Saudi and 12 matched American personas (plus no-persona), in English and Arabic, under eight ways of formulating the request: 1,200 prompts × 30 repetitions = 288,000 answers. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. ([Code](https://github.com/basseri1/calibrated-to-whom), 0★, Python, MIT, real non-fork verified via GitHub API, created 2026-10-03; draft promoted after license declared)

- [pozapas/kumo-jev-crash-records](https://github.com/pozapas/kumo-jev-crash-records) — Code for the study "Estimating Uncoded Crash Factors with Tabular Foundation and System One Models: Kumo Tabular and Jev" (Rafe and Das): joins the coded fields and officer narratives of 5.6 million Texas crashes (2017–2025) — Kumo Tabular reads the coded record of every crash, Jev reads the narratives of two probability samples, human judgments recalibrate Jev, and a multiwave predict-then-debias estimator joins the tiers; a second human tier checks the estimates by design. No crash data in the repo itself (`DATA_ACCESS.md` explains how to obtain the Texas CRIS extract). Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. (0★, Python, MIT, created 2026-10-04; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [arXiv:2609.37647 — "Evaluating and Benchmarking the System One Model Jev" (Deußer, Sparrenberg & Sifa; IAIS, Sankt Augustin, per search index; submitted 2026-09-29)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37647) — Zero-shot evaluation of `jev-1.13.0` on 37 datasets (classification, routing, NLI, reading comprehension, commonsense, moderation, legal clause analysis, rubric scoring): 346,009 requests for under US$10. Jev reaches 95–99% on IMDB/SST-2/HellaSwag/ARC and 86.7% on Belebele across 122 languages; beats Qwen3.8-27B on 27 of 37 datasets and Gemma-4-E4B on all 37. Choice probabilities are well calibrated and support selective prediction; binary probabilities need tuned thresholds (UNFAIR-ToS micro-F₁ 0.50 → 0.75). Code, harness and all raw responses released. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. Abstract and submission history verified on arXiv.
- [arXiv:2609.23136 — "Intent Interpretation at RIC Timescales: Jev Decision Models versus Large Language Models in 6G Open RAN" (Li et al.; submitted 2026-09-19)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23136) — Compares Jev-1.13.0 against LLMs for turning intents into A1 policies inside the RAN intelligent controller loop; per MarkTechPost's summary of the paper, at matched correctness Jev cut median decision latency 22.4% vs DeepSeek and 61.9% vs Gemini. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. Abstract verified on arXiv.
- [arXiv:2609.22753 — "Replacing Large Language Models with Jev Decision Models for Low-Latency Edge Service Orchestration" (Li et al.; submitted 2026-09-19)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22753) — Companion study integrating Jev's API into edge-service orchestration: 4–8 bounded intent fields extracted per request with shared validation and admission, reporting lower decision latency, end-to-end latency and API cost than structured-output DeepSeek baselines when interpretation is not cached. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. Abstract verified on arXiv.
- [arXiv:2609.24052 — "Calibrated Decisions at Scale: Converting Police Crash Narratives into Probabilistic Crash Variables with a System One Model (Jev)" (Rafe & Das; submitted 2026-09-21)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24052) — Frames narrative coding of police crash reports as gated, typed decisions answered by Jev: frontier-LLM text generation is costly and unverifiable at scale, while Jev's typed outputs can be audited. Same author team as the Texas CRIS study whose code ([pozapas/kumo-jev-crash-records](https://github.com/pozapas/kumo-jev-crash-records)) is already listed above. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. Abstract verified on arXiv.
- [arXiv:2609.24395 — "JEVQA: Video Quality from Metadata, Bitstream, and Pixel Features with a General-Purpose Decision Model" (Robitza; submitted 2026-09-21)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24395) — Tests whether Jev (a commercial System One model returning probability distributions) can judge video quality without task-specific training. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. Abstract verified on arXiv.
- [arXiv:2609.27535 — "KITE: Scaling Jev Population Experiments with Sparse Flagship Calibration" (submitted 2026-09-23)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27535) — Queries a typed behavioral kernel once per unique state, then executes populations of any size from the table; reserves an expensive flagship model for sparse paired anchors and propagates measured human-model discrepancy as shared error into every conclusion. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe.
- [arXiv:2610.00381 — "OmniMed-Jev: Calibrating LVLM Confidence for Trustworthy Medical Multimodal Decisions via System One" (Tang & Chen; submitted 2026-09-30)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00381) — Motivated by decision-native interfaces such as Jev: represents each medical decision (diagnosis, finding, cell count) as a Choice, Noul or Score decision over a runtime-supplied candidate set and returns a full distribution — so the reported probability reflects the decision itself rather than the next token. Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. Abstract and submission history verified on arXiv. Abstract verified on arXiv.
- [Alex Vidovich & Zivana Zerjal: "A Thermometer, Not an Analyst: Lawful Uses of a Text Judge in a Live Trading Lab" (working paper, 2026-10-09)](https://alexvidovich.com/papers/text-judge) — Independent researchers put a "small commercial judge, pinned to one version" (typed headline questions, Jev-style) next to a general chatbot on blind trading-headline rows beside a live system: six up-front checks (read a plain fact against a real label, beat its own headline count, no memory of famous events, give the same answer twice, always see the decision-time date, check forward verdicts against random flags), every study reported including failures. Results so far: one fact read well, small effects below the bar or vanished; no claim about either strategy's returns. Posted on SSRN ([doi:10.2139/ssrn.7589261](https://doi.org/10.2139/ssrn.7589261)). Independent research — not authored by TypeSafe. ([HN discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50035460); abstract and DOI verified)

Includes Mobile Jev (local Android agent via Mobilerun) and several pi- coding-agent extensions with experiment logs.

Caveat emptor: captions are auto-generated and uneven, and the site's "verified" label means only that a project passed an automated eligibility check — not that its claims were fact-checked.
- [heyjunpenn/awesome-jev](https://github.com/heyjunpenn/awesome-jev) — A competing curated Jev catalogue claiming 640 open-source projects, "verified" by its maintainers (528★, added 110 projects on 2026-09-22). Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps; their verification claims are unexamined by us — spot-check entries before citing them.
- [justmalhar/awesome-jev-apps](https://github.com/justmalhar/awesome-jev-apps) — A competing curated list of apps built with Jev: CI verifies every app's logic (no live calls, forks included), ships a shared Jev client plus smoke tests, and states plainly what Jev is bad at from TypeSafe's own [jaggedness notes](https://docs.typesafe.ai/model-jaggedness/jev-1.13) (can't count, compare dates, or generate — CI fails any question that tries). Worth cross-checking for coverage gaps; their app claims are code-verified, unlike most roundups.
- [Yifan-Lan/awesome-jev-robustness](https://github.com/Yifan-Lan/awesome-jev-robustness) — Robustness-only Jev list: 110 entries, almost all against `jev-1.13.0`, covering jaggedness, calibration, consistency, prompt injection and abstention. Ships machine-readable `data/entries.tsv` and one "what the evidence says" line per property. Numbers are the linked authors' own — reads as evidence to inspect, not settled results (5★, created 2026-09-23).
- [maiasalti/wait-which-model](https://github.com/maiasalti/wait-which-model) — Model/release tracker repo (TypeScript) that added TypeSafe AI's Jev on 2026-09-24: structured `data/companies.json`, `data/models.json`, `data/news.json` with palette/integrity checks; benchmark cells deliberately null since Jev "is not an LLM" — strengths and weaknesses drawn from TypeSafe's own limitations page (1★, active on 2026-09-24).
- [AnotiaWang/awesome-jev](https://github.com/AnotiaWang/awesome-jev) — A competing curated Jev list (351★, created 2026-09-17, CC0-1.0): Official / SDKs & Clients / Applications / Demos & Games / Agent Tools / Research & Open Models / Articles sections, EN + 中文 READMEs. Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps — it surfaced the independent "Jev Does Not Play Dice" calibration check in Benchmarks above.
- [shipwithjev.com](https://shipwithjev.com) — Catalog of 551 application scenarios built on Jev (agents, games, research, trading, content and data work), organized by category and updated ongoing (spotted 2026-09-25). Announced by Vital Dulov, who says it was compiled with TypeSafe AI's input — claim attributed to the author ([Threads](https://www.threads.com/@vi.dulov/post/Ddsz2d9DYIV)).
- [RadRebelSam/awesome-jev](https://github.com/RadRebelSam/awesome-jev) — Crawler-maintained directory of Jev projects: daily GitHub + npm sweep, human-merged (1★, no license declared, pushed 2026-09-24). Treat auto-generated listings as discovery leads, not verified claims.
- [systemonemodels.org: \"Jev AI by TypeSafe: specs, pricing, API limits and versions\"](https://systemonemodels.org/models/jev/) — Independent spec/audit page: consolidated 1.13 facts (64K total context / 32K state budget, 250K tok/s + 1,200 req/min rate limits), per-version [jaggedness](https://docs.typesafe.ai/model-jaggedness/jev-1.13) links covering all 9 documented failure modes (literal reading, math, dates, indirection, context rot, adversarial content…), and a warning to pin `jev-1.13.0` because the `jev-latest` alias floats. Also notes the no-training-on-customer-data claim has not been independently audited, and documents the Python SDK vs quickstart serialization discrepancy (spotted 2026-09-25).
- [v-modal/awesome-jev-tools](https://github.com/v-modal/awesome-jev-tools) — Curated list of tools built for Jev (718★, real non-fork verified via GitHub API, created 2026-09-19): measured entries like mastra-jev-moderation (Jev asks "must this message be blocked?" + a category Choice in one request; in production blocked 9/9 hostile and 0/49 real messages at ~0.4s median, ~4× cheaper than an LLM moderator), plus HN/X/Reddit discussion links. Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps; their app claims are the linked authors' own, not ours.
- [frank-zy-dou/awesome-jev](https://github.com/frank-zy-dou/awesome-jev) — A competing curated Jev list focused on robot control (physical hardware + simulation), 3D modeling/animation, vehicles/drones/games in simulation and design tools: every example carries sources and archived media, plus a reported-numbers section with methodology notes. Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps on the robotics side. (16★, no license, README + assets only)
- [@truehorizonai: Jev vs GPT-5.6 Luna internal routing test (Instagram carousel, 2026-09-24)](https://www.instagram.com/p/DdsNVzZGDPV/) — TrueHorizon.ai's internal engineering test (run 2026-09-21/22) comparing TypeSafe Jev against GPT-5.6 Luna for agent routing: Jev 6.5× faster (404ms vs 2,633ms median). Accuracy split — on dev cases Jev 99.3% (143/144) vs Luna 85.4%; on held-out unseen cases Luna led 96.8% (90/93) vs Jev 93.5% (87/93). Cost: $0.0031 Jev vs $0.0309 Luna. Honest methodology notes throughout: a prompt defect favoring the most recent agent was found and fixed (96/96 reply checks after fix), one repeat-request failure kept (Jev flagged it correctly, their policy ignored it), and probability calibration flagged as unverified (474 valid calls, 1 invalid route answer in a later 639-call study). Next step stated: retest corrected Jev against Luna on fresh cases. Figures from the author's posted carousel, not independently verified.
- [@dailyaionly: "Xor: a decision model, measured against Jev" (Threads, 2026-09-25)](https://www.threads.com/@dailyaionly/post/DdtYCROCcu7) — Static infographic comparing three systems on 231 public JevBench v1.2 decisions: Xor (described as post-trained on Qwen-3.6-35B) Accuracy 88.3% vs Official Jev (TypeSafe, hosted) 86.1% vs Qwen-3.6-35B vanilla baseline 84.8%. Caveats: single-source infographic, numbers read from the posted graphic, author unaffiliated/unverified, no published repo or weights to check.
- [kraayenjon/awesome-jev](https://github.com/kraayenjon/awesome-jev) — A competing curated Jev list (140★, the largest competing catalogue so far): Jev-vs-LLM comparison table, pricing/limits/access snapshot reviewed 2026-09-18, use cases / projects / SDKs / resources sections. Same author behind madewithjev.com and "Shapeshift". Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps.
- [mrjev/awesome-jev](https://github.com/mrjev/awesome-jev) — A competing curated list (12★, CC0-1.0, updated 2026-09-26): sortable hands-on project reviews at mrjev.com/projects plus a dated "Recent Developments" changelog with sources (SDK releases, Gateway listings). Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps.
- [rhc98/awesome-jev](https://github.com/rhc98/awesome-jev) — A competing Jev catalogue with a distinctive method: candidates are pulled from GitHub, then Jev itself judges each repo in one call (genuinely about Jev? which category, which decision pattern, how substantial) — code applies the policy, nothing hand-picked. README + [awesome-jev.xyz](https://awesome-jev.xyz) (5,771 listed, published daily) carry the full index with per-repo probabilities. Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps. Independent community project, not affiliated with TypeSafe. (2★, active 2026-09-26; real non-fork verified via GitHub API)
- [@testmuai: Jev "Agent Assurance" cautionary demo (Instagram reel, 2026-09-26)](https://www.instagram.com/reel/DdwCd_JCE4q/) — The sharpest short-form critique of the "can't hallucinate" framing we've seen: an expense-approval agent asks the Jev runtime "Is this claim approved?" for EXP-1002 and gets back `approved: true` at 0.94 confidence in 72–500ms — but the actual claim was $450 against a $200 approval limit, and the agent had misread its own numbers. Point of the demo: a typed model produces the same wrong decision with no tell, and the confidence number is self-reported. Honest jaggedness evidence, one test, verifiable post.
- [@99hud (Hudson Brendon): 27-question ChatGPT vs Jev terminal demo (Instagram reel, 2026-09-26, Portuguese)](https://www.instagram.com/reel/Ddw2Aq0Curj/) — Same 27 questions through both: ChatGPT (GPT-5.6) $0.013880 / 8.566s vs Jev $0.000081 / 0.114s, terminal screenshot extrapolated to 1,000 queries ($13.88 vs $0.08); author claims ~170× cheaper and ~75× faster. Self-reported single source, screenshot-based — logged as an author claim, not an independent benchmark.
- [Zaious/jev-capability-atlas](https://github.com/Zaious/jev-capability-atlas) — Independent, evidence-based "when does Jev hold up vs break" map built from recorded real API-call receipts rather than a leaderboard: bilingual 中文/English READMEs, a dozen+ cross-industry cases (browser automation, code review, moderation, finance, news, games) in `capability-map.md`, and an open invitation to contribute real experiment results. Frames the question as "in what situations it works and why", citing existing benchmarks rather than re-running them. Author's statement: no compensation or early access from TypeSafe. (27★, MIT, created 2026-09-18, pushed 2026-09-24; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [unclehobbot/aiwiki: "Typesafe.ai & Jev — Research Notes" (2026-09-21)](https://github.com/unclehobbot/aiwiki/blob/HEAD/sources/web/typesafe-ai-jev-research-notes.md) — Research-agent notes for a future wiki entry with the claims discipline we like: every official TypeSafe claim marked as such, third-party and community statements marked separately, inline citations, and raw caches of the key pages (homepage, launch blog, manifesto, docs, HN thread JSON) in the same directory. Covers the name collision with the old "Typesafe" (Scala/Lightbend), the RLCD + parallel-sampler story, documented jaggedness, pricing math, and an "independent checks" section (Vercel 5–18× faster, Bryo 10–20× cheaper, Bartosz Mikulski's pre-registered Quick-Draw experiment). (1★, real non-fork verified via GitHub API; full doc verified)
- [omnijev/awesome-jev-gallery](https://github.com/omnijev/awesome-jev-gallery) — Competing gallery catalogue of the System One ecosystem: 146 entries across 8 sections with a visual card gallery (omnijev.github.io), counting 43 open-source models/codebases and 24 independent Jev evaluations as of its Sept 22 ecosystem refresh; also tracks papers, commentary, and a "shape before Jev" lineage. Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps; the claims are the linked authors' own, not ours.
- [PongPong/token-savings-notes: TYPESAFE-JEV.md](https://github.com/pongpong/token-savings-notes/blob/HEAD/TYPESAFE-JEV.md) — Token-savings research notes with a dedicated Jev companion doc: a claim-source table for Jev (each official claim gets a "how to treat it" skepticism column — e.g. 193.6× marked "author ceiling, high end of real-world"), plus a 17-request Jev demo note (median ~178ms) with author-honest small-n caveats (two-sided sign-test p=0.25 — "not a broad agent benchmark") and a house rule against universal session token-% claims. (1★, updated 2026-09-27; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw doc verified)
- [jevdirectory/jevdirectory.org](https://github.com/jevdirectory/jevdirectory.org) — Community-curated Jev resource directory ([jevdirectory.org](https://www.jevdirectory.org), site live-verified): browsable/searchable catalog of official and community repos, SDKs, cookbooks, tools, demos, patterns, guides and evaluations with reviewed entries, plus original guides/articles. (0★, no license declared, repo created 2026-09-28; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

- [cl-victor1/jev-ecosystem-survey](https://github.com/cl-victor1/jev-ecosystem-survey) — Use-case classification of the Jev open-source ecosystem, surveyed 2026-09-27 and classified by `jev-1.13.0` itself: 14,742 public GitHub repos relate to Jev, 12,923 of them created in September 2026 (peak 1,783 on 2026-09-21); top TypeSafe-map categories are AI Automation Software (30.4%) and Harness Engineering (25.3%, but 52.1% of stars). Bilingual README, tables under `data/`. (2★, docs + data, no license declared, created 2026-09-28; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [scienceaix/jev](https://github.com/scienceaix/jev) — "The Awesome Jev Open-Source Ecosystem": a comprehensive survey field guide — projects, models, integrations and developer momentum, with a state-in/typed-decisions-out framing, local runtimes and open-research coverage. Longer analytical write-up style, distinct from cl-victor1's data-first classification survey listed above. (0★, docs, MIT, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [Sarim-MBZUAI/awesome-jev-security](https://github.com/Sarim-MBZUAI/awesome-jev-security) — Curated Jev security / robustness / safety reading list: four attack papers (JevAdvBench arXiv:2609.31142 — 812 typed questions, one appended opinion flips 12.1% of decisions; JevOut 2609.30243 — natural-looking context flips 61.4% of initially correct decisions; "Decision Hijacking" 2609.28613 — prompt injection on typed decisions, attacker target rarely picked at 1.8%; "Type-Safe Is Not Error-Free" 2609.26758 — decision heads follow option names, not rubrics), two detector papers (Evaluating System One Models for Agent Security Decisions 2609.33401; RLCDAlignBench 2609.29429 — also listed in Papers above), and two application papers (pentest harnesses 2609.28940; CallScreenBench open-Jev scam screening 2609.23959). The underlying numbers are each paper's authors' own — not independently replicated. (4★, curated list, no license declared, created 2026-09-29; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [ozers/jevsome-projects](https://github.com/ozers/jevsome-projects) — "Open-source projects that provably call Jev": every entry links to the line of code that proves it (an API call, SDK import, pinned route — a README sentence doesn't count). 72 verified projects, 1,210 search hits examined, refreshed daily ([jevsome.ozersubasi.com](https://jevsome.ozersubasi.com)). A competing directory — worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps; their proof bar is documented and strict. (1★, JavaScript, MIT, created 2026-09-18; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [jessie-qingyu/jev-in-the-wild](https://github.com/jessie-qingyu/jev-in-the-wild) — "Jev in the wild": real-world use cases, open-source projects, benchmarks and criticism — what people actually build with Jev and where it actually fails. 338 entries (172 of them Reddit/HN/YouTube/blog write-ups, where most first-hand reporting happens), a dedicated "where Jev struggles" criticism section, machine-readable `data/entries.json` (the README is generated from it), and a live searchable tracker companion. The claims are the linked authors' own, not ours. (5★, JavaScript, MIT, created 2026-09-22; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)
- [zeke/jev](https://github.com/zeke/jev) — Research notes on Jev ("what it's good for, and where it falls apart") plus a live Cloudflare Worker demo: paste a support message and Jev triages it (department, urgency, frustration) in one call with probability bars and confidence scores rendered live. (2★, TypeScript, no license declared, created 2026-09-24; real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

Worth cross-checking against ours for coverage gaps; no license declared. (real non-fork verified via GitHub API, raw README verified)

Unofficial and community-maintained; their inclusion bar is unverified by us — treat as a discovery lead.

## Contributing

PRs welcome. Rules:

1. One line of description per entry, with a real link. No link, no merge (post it in the issue first and we'll find it).
2. Numbers need sources. "10× faster" with no methodology gets cut.
3. No affiliate links, no waitlist-farming, no fake demos — the [@Steve8708 rule](https://x.com/typesafeai): if the demo isn't real, it doesn't belong here.
